“The sufferings of individuals,” she murmured, with a pathetic little sigh. “In truth ’tis but little heed you pay, Bertrand, to my sufferings these days.” She paused awhile, then added under her breath: “Since first you met Theresia Cabarrus, three months ago, you have eyes and ears only for her.”

===Chinese===
“我知道，我知道，贝特朗，”她应道，一只小手怯怯地探出去，可怜巴巴地想捉住他的手。“你们的志向辉煌得很。你们了不起，个个都了不起。我算什么呢，竟想哪怕用一句话、一句祷告，劝阻你们去做你们认为对的事？可是约瑟芬那么年轻，那么性急！她能帮你们什么忙呢？她才十七岁。还有雅克！他不过是个不知轻重的男孩子！想想吧，贝特朗，想想吧！这两个孩子要是出了什么事，妈妈会活不下去的！”

他耸了耸肩，把一声厌倦的叹息咽了回去。她终于捉住了他的手，怀着满腔热切的恳求，紧紧攥住不放。

“你和我永远也说不拢了，雷吉娜，”他开口道；随即又赶紧补上一句，“在这些事情上。”因为他说了那句狠心话之后，已经听见她发出一声细微的痛呼，那么像一只受伤小鸟的哀鸣，是违着她的本心从唇间溜出来的。“你不明白，”他接下去说，口气平静了些，“在一场伟大的事业里，个人的苦难比起眼前的辉煌成就来，根本算不得什么。”

“个人的苦难，”她喃喃地说，带着一声哀婉的轻叹。“说真的，这些日子来我的苦楚，你可没怎么放在心上啊，贝特朗。”她停了一会儿，又压低声音添上一句：“自从三个月前你结识了特蕾莎·卡巴吕斯（Theresia Cabarrus），你眼里耳里就再容不下别人了。”

===Original===
He smothered an angry exclamation.

“It is useless, Régine﻿—” he began.

“I know,” she broke in quietly. “Theresia Cabarrus is beautiful; she has charm, wit, power﻿—all things which I do not possess.”

“She has fearlessness and a heart of gold,” Bertrand rejoined and, probably despite himself, a sudden warmth crept into his voice. “Do you not know of the marvellous influence which she exercised over that fiend Tallien, down in Bordeaux? He went there filled with a veritable tiger’s fury, ready for a wholesale butchery of all the royalists, the aristocrats, the bourgeois, over there﻿—all those, in fact, whom he chose to believe were conspiring against this hideous Revolution. Well! under Theresia’s influence he actually modified his views and became so lenient that he was recalled. You know, or should know, Régine,” the young man added in a tone of bitter reproach, “that Theresia is as good as she is beautiful.”

===Chinese===
他强压下一声怒叱。

“说这些没有用，雷吉娜——”他开口道。

“我知道，”她平静地插嘴道。“特蕾莎·卡巴吕斯生得美；她有风韵，有才情，有势力——这一切我都没有。”

“她还有无所畏惧的胆量和一颗金子般的心，”贝特朗回道，多半是不由自主，嗓音里悄悄透出一股热意。“你难道不知道，她在波尔多（Bordeaux）时，对恶棍塔利安（Tallien）施展过多么神奇的影响？他到那里的时候，满肚子一股老虎也似的凶焰，准备对当地所有的保王党人、贵族、资产者来一场大开杀戒——其实，凡是他认定在密谋反对这场丑恶革命的人，他一个都不放过。结果呢！在特蕾莎的影响下，他居然改了主张，变得那样宽仁，以致被召了回来。你知道，或者说应该知道，雷吉娜，”年轻人又添了一句，声调里带着辛酸的责备，“特蕾莎人有多美，心就有多好。”

===Original===
“I do know that, Bertrand,” the girl rejoined with an effort. “Only﻿—”

“Only what?” he queried roughly.

“I do not trust her﻿ ﻿… that is all.” Then, as he made no attempt at concealing his scorn and his impatience, she went on in a tone which was much harsher, more uncompromising than the one she had adopted hitherto: “Your infatuation blinds you, Bertrand, or you﻿—an enthusiastic royalist, an ardent loyalist﻿—would not place your trust in an avowed Republican. Theresia Cabarrus may be kindhearted﻿—I don’t deny it. She may have done and she may be all that you say; but she stands for the negation of every one of your ideals, for the destruction of what you exalt, the glorification of the principles of this execrable Revolution.”

“Jealousy blinds you, Régine,” he retorted moodily.

===Chinese===
“这我知道，贝特朗，”姑娘费力地应道。“只是——”

“只是什么？”他粗声问道。

“我不信得过她……就这么一句。”接着，见他丝毫不去掩饰自己的轻蔑与不耐，她便换了一种比先前生硬得多、决绝得多的口气，继续说道：“是痴迷蒙住了你的眼，贝特朗，不然的话——你一个热诚的保王党人、一个赤胆的忠君者——绝不会把信任交给一个公开的共和派。特蕾莎·卡巴吕斯也许心肠好——我不否认。她也许做过、也许真是你说的一切；可她代表的是对你每一项理想的否定，代表毁掉你所尊崇的一切，给这场万恶革命的原则歌功颂德。”

“是嫉妒蒙住了你的眼，雷吉娜，”他郁郁地回嘴道。

===Original===
She shook her head.

“No, it is not jealousy, Bertrand﻿—not common, vulgar jealousy﻿—that prompts me to warn you, before it is too late. Remember,” she added solemnly, “that you have not only yourself to think of, but that you are accountable to God and to me for the innocent lives of Joséphine and of Jacques. By confiding in that Spanish woman﻿—”

“Now you are insulting her,” he broke in mercilessly. “Making her out to be a spy.”

“What else is she?” the girl riposted vehemently. “You know that she is affianced to Tallien, whose influence and whose cruelty are second only to those of Robespierre. You know it, Bertrand!” she insisted, seeing that at last she had silenced him and that he sat beside her, sullen and obstinate. “You know it, even though you choose to close your eyes and ears to what is common knowledge.”

===Chinese===
她摇了摇头。

“不，这不是嫉妒，贝特朗——不是那种寻常、粗俗的嫉妒——促使我趁还来得及的时候警告你。记住，”她郑重地补充道，“你要顾的不光是你自己；对约瑟芬和雅克这两条无辜的性命，你在上帝面前、在我面前，都担着干系。你居然把心事托付给那个西班牙女人——”

“你这可是在侮辱她了，”他毫不留情地打断道。“把她编排成奸细。”

“不然她还能是什么？”姑娘激烈地反击道。“你知道她已同塔利安订了婚，而此人的权势和残忍，仅次于罗伯斯庇尔。你知道的，贝特朗！”她见他终于被说得哑口无言、闷坐在身旁，一副执拗相，便又逼紧了一句。“你知道的，哪怕你硬要对这尽人皆知的事闭眼塞耳。”

===Original===
There was silence after that for a while in the narrow porch, where two hearts once united were filled now with bitterness, one against the other. Even out in the street it had become quite dark, the darkness of a spring night, full of mysterious lights and grey, indeterminate shadows. The girl shivered as with cold and drew her tattered shawl more closely round her shoulders. She was vainly trying to swallow her tears. Goaded into saying more than she had ever meant to, she felt the finality of what she had said. Something had finally snapped just now: something that could never in after years be put together again. The boy and girl love which had survived the past two years of trouble and of stress, lay wounded unto death, bleeding at the foot of the shrine of a man’s infatuation and a woman’s vanity. How impossible this would have seemed but a brief while ago!

===Chinese===
此后，狭窄的门廊里沉寂了一阵子；两颗曾经紧紧相连的心，此刻都盛满了怨苦，一个怨着一个。连外面的街上也已全黑，那是春夜的黑暗，满是神秘的光亮和灰蒙蒙、辨不真切的暗影。姑娘像发冷似的打了个战，把破披巾往肩头拢得更紧了些。她在徒然地想把眼泪咽回去。她被逼着说出了远非本意的话，此刻感到了这些话的无可挽回。方才有什么东西终于断了：一样往后多少年也再接不起来的东西。那段挨过了过去两年忧患与熬煎的少男少女之情，此刻躺在那里，伤得只剩一死，在一个男人的痴迷和一个女人的虚荣筑成的神龛脚下流着血。可就在片刻之前，这一切还显得多么不可能啊！

===Original===
Through the darkness, swift visions of past happy times came fleeting before the girl’s tear-dimmed gaze: visions of walks in the woods round Auteuil, of drifting downstream in a boat on the Seine on hot August days﻿—aye! even of danger shared and perilous moments passed together, hand in hand, with bated breath, in darkened rooms, with curtains drawn and ears straining to hear the distant cannonade, the shouts of an infuriated populace or the rattle of death carts upon the cobblestones. Swift visions of past sorrows and past joys! An immense self-pity filled the girl’s heart to bursting. An insistent sob that would not be suppressed rose to her throat.

“Oh, Mother of God, have mercy!” she murmured through her tears.

Bertrand, shamed and confused, his heart stirred by the misery of this girl whom he had so dearly loved, his nerves strained beyond endurance through the many mad schemes which his enthusiasm was forever evolving, felt like a creature on the rack, torn between compunction and remorse on the one hand and irresistible passion on the other.

===Chinese===
黑暗中，往昔欢乐时光的幢幢幻影，飞也似的掠过姑娘泪眼蒙眬的目光：在欧特伊（Auteuil）四郊林中散步的情景，八月炎天在塞纳河（Seine）上泛舟顺流漂下的情景——是啊！甚至还有共历危难、同挨险境的情景：手拉着手，屏住呼吸，在放下了帘子、黑黢黢的房间里，侧耳细听远处的炮声、暴民的怒吼，或是死囚车碾过鹅卵石街面的辚辚声。往日的悲，往日的欢，幻影纷纷！一股无边无际的自怜涌上姑娘的心头，几乎要把心撑破。一声压抑不住的呜咽，执拗地涌到了她的喉咙口。

“哦，圣母玛利亚，发发慈悲吧！”她噙着泪喃喃道。

贝特朗又羞又乱；他的心被这姑娘——他曾那样深爱过的姑娘——的悲苦搅动了；他的神经又被热狂不断孵化出的那许多疯狂密谋绷得再受不住；他觉得自己像一头上了拉肢刑架的活物，一头是愧疚与悔恨在撕扯，一头是那按捺不住的痴情在撕扯。

===Original===
“Régine,” he pleaded, “forgive me! I am a brute, I know﻿—a brute to you, who have been the kindest little friend a man could possibly hope for. Oh, my dear,” he added pitiably, “if you would only understand﻿ ﻿…”

At once her tender, womanly sentiment was to the fore, sweeping pride and just resentment out of the way. Hers was one of those motherly natures that are always more ready to comfort than to chide. Already she had swallowed her tears, and now that with a wearied gesture he had buried his face in his hands, she put her arm around his neck, pillowed his head against her breast.

“I do understand, Bertrand,” she said gently. “And you must never ask my forgiveness, for you and I have loved one another too well to bear anger or grudge one toward the other. There!” she said, and rose to her feet, and seemed by that sudden act to gather up all the moral strength of which she stood in such sore need. “It is getting late, and *maman* will be anxious. Another time we must have a more quiet talk about our future. But,” she added, with renewed seriousness, “if I concede you Theresia Cabarrus without another murmur, you must give me back Joséphine and Jacques. If﻿—if I﻿—am to lose you﻿—I could not bear to lose them as well. They are so young﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“雷吉娜，”他恳求道，“原谅我吧！我是个畜生，我知道——尤其对你，你这个男人所能盼望的最善良体贴的小知己，我竟是个畜生。哦，亲爱的，”他又可怜巴巴地添了一句，“但愿你能明白……”

顿时，她那温柔的女人心肠便占了上风，把自尊和正当的怨忿一扫而空。她天生是那种母性的人，比起责怪来，永远更急于抚慰。她已经把眼泪咽了下去；此刻，见他倦怠地把脸埋进双手，她便伸臂搂住他的颈项，让他的头枕在自己胸前。

“我明白的，贝特朗，”她温柔地说。“你也永远不要求我饶恕，因为你和我彼此爱得那样深，谁也不会向谁动怒、记恨。好了！”她说着站起身来，仿佛借这个突然的举动，把她此刻迫切需要的全部精神力量都聚拢到了身上。“天晚了，妈妈该着急了。改日咱们得安安静静地谈谈咱们的将来。可是，”她又正色补充道，“如果我毫无怨言地把特蕾莎·卡巴吕斯让给你，那你必须把约瑟芬和雅克还给我。如果——如果我——注定要失去你——我可再也经不起连他们也失去了。他们还那么年轻……”

===Original===
“Who talks of losing them?” he broke in, once more impatient, enthusiastic﻿—his moodiness gone, his remorse smothered, his conscience dead to all save to his schemes. “And what have I to do with it all? Joséphine and Jacques are members of the Club. They may be young, but they are old enough to know the value of an oath. They are pledged just like I am, just like we all are. I could not, even if I would, make them false to their oath.” Then, as she made no reply, he leaned over to her, took her hands in his, tried to read her inscrutable face through the shadows of night. He thought that he read obstinacy in her rigid attitude, the unresponsive placidity of her hands. “You would not have them false to their oath?” he insisted.

She made no reply to that, only queried dully:

“What are you going to do tonight?”

“Tonight,” he said with passionate earnestness, his eyes glowing with the fervid adour of self-immolation, “we are going to let hell loose around the name of Robespierre.”

===Chinese===
“谁说失去他们了？”他打断道，又是那副急切、狂热的样子——郁闷没了，悔恨压下了，良心对一切都死了，只剩他的密谋还活着。“再说，这一切又与我何干？约瑟芬和雅克都是俱乐部的成员。他们年纪也许小，可已经到了懂得誓言分量的年纪。他们宣了誓，同我一样，同我们大家一样。我就是想，也不能叫他们背弃誓言。”随后，见她不作声，他便俯身向她，把她的双手握在自己手里，想透过夜影辨认她那莫测的神情。他从她僵直的姿态里、从她双手那种毫无回应的沉静里，读出了固执。“你总不至于要他们背弃誓言吧？”他追逼道。

对此她不置一辞，只木木然问道：

“你们今晚打算做什么？”

“今晚，”他激昂而恳切地说，眼里燃着甘愿舍身自焚的灼热激情，“我们要在罗伯斯庇尔这个名字的四周，放开地狱。”

===Original===
“Where?”

“At the open-air supper in the Rue St. Honoré. Joséphine and Jacques will be there.”

She nodded mechanically, quietly disengaged her hands from his feverish grasp.

“I know,” she said quietly. “They told me they were going. I have no influence to stop them.”

===Chinese===
“在哪儿？”

“在圣奥诺雷街（Rue St. Honoré）的露天晚餐会上。约瑟芬和雅克都会到场。”

她机械地点了点头，悄悄把手从他滚烫的紧握中抽了出来。

“我知道，”她安安静静地说。“他们告诉我要去。我没有本事拦住他们。”

===Original===
“You will be there, too?” he asked.

“Of course. So will poor *maman*,” she replied simply.

“This may be the turning point, Régine,” he said with passionate earnestness, “in the history of France!”

“Perhaps!”

===Chinese===
“你也去吗？”他问。

“当然去。可怜的妈妈也去，”她简单地答道。

“这说不定就是那个转折点呢，雷吉娜，”他激昂恳切地说，“法兰西历史上的转折点！”

“也许吧！”

===Original===
“Think if it, Régine! Think of it! Your sister, your young brother! Their name may go down to posterity as the saviours of France!”

“The saviours of France!” she murmured vaguely.

“One word has swayed a multitude before now. It may do so again﻿ ﻿… tonight!”

“Yes,” she said. “And those poor children believe in the power of their oratory.”

===Chinese===
“想想这件事吧，雷吉娜！想想吧！你的妹妹，你的小弟弟！他们的名字或许会作为法兰西的救星流传后世！”

“法兰西的救星！”她神思恍惚地低声道。

“一句话撼动过大众，这是早先就有过的事。说不定还会再来一回……就在今晚！”

“是啊，”她说。“那两个可怜的孩子，还真信他们自己那套口才的力量呢。”

===Original===
“Do not you?”

“I only remember that you, Bertrand, have probably spoken of your plan to Theresia Cabarrus, that the place will be swarming with the spies of Robespierre, and that you and the children will be recognised, seized, dragged into prison, then to the guillotine! My God!” she added, in a pitiful murmur. “And I am powerless to do anything but look on like an insentient log, whilst you run your rash heads into a noose, and then follow you all to death, whilst *maman* is left alone to perish in misery and in want.”

“A pessimist again, Régine!” he said with a forced laugh, and in his turn rose to his feet. “ ’Tis little we have accomplished this evening,” he added bitterly, “by talking.”

===Chinese===
“你就不信吗？”

“我只记得，你，贝特朗，多半已经把你们的计划讲给特蕾莎·卡巴吕斯听过了；只记得那个地方将挤满罗伯斯庇尔的暗探；只记得你和那两个孩子会被认出来、被抓住、被拖进监狱，再送上断头台！我的天哪！”她又可怜巴巴地低声说道。“而我无能为力，只能像根没有知觉的木头一样眼睁睁看着，看你们把莽撞的脑袋伸进绞索，然后再跟着你们一个个去死，撇下妈妈孤零零一个人，在穷苦愁困中了却残生。”

“又悲观了，雷吉娜！”他强笑着说，也站起身来。“靠这样空谈，”他辛酸地补了一句，“咱们今晚可算是一事无成。”

===Original===
She said nothing more. An icy chill had hold of her heart. Not only of her heart, but of her brain and her whole being. Strive as she might, she could not enter into Bertrand’s schemes, and as his whole entity was wrapped up in them she felt estranged from him, out of touch, shut out from his heart. Unspeakable bitterness filled her soul. She hated Theresia Cabarrus, who had enslaved Bertrand’s fancy, and above all she mistrusted her. At this moment she would gladly have given her life to get Bertrand away from the influence of that woman and away from that madcap association which called itself “the Fatalists,” and into which he had dragged both Joséphine and Jacques.

===Chinese===
她再没有说什么。一股冰冷的寒意攫住了她的心。不光是她的心，还有她的头脑、她的整个身心。任她怎么努力，她也无法领会贝特朗的那些密谋；而既然他的全副身心都缠在里面，她便觉得自己和他疏远了、隔膜了，被他关在了心门之外。说不出的悲苦涨满了她的灵魂。她恨特蕾莎·卡巴吕斯，是她迷住了贝特朗的心智；而更甚于此的，是她信不过她。此刻，只要能把贝特朗从那女人的影响之下拉出来，从那个自称“宿命论者”的狂妄团体里拉出来——他还把约瑟芬和雅克都拖了进去——她甘愿交出自己的性命。

===Original===
Silently she preceded him out of the little church porch, the habitual trysting-place, where at one time she had spent so many happy hours. Just before she turned off into the street, she looked back, as if through the impenetrable darkness which enveloped it now she would conjure up, just once more, those happy images of the past, but the darkness made no response to the mute cry of her fancy, and with a last sigh of intense bitterness, she followed Bertrand down the street.

===Chinese===
她默默地走在前头，同他一道走出那小小的教堂门廊——那惯常的幽会之地，她曾在那儿度过多少快活的时光。就在拐进大街之前，她回头望了一眼，仿佛要穿透此刻笼罩着它的、密不透光的黑暗，最后一次招来那些往昔的欢乐景象；可是黑暗对她这无声的痴念毫无回应，于是她带着最后一声深重的苦叹，跟着贝特朗沿街走去。

### II / II

===Original===
Less than five minutes after Bertrand and Régine had left the porch of Petit St. Antoine, the heavy oak door of the church was cautiously opened. It moved noiselessly upon its hinges, and presently through the aperture the figure of a man emerged, hardly discernible in the gloom. He slipped through the door into the porch, then closed the former noiselessly behind him.

A moment or two later his huge, bulky figure was lumbering up the Rue St. Antoine, in the direction of the Arsenal, his down-at-heel shoes making a dull clip-clop on the cobblestones. There were but very few passersby at this hour, and the man went along with his peculiar shuffling gaint until he reached the Porte St. Antoine. The city gates were still open at this hour, for it was only a little while ago that the many church clocks of the quartier had struck eight, nor did the sergeant at the gate pay much heed to the beggarly caitiff who went by; only he and the half-dozen men of the National Guard who were in charge of the gate, did remark that the belated wayfarer appeared to be in distress with a terrible asthmatic cough which caused one of the men to say with grim facetiousness:

===Chinese===
贝特朗和雷吉娜离开小圣安托万教堂（Petit St. Antoine）的门廊还不到五分钟，教堂那扇厚重的橡木大门便被小心翼翼地推开了。门在铰链上悄然转动，不多时，一条人影从门缝里钻了出来，在昏暗中几乎辨认不清。他闪身进了门廊，又把身后那扇门无声无息地掩上了。

过了一两分钟，他那庞大笨重的身影已经顺着圣安托万街（Rue St. Antoine），朝兵工厂（Arsenal）的方向蹒跚而去，磨塌了后跟的鞋在鹅卵石路面上踏出沉闷的嗒嗒声。这个时辰过路的人已经寥寥无几，那人迈着他那特有的拖沓步子，一路走到圣安托万门（Porte St. Antoine）。此刻城门还开着，因为这个街区（quartier）的许多座教堂钟不多时前刚敲过八点；守门的军士也没怎么理会这个乞丐似的可怜虫；只有他和守门的六名国民自卫军（National Guard）士兵都注意到，这个赶夜路的行人似乎正被一阵可怕的哮喘咳嗽折磨着，惹得其中一个士兵阴损地打趣道：

===Original===
“*Pardi!* but here’s a man who will not give *maman* guillotine any trouble!”

They all noticed, moreover, that after the asthmatic giant had passed through the city gate, he turned his shuffling footsteps in the direction of the Rue de la Planchette.

===Chinese===
“当然啦（Pardi）！这号人，可省得‘断头台妈妈’（maman guillotine）费事啦！”

而且，他们全都注意到，那患哮喘的巨人出了城门以后，便拖着那双拖沓的脚步，转向普朗谢特街（Rue de la Planchette）的方向去了。

## V. Rascality Rejoices / V. 无赖们的欢庆

### I / I

===Original===
The Fraternal Suppers were a great success. They were the invention of Robespierre, and the unusual warmth of these early spring evenings lent the support of their balmy atmosphere to the scheme.

Whole Paris is out in the streets on these mild April nights. Families out on a holiday, after the daily spectacle of the death-cart taking the enemies of the people, the conspirators against their liberty, to the guillotine.

And *maman* brings a basket filled with whatever scanty provisions she can save from the maximum per day allowed for the provisioning of her family. Beside her, papa comes along, dragging his youngest by the hand﻿—the latter no longer chubby and rosy, as were his prototypes in the days gone by, because food is scarce and dear, and milk unobtainable; but looking a man for all that, though barefooted and bare-kneed, with the red cap upon his lank, unwashed locks, and hugging against his meagre chest a tiny toy guillotine, the latest popular fancy, all complete with miniature knife and pulleys, and frame artistically painted a vivid crimson.

===Chinese===
兄弟晚餐会大获成功。它是罗伯斯庇尔的发明，而今岁早春夜晚少见的和暖，又以温煦宜人的气象，替这项创举帮了腔。

在这四月里温和的夜晚，整个巴黎都涌到了街上。一家家出门过节——白天刚看完每日一度的热闹：死囚车载着人民的敌人、戕害他们自由的阴谋分子，驶向断头台。

妈妈（maman）挎着一只篮子，里面装着按每日最高限价（maximum）配给一家人的口粮中、她能省下来的那一点点可怜吃食。她身边，爸爸一路走来，手里拽着最小的孩子——这孩子早已不像从前的娃娃那样白白胖胖、红扑扑粉嫩嫩了，因为食物又缺又贵，牛奶更是弄不到；可他瞧着到底有股大人模样，虽说光着脚、露着膝盖，稀黄没洗的乱发上扣着红便帽，瘦瘪的胸口还搂着一具小小的玩具断头台——时下最风行的新玩意儿，迷你刀片、滑轮一应俱全，架子还颇具匠心地漆成一片猩红。

===Original===
The Rue St. Honoré is a typical example of what goes on all over the city. Though it is very narrow and therefore peculiarly inconvenient for the holding of outdoor entertainments, the Fraternal Suppers there are extensively patronised, because the street itself is consecrated as holding the house wherein lives Robespierre.

Here, as elsewhere, huge braziers are lit at intervals, so that materfamilias may cook the few herrings she has brought with her if she be so minded, and all down the narrow street tables are set, innocent of cloths or even of that cleanliness which is next to the equally neglected virtue of godliness. But the tables have an air of cheeriness nevertheless, with resin torches, tallow candles, or old stable lanterns set here and there, the flames flickering in the gentle breeze, adding picturesqueness to the scene which might otherwise have seemed sordid, with those pewter mugs and tin plates, the horn-handled knives and iron spoons.

The scanty light does little more than accentuate the darkness around, the deep shadows under projecting balconies or lintels of portes-cochères carefully closed and barred for the night; but it glints with weird willo’-the-wisp-like fitfulness on crimson caps and tricolour cockades, on drawn and begrimed faces, bony arms, or lean, brown hands.

===Chinese===
圣奥诺雷街（Rue St. Honoré）是全城这番景象的一个典型。街道很窄，办露天宴会本该格外不便，可这里的兄弟晚餐会却宾客盈门，因为这条街本身，便因坐落着罗伯斯庇尔的寓所而被奉为了圣地。

这里同别处一样，沿街隔一段便生起大火盆，好让各家主妇高兴时烤一烤随身带来的那几条鲱鱼；窄窄的街道两旁一路摆开桌子，桌上既没有桌布，也谈不上干净——须知「清洁仅次于虔诚」，而虔诚这门德行，如今也同样乏人问津。不过桌子倒自有几分喜气：松脂火把、牛油蜡烛、旧马厩灯，东一盏西一盏地点着，火苗在微风中摇曳，给这幅景象平添了几分画意——否则，那白镴大杯、锡盘、角柄刀、铁汤匙，本要显得寒碜的。

稀疏的灯光照不出什么名堂，反倒把四周的黑夜衬得更深：挑出的阳台底下、那些入夜便仔细关严上闩的车马大门的门楣底下，一片片浓影。但这光也带着鬼火似的明灭，闪闪烁烁，落在红便帽和三色帽徽上，落在一张张枯槁污黑的脸上、一条条嶙峋的胳膊上、一只只干瘦蜡黄的手上。

===Original===
A motley throng, in truth! The workers of Paris, its proletariat, all conscripted servants of the State﻿—slaves, we might call them, though they deem themselves free men﻿—all driven into hard manual labour, partly by starvation and wholly by the decree of the Committees, who decide how and when and in what form the nation requires the arms or hands﻿—not the brains, mind you!﻿—of its citizens. For brains the nation has no use, only in the heads of those who sit in Convention or on Committees. “The State hath no use for science,” was grimly said to Lavoisier, the great chemist, when he begged for a few days’ surcease from death in order to complete some important experiments.

===Chinese===
真是一群五花八门的杂色人等！巴黎的工人们，巴黎的无产者，全是国家征来的仆役——我们不妨直呼为奴隶，虽说他们自以为是自由人——全被驱去做沉重的苦工，一半是叫饥饿逼的，全然是出于各委员会的法令：国家何时需要、以何种方式需要公民们的臂膀或双手——请注意，可不需要脑筋！——全由他们裁定。因为脑筋这东西，国家毫无用处，只除了坐在国民公会里和各委员会里的那些脑袋。「国家不需要科学」——大化学家拉瓦锡（Lavoisier）恳求宽限几日再死、好完成几项重要实验的时候，人家就这般冷冰冰地对他说。

===Original===
But coal-heavers are useful citizens of the State; so are smiths and armourers and gunmakers, and those who can sew and knit stockings, do anything in fact to clothe and feed the national army, the defenders of the sacred soil of France. For them, for those workers﻿—the honest, the industrious, the sober﻿—are the Fraternal Suppers invented; but not for them only. There are the “*tricotteuses*,” sexless hags, who, by order of the State, sit at the foot of the scaffold surrounded by their families and their children and knit, and knit, the while they jeer﻿—still by order of the State﻿—at the condemned﻿—old men, young women, children even, as they walk up to the guillotine. There are the “*insulteuses publiques*,” public insulters, women mostly﻿—save the mark!﻿—paid to howl and blaspheme as the death-carts rattle by. There are the “*tappe-durs*,” the hit-hards, who, armed with weighted sticks, form the bodyguard around the sacred person of Robespierre. Then, the members of the Société Révolutionnaire, recruited from the refuse of misery and of degradation of this great city; and﻿—oh, the horror of it all!﻿—the “*Enfants Rouges*,” the red children, who cry “Death” and “*à la lanterne*” with the best of them﻿—precocious little offsprings of the new Republic. For them, too, are the Fraternal Suppers established: for all the riffraff, all the sweepings of abject humanity. For they too must be amused and entertained, lest they sit in clusters and talk themselves into the belief that they are more wretched, more indigent, more abased, than they were in the days of monarchical oppression.

===Chinese===
不过，运煤工倒算国家有用的公民；铁匠、军械匠、造枪匠也一样，还有那些会缝会织袜子的，凡能给国家军队——法兰西神圣国土的保卫者——供衣供食的行当，都有用处。为这些人，为那些工人——诚实的、勤勉的、清醒的——兄弟晚餐会才被发明出来；但也不光是为他们。还有那些「编织妇」（tricotteuses），不男不女的老妖婆，奉国家之命坐在断头台脚下，自家儿女围作一圈，一边织啊，织啊，一边嘲骂——同样奉国家之命——那些走向断头台的死囚：老翁、少妇，连孩童也不放过。还有「公共辱骂妇」（insulteuses publiques），职业辱骂手——这称呼于她们真是抬举——多为女人，受雇在死囚车辚辚驶过时嚎叫咒骂。还有「打手」（tappe-durs），一手灌铅的棍棒，围着罗伯斯庇尔神圣的尊躯组成卫队。再有，革命社（Société Révolutionnaire）的社员，从这座大城的苦难与堕落的渣滓里招募而来；还有——哦，这一切想想都叫人胆寒！——「红色孩童」（Enfants Rouges），一帮红孩子，喊起「处死」和「吊上灯杆！」（à la lanterne）来不输任何好汉——新共和国早熟的小苗苗。为他们，兄弟晚餐会也同样设立：为一切无赖卑贱之徒，为人类渣滓的全数扫拢。因为这些货色也须得有人逗乐取悦，免得他们成堆坐在一处，你一言我一语说动了心，真相信自己比在君主专制的年头更凄惨、更穷困、更下贱。

### II / II

===Original===
And so, on these balmy evenings of mid-April, family parties are gathered in the open air, around meagre suppers that are “fraternal” by order of the State. Family parties which make for camaraderie between the honest man and the thief, the sober citizen and the homeless vagabond, and help one to forget awhile the misery, the starvation, the slavery, the daily struggle for bare existence, in anticipation of the belated Millennium.

There is even laughter around the festive boards, fun and frolic. Jokes are cracked, mostly of a grim order. There is intoxication in the air: spring has got into the heads of the young. And there is even kissing under the shadows, lovemaking, sentiment; and here and there perhaps a shred of real happiness.

The provisions are scanty. Every family brings its own. Two or three herrings, sprinkled with shredded onions and wetted with a little vinegar, or else a few boiled prunes or a pottage of lentils and beans.

===Chinese===
就这样，在四月中旬这些温和的夜晚，一家家人聚到露天里，围着奉国家之命办得「友爱」的寒酸晚餐。这样的家庭聚会，倒促成了诚实人与窃贼、安分公民与无家流浪汉之间的称兄道弟，教人暂且忘掉苦难、饥饿、奴役、为糊口而日日的挣扎，一心盼着那迟迟不来的千禧年。

节日的餐桌四周甚至还有笑声，有打趣，有嬉闹。笑话讲了一箩筐，多半阴森森的。空气里浮着一股醉意：春天钻进了年轻人的脑袋。暗影里甚至还有人接吻，谈情，说爱；这里那里，说不定还真有一星半点实实在在的幸福。

吃食是菲薄的。家家自带。两三条鲱鱼，撒一把葱丝，浇一点醋；再不，就是几颗煮梅子，或者一锅扁豆菜豆糊。

===Original===
“Can you spare some of that bread, citizen?”

“Aye! if I can have a bite of your cheese.”

They are fraternal suppers! Do not, in the name of Liberty and Equality, let us forget that. And the whole of it was Robespierre’s idea. He conceived and carried it through, commanded the voices in the Convention that voted the money required for the tables, the benches, the tallow candles. He lives close by, in this very street, humbly, quietly, like a true son of the people, sharing house and board with citizen Duplay, the cabinetmaker, and with his family.

===Chinese===
「公民，你那面包能匀我一点儿吗？」

「行啊！只要让我咬一口你的奶酪。」

这可是兄弟晚餐会啊！看在自由与平等的份上，咱们可千万别忘了这一点。而这一切全是罗伯斯庇尔的主意。他想出来，办成了，还指挥国民公会里那些唯命是从的嗓子，投票通过办这事儿所需的款项，购置桌子、板凳、牛油蜡烛。他自己就住在近旁，就在这条街上，住得朴素，住得安静，像人民真正的好儿子，同木匠迪普莱（Duplay）公民一家同吃同住。

===Original===
A great man, Robespierre! The only man! Men speak of him with bated breath, young girls with glowing eyes. He is the fetish, the idol, the demigod. No benefactor of mankind, no saint, no hero-martyr was ever worshipped more devotedly than this death-dealing monster by his votaries. Even the shade of Danton is reviled in order to exalt the virtues of his successful rival.

“Danton was gorged with riches: his pockets full, his stomach satisfied! But look at Robespierre!”

“Almost a wraith!﻿—so thin, so white!”

“An ascetic!”

===Chinese===
伟大的人物，罗伯斯庇尔！举世无双的人物！人们提起他，屏息凝神；姑娘们说起他，两眼放光。他是神物，是偶像，是半神。人间的恩主、圣徒、殉道的英烈，从没有谁受信徒崇奉得比这个杀人魔王更虔诚的。就连丹东的亡魂，也要遭人唾骂，好衬出这位得胜对手的种种美德。

「丹东叫钱财撑饱了：口袋满满的，肚皮饱饱的！你倒再看看罗伯斯庇尔！」

「简直是一缕幽魂！——又瘦，又白！」

「一个苦行者！」

===Original===
“Consumed by the fire of his own patriotism.”

“His eloquence!”

“His selflessness!”

“You have heard him speak, citizen?”

===Chinese===
「叫自己那腔爱国之火烧干了。」

「他的口才！」

「他的无私！」

「你听他讲过话吧，公民？」

===Original===
A girl, still in her ’teens, her elbows resting on the table, her hands supporting her rounded chin, asks the question with bated breath. Her large grey eyes, hollow and glowing, are fixed upon her vis-à-vis, a tall, ungainly creature, who sprawls over the table, vainly trying to dispose of his long limbs in a manner comfortable to himself.

His hair is lank and matted with grease, his face covered in coal-dust; a sennight’s growth of beard, stubbly and dusty, accentuates the squareness of his jaw even whilst it fails to conceal altogether the cruel, sarcastic curves of his mouth. But for the moment, in the rapt eyes of the young enthusiast, he is a prophet, a seer, a human marvel: he has heard Robespierre speak.

“Was it in the Club, citizen Rateau?” another woman asks﻿—a young matron with a poor little starveling at her breast.

===Chinese===
一个还不到二十岁的姑娘，胳膊肘支在桌上，双手托着圆圆的下巴，屏着气息问出这句话。她那双灰色的大眼睛，深陷而灼灼发亮，直勾勾盯住对面那人——一个高大笨拙的家伙，摊手摊脚趴在桌子上，怎么也安放不好自己那两条长胳膊长腿。

他头发稀软，板结着油垢，满脸煤灰；七天没刮的胡子茬，又硬又脏，愈发衬出他下巴的方阔，却仍旧遮不尽那张嘴残酷刻毒的弧线。可是此刻，在这年轻崇拜者出神的眼睛里，他是先知，是先见，是人间的奇人：他听过罗伯斯庇尔讲话。

「是在俱乐部里吗，拉托（Rateau）公民？」另一个女人问道——是个年轻的母亲，怀里抱着个可怜巴巴、面黄肌瘦的小奶娃。

===Original===
The man gives a loud guffaw, displays in the feeble, flickering light of the nearest torch a row of hideous uneven teeth, scored with gaps and stained with tobacco juice.

“In the Club?” he says with a curse, and spits in a convenient direction to show of his contempt for that or any other institution. “I don’t belong to any Club. There’s no money in my pocket. And the Jacobins and the Cordeliers like to see a man with a decent coat on his back.”

His guffaw broke in a rasping cough which seemed to tear his broad chest to ribbons. For a moment speech was denied him; even oaths failed to reach his lips, trembling like an unset jelly in this distressing spasm. His neighbours alongside the table, the young enthusiast opposite, the comely matron, paid no heed to him﻿—waited indifferently until the clumsy lout had regained his breath. This, mark you, was not an era of gentleness or womanly compassion, and an asthmatic mudlark was not like to excite pity. Only when he once more stretched out his long limbs, raised his head and looked about him, panting and blear-eyed, did the girl insist quietly:

===Chinese===
那男人放声哈哈一笑，就着最近一支火把微弱摇曳的光，露出一口参差不齐的难看牙齿——豁着缝，浸透了烟油子的黄。

「俱乐部里？」他骂骂咧咧地说，朝顺手的方向啐了一口，表示他对那个机构以及任何机构的不屑。「我什么俱乐部也不沾。我兜里没有一个子儿。雅各宾俱乐部和科德利埃俱乐部，可爱看人穿件像样的外衣呢。」

一声大笑随即迸成一阵撕心的咳嗽，仿佛要把他那宽阔的胸膛撕成碎片。有那么一会儿，他说不出话来，连咒骂都够不着嘴边，只在那阵难堪的痉挛里抖得像一坨没凝住的肉冻。同桌的邻座、对面的年轻崇拜者、那位俊俏的母亲，谁也不理会他——只漠然等着这个笨拙的蠢汉缓过气来。要知道，这可不是个温柔存厚的年代，一个喘得像拉风箱的拾荒汉，是引不来怜悯的。直到他终于又伸开那两条长腿，抬起头，喘着粗气、眨着模糊的眼睛四下张望，那姑娘才执拗地轻声追问：

===Original===
“But you have heard *Him* speak!”

“Aye!” the ruffian replied drily. “I did.”

“When?”

“Night before last. Tenez! He was stepping out of citizen Duplay’s house yonder. He saw me leaning against the wall close by. I was tired, half asleep, what? He spoke to me and asked me where I lived.”

===Chinese===
「可你听过『他』讲话！」

「听过！」那粗汉干巴巴地答道。「听过。」

「什么时候？」

「前天夜里。你瞧（Tenez）！他正从那边的迪普莱公民家里出来。他看见我靠在近旁的墙上。我累了，半睡半醒的，怎么样？他跟我说话，还问我住在哪儿。」

===Original===
“Where you lived?” the girl echoed, disappointed.

“Was that all?” the matron added with a shrug of her shoulders.

The neighbours laughed. The men enjoyed the discomfiture of the women, who were all craning their necks to hear something great, something palpitating, about their idol.

The young enthusiast sighed, clasped her hands in favour.

===Chinese===
「问您住哪儿？」姑娘应了一声，好不失望。

「就这些？」那位母亲耸耸肩，补了一句。

邻座的人都笑了。男人们乐得看女人们受窘——她们方才还一个个伸长脖子，正想听听关于她们偶像的了不起的、动人心魄的大事呢。

年轻的崇拜者叹了口气，满心虔诚地合起双掌。

===Original===
“He saw that you were poor, citizen Rateau,” she said with conviction; “and that you were tired. He wished to help and comfort you.”

“And where did you say you lived, citizen?” the young matron went on, in her calm, matter-of-fact tone.

“I live far from here, the other side of the water, not in an aristocratic quarter like this one﻿—what?”

“You told *Him* you lived there?” the girl still insisted. Any scrap or crumb of information even remotely connected with her idol was manna to her body and balm to her soul.

===Chinese===
「他看出来您穷，拉托公民，」她深信不疑地说，「也看出来您累了。他是想帮您、安慰您来着。」

「您方才说您住在哪儿来着，公民？」那位年轻母亲又往下问，用的是她那种心平气和、就事论事的调子。

「我住得远，在河对岸，可住不惯这么个贵族区——怎么样？」

「您跟『他』说了，您就住那儿？」姑娘还不肯罢休。但凡与她的偶像沾上一星半点边的关系，哪怕一鳞半爪的消息，于她的身体都是吗哪，于她的灵魂都是香膏。

===Original===
“Yes, I did,” citizen Rateau assented.

“Then,” the girl resumed earnestly, “solance and comfort will come to you very soon, citizen. He never forgets. His eyes are upon you. He knows your distress and that you are poor and weary. Leave it to him, citizen Rateau. He will know how and when to help.”

“He will know, more like,” here broke in a harsh voice, vibrating with excitement, “how and when to lay his talons on an obscure and helpless citizen whenever his Batches for the guillotine are insufficient to satisfy his lust!”

===Chinese===
「说了。」拉托公民认了。

「那么，」姑娘又恳切地说下去，「安慰和舒坦很快就会临到您头上，公民。他从不忘记人。他的眼睛看着您呢。他知道您的苦处，知道您又穷又累。只管交托给他，拉托公民。他自会知道该怎样帮、何时帮。」

「更可能的倒是，」这时一个尖利的嗓门插了进来，激动得发颤，「他知道该怎样、该几时把魔爪攫住某个无名无势的小民——每当送上断头台的那一批批『货』不够填他的欲壑的时候！」

===Original===
A dull murmur greeted this tirade. Only those who sat close by the speaker knew which he was, for the lights were scanty and burnt dim in the open air. The others only heard﻿—received this arrow-shot aimed at their idol﻿—with for the most part a kind of dull resentment. The women were more loudly indignant. One or two young devotees gave a shrill cry or so of passionate indignation.

“Shame! Treason!”

“Guillotine, forsooth! The enemies of the people all deserve the guillotine!”

And the enemies of the people were those who dared raise their voice against their Chosen, their Fetish, the great, incomprehensible Mystery.

===Chinese===
这番话招来一阵低沉的嗡嗡声。只有坐在说话人近旁的才知道说话的是谁，因为露天里灯光稀疏，烧得昏暗。旁的人只是听见了——领受了这支射向他们偶像的暗箭——多半带着一种闷闷的愤懑。女人们嚷嚷得更响。一两个年轻女信徒发出几声激愤难当的尖叫。

「无耻！叛国！」

「断头台？亏你说得出口！人民的敌人个个都该上断头台！」

而所谓人民的敌人，就是那些胆敢开口反对他们的选民、他们的神物——那伟大而莫可究诘的玄秘——的人。

===Original===
Citizen Rateau was once more rendered helpless by a tearing fit of coughing.

But from afar, down the street, there came one or two assenting cries.

“Well spoken, young man! As for me, I never trusted that bloodhound!”

And a woman’s voice added shrilly: “His hands reek of blood. A butcher, I call him!”

“And a tyrant!” assented the original spokesman. “His aim is a dictatorship, with his minions hanging around him like abject slaves. Why not Versailles, then? How are we better off now than in the days of kingship? Then, at least, the streets of Paris did not stink of blood. Then, at least﻿—”

===Chinese===
拉托公民又被一阵撕心裂肺的咳嗽折腾得没了办法。

可是从远处街那头，传来了一两声应和的呼喊。

「说得好，小伙子！要我说，那条猎犬，我从来没信过！」

一个女人的嗓音又尖尖地帮腔：「他两只手血腥气冲天。要我说，他就是个屠夫！」

「还是个暴君！」最初那位发言人应道。「他要的是独裁，好让他的走狗们像卑贱的奴隶一样围着他打转。那何不干脆回凡尔赛（Versailles）去？如今咱们的日子，比王政那阵子又强在哪儿？那时候，至少巴黎的街道还不带血腥臭。那时候，至少——」

===Original===
But the speaker got no father. A hard crust of very dry, black bread, aimed by a sure hand, caught him full in the face, whilst a hoarse voice shouted lustily:

“Hey there, citizen! If thou’lt not hold thy tongue ’tis thy neck that will be reeking with blood o’er soon, I’ll warrant!”

“Well said, citizen Rateau!” put in another, speaking with his mouth full, but with splendid conviction. “Every word uttered by that jackanapes yonder reeks of treason!”

===Chinese===
可是这位发言人再没能往下说。一块又干又硬的黑面包皮，被一只稳当的手掷出，不偏不倚正砸在他脸上，同时一个沙哑的嗓门起劲地吼道：

「喂，那边的公民！你要是不肯闭上嘴，很快就要轮到你的脖子淌血啦，我敢担保！」

「说得好，拉托公民！」另一个人插嘴，嘴里塞得鼓鼓的，话说得却斩钉截铁。「那边那只轻狂的小丑吐出来的每一个字，都冒着叛国的臭气！」

===Original===
“Shame!” came from every side.

“Where are the agents of the Committee of Public Safety? Men have been thrown into prison for less than this.”

“Shame!”

“Denounce him!”

“Take him to the nearest Section!”

“Ere he wreaks mischief more lasting than words!” cried a woman, who tried as she spoke to give her utterance its full, sinister meaning.

===Chinese===
「无耻！」四面八方都喊了起来。

「公共安全委员会的暗探都上哪儿去了？比这轻的话，都有人蹲了大牢呢。」

「无耻！」

「告发他！」

「把他扭送到最近的区（Section）去！」

「趁他还没闯出比几句话更经久的祸害来！」一个女人喊道，一面喊，一面竭力让这话带上它全部阴森的分量。

===Original===
“Shame! Treason!” came soon from every side. Voices were raised all down the length of the tables﻿—shrill, full-throated, even dull and indifferent. Some really felt indignation﻿—burning, ferocious indignation; others only made a noise for the sheer pleasure of it, and because the past five years had turned cries of “Treason!” and of “Shame!” into a habit. Not that they knew what the disturbance was about. The street was long and narrow, and the cries came some way from where they were sitting; but when cries of “Treason!” flew through the air these days, ’twas best to join in, lest those cries turned against one, and the next stage in the proceedings became the approach of an Agent of the Sûreté, the nearest prison, and the inevitable guillotine.

So everyone cried “Shame!” and “Treason!” whilst those who had first dared to raise their voices against the popular demagogue drew together into a closer batch, trying no doubt to gather courage through one another’s proximity. Eager, excited, a small compact group of two men﻿—one a mere boy﻿—and three women, it almost seemed as if they were suffering from some temporary hallucination. How else would five isolated persons﻿—three of them in their first youth﻿—have dared to brave a multitude?

===Chinese===
「无耻！叛国！」很快也四面八方响成了一片。长桌两侧处处扬起了嗓门——尖利的、敞开喉咙的，也有麻木不仁随口嚷嚷的。有的人是当真心怀义愤——火烧火燎、凶猛狂暴的义愤；有的人纯粹是起哄，图个热闹，何况过去这五年，早把「叛国！」「无耻！」喊成了习惯。他们才不知道这场喧闹是为了什么呢。街又长又窄，喊声离他们坐的地方还有好一段路；可如今只要「叛国！」的喊声在空中飞，最好是跟着一起喊，免得那喊声掉转头来冲着自己，事态的下一步，便成了治安警务处（Sûreté）的密探登门、最近的一座监狱伺候、还有那躲不掉的断头台收场。

于是人人都在喊「无耻！」「叛国！」而那几个头一个胆敢冲着这位得人心的煽动家开腔的人，却缩作了一小团，挤得更紧了——多半是想靠彼此的贴近壮一壮胆。两个男人——其中一个不过是半大的孩子——加上三个女人，一小群挤作一团的人，兴头十足，激动万分，看上去倒像是集体犯了什么一时的癔症。不然的话，五个孤零零的人——其中三个还正当青春年少——怎么敢去犯一大群人的众怒呢？

===Original===
In truth Bertrand Moncrif, face to face as he believed with martyrdom, was like one transfigured. Always endowed with good looks, he appeared like a veritable young prophet, haranguing the multitude and foretelling its doom. The gloom partly hid his figure, but his hand was outstretched, and the outline of an avenging finger pointing straight out before him, appeared in the weird light of the resin torch, as if carved in glowing lava. Now and then the fitful light caught the sharp outline of his face﻿—the straight nose and pointed chin, and brown hair matted with the sweat of enthusiasm.

Beside him Régine, motionless and white as a wraith, appeared alive only by her eyes, which were fixed on her beloved. In the hulking giant with the asthmatic cough she had recognised the man to whom she had ministered earlier in the day. Somehow, his presence here and now seemed to her sinister and threatening. It seemed as if all day he had been dogging her footsteps: first at the soothsayer’s, then he surely must have followed her down the street. Then he had inspired her with pity; now his hideous face, his grimy hands, that croaking voice and churchyard cough, filled her with nameless terror.

He appeared to her excited fancy like a veritable spectre of death, hovering over Bertrand and over those she loved. With one arm she tried to press her brother Jacques closer to her breast, to quench his eagerness and solence his foolhardy tongue. But he, like a fierce, impatient young animal, fought to free himself from her loving embrace, shouted approval to Bertrand’s oratory, played his part of the young propagandist, heedless of Régine’s warnings and of his mother’s tears. Next to Régine, her sister Joséphine﻿—a girl not out of her ’teens, with all the eagerness and exaggeration of extreme youth, was shouting quite as loudly as her brother Jacques, clapping her small hands together, turning glowing, defying, arrogant eyes on the crowd of great unwashed whom she hoped to sway with her ardour and her eloquence.

===Chinese===
说实在的，贝特朗·蒙克里夫（Bertrand Moncrif）自以为正直面对着殉道，整个人像脱胎换骨了一般。他本就生得俊美，此刻俨然一位真正的年轻先知，正向群众慷慨陈词，预言着他们的劫数。幽暗遮住了他的半边身影，可他的手伸了出去，一支复仇的手指笔直指向前方，轮廓映在松脂火把诡异的亮光里，仿佛用灼热的熔岩雕成。时明时灭的光，不时照出他脸庞分明的轮廓——挺直的鼻梁，尖尖的下巴，还有因热狂的汗水而黏结的棕色头发。

他身旁的雷吉娜（Régine），一动不动，白得像个幽灵，全靠那双眼睛才显出她是个活人——那双眼睛定定地望着她心爱的人。她认出了那个咳嗽不停、害着哮喘的大块头巨人，正是今天早些时候她照应过的那个人。不知怎么，他此时此刻出现在这里，在她看来透着凶险，透着威胁。他仿佛一整天都在缀着她的脚步：先是在女占卜师那里，后来，他又必定是尾随着她穿过了那条街。那时候，他引起的是她的怜悯；而现在，他那狰狞的脸、污黑的手、那副嘎嘎的嗓音和坟场似的咳嗽，却让她满心无名恐惧。

在她激动失措的想象里，他俨然是一个真正的死神厉鬼，盘旋在贝特朗和她所爱的人们头顶。她用一条胳膊把弟弟雅克（Jacques）往怀里搂得更紧，想按住他的热狂，堵住他那条莽撞的舌头。可他像一头凶猛急躁的小兽，拼命要从她那满怀爱意的搂抱里挣出去，高声为贝特朗的演说喝彩，卖力扮着他那少年鼓动家的角色，全不顾雷吉娜的警告和母亲的眼泪。雷吉娜旁边是妹妹约瑟芬（Joséphine）——一个还没满二十的姑娘，带着妙龄的全部热切与偏激，喊得同弟弟雅克一样响亮，一双小手拍得啪啪的，把灼灼的、挑战的、傲慢的眼睛投向那群汗臭熏天的芸芸众生，满心指望凭自己的热忱和口才把他们鼓动起来。

===Original===
“Shame on us all!” she cried with passionate vehemence. “Shame on us French women and French men that we should be the abject slaves of such a bloodthirsty tyrant!”

Her mother, pale-faced, delicate, had obviously long since given up all hope of controlling this unruly little crowd. She was too listless, too anemic, had no doubt suffered too much already, to be afraid for herself or for her children. She was past any thought or fear. Her wan face only expressed despair﻿—despair that was absolutely final﻿—and the resignation of silent self-immolation, content to suffer beside those she loved, only praying to be allowed to share their martyrdom, even though she had no part in their enthusiasm.

Bertrand, Joséphine and Jacques had all the ardour of martyrdom. Régine and her mother all its resignation.

===Chinese===
「我们大家都该羞死！」她激愤填膺地喊道。「法兰西的男人女人竟做了这么一个嗜血暴君的卑贱奴隶，我们都该羞死！」

她们的母亲，脸色苍白，身单力薄，显然早就断了管束这一小群桀骜不驯者的指望了。她太倦怠，太贫血，无疑也已经受够了苦，再也吓不动了——无论为自己，还是为孩子。她已超脱了一切思虑与恐惧。那张憔悴的脸上只写着绝望——一种彻底到头、再无余地的绝望——和默默自祭般的听天由命：能守在她所爱的人身边一同受苦便心满意足，只求容她分享他们的殉道，哪怕他们的热狂，她分毫也无意沾染。

贝特朗、约瑟芬和雅克，怀有殉道的全部热狂；雷吉娜和母亲，则怀有殉道的全部隐忍。

### III / III

===Original===
The Fraternal Supper threatened to end in a free fight, wherein the only salvation for the young fire-eaters would lie in a swift taking to their heels. And even then the chances would be hopelessly against them. Spies of the Convention, spies of the Committees, spies of Robespierre himself, swarmed all over the place. They were marked men and women, those five. It was useless to appear defiant and high-minded and patriotic. Even Danton had gone to the guillotine for less.

“Shame! Treason!”

The balmy air of mid-Apirl seemed to echo the sinister words, but Bertrand appeared unconscious of all danger. Nay! it almost seemed as if he courted it.

===Chinese===
这场兄弟晚餐会眼看要以一场混战收场，到了那步田地，这几个逞血气之勇的年轻人，唯一的活路便是撒腿就跑。可就算跑了，胜算也渺茫得没了指望。国民公会的密探、各委员会的密探、罗伯斯庇尔本人的密探，满场乱钻。他们那五个人，早被记上名、画上卯了。装出一副桀骜、清高、爱国的架势，毫无用处。比这轻的罪名，连丹东都上了断头台。

「无耻！叛国！」

四月中旬温和的空气，仿佛也在回荡这阴森的字眼。可贝特朗对一切危险浑然不觉。岂止不觉！他简直是在自招灾祸。

===Original===
“Shame on you all!” he called out loudly, and his fresh, sonorous voice rang out above the tumult and the hoarse murmurings. “Shame on the people of France for bowing their necks to such monstrous tyranny. Citizens of Paris, think on it! Is not Liberty a mockery now? Do you call your bodies your own? They are but food for cannon at the bidding of the Convention. Your families? You are parted from those you love. Your wife? You are torn from her embrace. Your children? They are taken from you for the service of the State. And by whose orders? Tell me that! By whose orders, I say?”

He was lashing himself into a veritable fury of self-sacrifice, stood up beside the table and with a gesture even bade Joséphine and Jacques be still. As for Régine, she hardly was conscious that she lived, so acute, so poignant was her emotion, so gaunt and real the approach of death which threatened her beloved.

===Chinese===
「你们大家都该羞死！」他高声喊道，清朗洪亮的嗓音压过了那片喧嚣与沙哑的低吼。「法兰西的人民竟把脖子弯给这样滔天的暴政，该羞死！巴黎的公民们，想一想吧！自由如今岂不成了笑柄？你们还能管自己的身体叫自己的吗？那不过是国民公会一声令下、填进炮膛的炮灰。你们的家庭呢？你们被迫与所爱的人离散。你们的妻子呢？你们被人从她的怀抱里撕走。你们的孩子呢？被国家夺了去当差。这都凭着谁的命令？告诉我！凭着谁的命令，我问你们？」

他把自己鞭策进一种货真价实的舍身狂怒里，从桌边站起身来，还挥了挥手，示意约瑟芬和雅克安静。至于雷吉娜，她简直意识不到自己还活着——她的情感太锐、太痛，死亡的迫近太嶙峋、太真切，正一步步逼向她所爱的人。

===Original===
This of course was the end﻿—this folly, this mad, senseless, useless folly! Already through the gloom she could see as in a horrible vision all those she cared for dragged before a tribunal that knew of no mercy; she could hear the death-carts rattling along the cobblestones, she could see the hideous arms of the guillotine, ready to receive this unique, this believed, this precious prey. She could feel Joséphine’s arms clinging pitiably to her for courage; she could see Jacques’ defiant young face, glorying in martyrdom; she could see *maman*, drooping like a faded flower, bereft of what was life to her﻿—the nearness of her children. She could see Bertrand, turning with a dying look of love, not to her but to the beautiful Spaniard who had captured his fancy and then sold him without compunction to the spies of Robespierre and of her own party.

===Chinese===
这一下自然是全完了——这桩蠢事，这桩疯狂、无谓、毫无用处的蠢事！透过幽暗，她已经能像看见一场恐怖的幻象似的，看见她牵挂的所有人被拖到一个不知慈悲为何物的法庭前；她能听见死囚车在鹅卵石上辚辚驶过，能看见断头台狰狞的铁架，正候着承接这份独一无二、备受珍爱、无比贵重的猎物。她能感到约瑟芬的手臂可怜巴巴地紧挽着她讨勇气；能看见雅克那张倔强的少年脸庞，正为殉道而神采飞扬；能看见妈妈，像一朵凋残的花般枯萎下去——失去了她的性命所系：儿女的依傍。她还能看见贝特朗，临终投去含情的一瞥——不是望着她，而是望着那个先迷住了他的心、后来又毫无愧色地把他出卖给罗伯斯庇尔的暗探以及她自家一党的美艳西班牙女子。

### IV / IV

===Original===
But for the fact that this was a “Fraternal Supper,” that people had come out here with their families, their young children, to eat and to make merry and to forget all their troubles as well as the pall of crime that hung over the entire city, I doubt not but what the young Hotspur and his crowd of rashlings would ere now have been torn from their eats, trampled under foot, at best been dragged to the nearest Commissary, as the asthmatic citizen Rateau had already threatened. Even as it was, the temper of many a paterfamilias was sorely tried by this insistence, with wilful twisting of the tigers’ tails. And the women were on the verge of reprisals. As for Rateau, he just seemed to gather his huge limbs together, uttered an impatient oath and an angry: “By all the cats and dogs that render this world hideous with their howls, I have had about enough of this screeching oratory.” Then he threw one long leg over the bench on which he had been sitting, and in an instant was lost in the gloom, only to reappear in the dim light a few seconds later, this time on the farther side of the table, immediately behind the young rhetorician, his ugly, begrimed face with its grinning, toothless mouth and his broad, bent shoulders towering above the other’s slender figure.

===Chinese===
若不是这到底是场「兄弟晚餐会」，若不是人们携妻带子出来吃吃喝喝、寻个乐子、好把满腹烦忧连同笼罩全城的罪孽之幕一概忘掉，我毫不怀疑，这位少年急先锋（Hotspur）和他那一伙莽撞的毛头小子，这会儿早被人从吃食跟前撕下来、踩到脚底下去了，至多也就是被拖到最近的警务专员（Commissary）那里——那个害哮喘的拉托公民早就这么撂过狠话了。饶是如此，这般存心捋虎须、没完没了地纠缠，多少位一家之长的火气也被撩得几乎按捺不住。女人们也眼看就要动手还击了。至于拉托，他只是把庞大的四肢一拢，骂了句不耐烦的粗话，又怒冲冲添上一句：「凭着满世界那些嚎得人间乌烟瘴气的猫儿狗儿起誓，这种尖嗓子的演说，我可听够了。」说着，他把一条长腿跨过坐着的板凳，转眼便没入了黑暗，几秒钟后又在昏暗的光里现出身来——这回是在桌子那一头，恰在那位年轻雄辩家的背后：那张污黑的丑脸龇着没牙的嘴直乐，宽阔佝偻的双肩，高高耸在对方那纤瘦的身躯之上。

===Original===
“Knock him down, citizen!” a young woman cried excitedly. “Hit him in the face! Silence his abominable tongue!”

But Bertrand was not to be silenced yet. No doubt the fever of notoriety, of martyrdom, had got into his blood. His youth, his good looks﻿—obvious even in the fitful light and despite his tattered clothes﻿—were an asset in his favour, no doubt; but a man-eating tiger is apt to be indiscriminate in his appetites and will devour a child with as much gusto as a gaffer; and this youthful firebrand was teasing the man-eating tiger with reckless insistence.

“By whose orders,” he reiterated, with passionate vehemence, “by whose orders are we, free citizens of France, dragged into this abominable slavery? Is it by those of the Representatives of the People? No! Of the Committees chosen by the People? No! Of your Municipalities? your Clubs? your Sections? No! and again No! Your bodies, citizens, your freedom, your wives, your children, are all slaves, the property, the toys of one man﻿—real tyrant and traitor, the oppressor of the weak, the enemy of the people; and that man is﻿—”

===Chinese===
「揍倒他，公民！」一个年轻女人激动地喊道。「照他脸上打！叫那条可恶的舌头住嘴！」

可是贝特朗还不肯住嘴。无疑，那种求名的热病、殉道的热病，早已钻进了他的血液。他年轻，他俊美——即便在明灭不定的光里、纵然一身褴褛，也遮掩不住——这无疑是他的本钱；可是吃人的老虎胃口是不加分辨的，吞个孩子同吞个老汉一样有滋有味；而这根年轻的火捻子，正不管不顾、一而再再而三地撩弄这头吃人的老虎。

「凭着谁的命令，」他再三逼问，激愤填膺，「凭着谁的命令，我们法兰西的自由公民，被拖进了这可憎的奴役？是人民代表们的命令吗？不是！是人民选出的各委员会的吗？不是！是你们市政府的？你们俱乐部的？你们各区的？不！还是不！公民们，你们的身体、你们的自由、你们的妻子、你们的孩子，全都是奴隶，是一个人的财产、一个人的玩物——一个不折不扣的暴君和叛徒，欺压弱小者，人民的公敌；而那个人就是——」

===Original===
Again he was interrupted, this time more forcibly. A terrific blow on the head deprived him of speech and of sight. His senses reeled, there was a mighty buzzing in his ears, which effectually drowned the cries of execration or of approval that greeted his tirade, as well as a new and deafening tumult which filled the whole narrow street with its weird and hideous sounds.

Whence the blow had come, Bertrand had no notion. It had all been so swift. He had expected to be torn limb from limb, to be dragged to the nearest Commissariat: he courted condemnation, envisaged the guillotine; ’stead of which, he was prosily knocked down by a bow which would have felled an ox.

===Chinese===
他又被打断了，这一回却来得更结实。当头狠狠一击，打得他登时丧失了言语和视觉。他的知觉天旋地转，耳朵里嗡的一声巨响，把那番话招来的咒骂也好、喝彩也好，统统盖了下去，也盖住了另一阵新的、震耳欲聋的骚动——那阵怪诞而狰狞的声响，塞满了整条窄街。

这一拳是打哪儿来的，贝特朗全无概念。一切来得太快。他原想着要被撕成碎块，要被拖到最近的警务分署去：他求的就是被判罪，想的就是断头台；不想人家却平平常常一拳把他撂倒——这一拳，连头公牛也打得翻。

===Original===
Just for a second, his fast-fading perceptions struggled back into consciousness. He had a swift vision of a giant form towering over him, with grimy fist uplifted and toothless mouth grinning hideously, and of the crowd, rising from their seats, turning their backs upon him, waving their arms and caps frantically, and shouting, shouting, with vociferous lustiness. He also had an equally swift pang of remorse as the faces of his companions﻿—of Régine and Mme. de Serval, of Joséphine and Jacques﻿—whom he dragged with him into this mad and purposeless outburst, rose prophetically before him fro out the gloom, with wide-eyed, scared faces and arms uplifted to ward off vengeful blows.

But the next moment these lightning-like visions faded into complete oblivion. He felt something hard and heavy hitting him in the back. All the lights, the faces, the outstretched hands, danced wildly before his eyes, and he sank like a log on the greasy pavement, dragging pewter plates, mugs and bottles down with him in his fall.

===Chinese===
只一秒钟，他迅速消散的知觉又挣扎着回到了清醒。他飞快地瞥见一个巨大的身影耸立在他头顶，扬着污黑的拳头，没牙的嘴狞笑着；又瞥见人群纷纷离座，背转身去对着他，发狂似的挥着手臂和帽子，扯开嗓子喊啊，喊啊，喊得中气十足。他也同样飞快地感到一阵悔恨的刺痛——他的同伴们的脸——雷吉娜和德·塞尔瓦尔夫人（Mme. de Serval），约瑟芬和雅克，那些被他拖进这场疯狂而无谓的发作里的人——一张张从幽暗中预兆似的在他眼前升起：瞪着惊恐的眼睛，高举着手臂，招架着复仇的老拳。

可下一刻，这些闪电似的幻象便消隐于全然的遗忘。他觉出一件又硬又重的东西砸在他背上。所有的灯火、脸庞、伸出的手，都在他眼前狂乱地飞舞，他像根木头似的栽倒在油腻的街石上，白镴盘子、大杯、酒瓶，都叫他一带，跟着他一齐栽了下去。

## VI. One Crowded Hour of Glorious Life / VI. 光辉人生中浓烈充实的一小时

### I / I

===Original===
And all the while, the people were shouting:

“*Le violà!*”

“Robespierre!”

===Chinese===
这时候，人群一直在喊：

“瞧，他来啦！（Le violà!）”

“罗伯斯庇尔！”

===Original===
The Fraternal Supper was interrupted. Men and women pushed and jostled and screamed, the while a small, spare figure in dark cloth coat and immaculate breeches, with smooth brown hair and pale-ascetic face, stood for a moment under the lintel of a gaping porte-cochère. He had two friends with him; handsome, enthusiastic St. Just, the right hand and the spur of the bloodthirsty monster, own kinsman to Armand St. Just the renegade, whose sister was married to a rich English millor; and Couthon, delicate, half-paralysed, wheeled about in a chair, with one foot in the grave, whose devotion to the tyrant was partly made up of ambition, and wholly of genuine admiration.

===Chinese===
兄弟晚餐会被打断了。男男女女推推搡搡、尖声喊叫，这时，一个清瘦矮小的身影在一扇洞开的车马大门（porte-cochère）的门楣下站了片刻。他身穿深色呢外套，套裤纤尘不染，棕色头发梳得平平整整，一张苦修者般苍白的面孔。他身边带着两个朋友：一个是俊美而狂热的圣茹斯特（St. Just）——那嗜血魔王的右手、策马的马刺，又是变节者阿尔芒·圣茹斯特（Armand St. Just）的嫡亲族人，而阿尔芒的姐姐，嫁给了一位富有的英国爵爷；另一个是库东（Couthon），孱弱多病、半身不遂，坐在椅中由人推着走，一只脚已经踏进了坟墓——他对暴君的赤诚，部分出于野心，而整个儿出于由衷的倾慕。

===Original===
At the uproarious cheering which greeted his appearance, Robespierre advanced into the open, whilst a sudden swift light of triumph darted from his narrow, pale eyes.

“And you still hesitate!” St. Just whispered excitedly in his ear. “Why, you hold the people absolutely in the hollow of your hand!”

“Have patience, friend!” Couthon remonstrated quietly. “Robespierre’s hour is about to strike. To hasten it now, might be courting disaster.”

===Chinese===
他在震耳欲聋的欢呼中出现，朝空地当中走去，一对细窄的苍白眼睛里，蓦地闪过一道迅疾的得意光芒。

“可您还在犹豫！”圣茹斯特在他耳边兴奋地低语。“嗳，人民分明已经稳稳攥在您的掌心里了！”

“要有耐心，朋友！”库东平静地劝道。“罗伯斯庇尔的时辰就要敲响了。此刻强求提早，只怕是自招祸殃。”

===Original===
Robespierre himself would, in the meanwhile, have been in serious danger through the exuberant welcome of his admirers. Their thoughtless crowding around his person would easily have given some lurking enemy or hotheaded, would-be martyr the chance of wielding an assassin’s knife with success, but for the presence amongst the crowd of his “*tappe-durs*”﻿—hit-hards﻿—a magnificent bodyguard composed of picked giants from the mining districts of Eastern France, who rallied around the great man, and with their weighted sticks kept the enthusiastic crowd at bay.

===Chinese===
与此同时，崇拜者们的狂热欢迎，本会把罗伯斯庇尔本人置于严重的危险之中。他们只顾不假思索地簇拥在他身边，本容易让某个潜伏的仇敌，或者哪个头脑发热、一心想当殉道烈士的家伙，得了挥匕行刺、一击得手的机会；幸而人群中有他的那些“打手”（tappe-durs）——一支威风凛凛的卫队，由法兰西东部矿区精选出的巨汉组成。他们聚拢在这位伟人四周，用坠了分量的棍棒，将狂热的人群逼开在圈外。

===Original===
He walked a few steps down the street, keeping close to the houses on his left; his two friends, St. Just and Couthon in his carrying chair, were immediately behind him, and between these three and the mob, the *tappe-durs*, striding two abreast, formed a solid phalanx.

Then, all of a sudden, the great man came to a halt, faced the crowd, and with an impressive gesture imposed silence and attention. His bodyguard cleared a space for him and he stood in the midst of them, with the light of a resin torch striking full upon his spare figure and bringing into bold relief that thin face so full of sinister expression, the cruel mouth and the coldly glittering eyes. He was looking straight across the table, on which the debris of Fraternal Suppers lay in unsavoury confusion.

===Chinese===
他沿街走了几步，紧靠左手边的房屋；他的两位朋友——圣茹斯特和坐在代步椅里的库东——紧随其后；在这三人与乱民之间，“打手”们两人一排、迈着大步，结成一道铁壁似的方阵。

忽然，这位伟人停下脚步，转过身来面向人群，以一个威严的手势压下喧哗，要众人肃静聆听。卫队为他清出一块空地，他立在卫队中央，一支松脂火把的光正正照在他清瘦的身形上，把那张阴鸷毕露的瘦脸、那张冷酷的嘴和那双寒光凛凛的眼睛，映衬得赫然分明。他的目光笔直地越过那张桌子——桌上，兄弟晚餐会的残羹剩肴，正摊成一片不堪入目的狼藉。

===Original===
On the other side of the table, Mme. de Serval with her three children sat, or rather crouched, closely huddled against one another. Joséphine was clinging to her mother, Jacques to Régine. Gone was the eagerness out of their attitude now, gone the enthusiasm that had reviled the bloodthirsty tyrant in the teeth of a threatening crowd. It seemed as if, with that terrific blow dealt by a giant hand to Bertrand who was their leader in this mad adventure, the awesome fear of death had descended upon their souls. The two young faces as well as that of Mme. de Serval appeared distorted and haggard, whilst Régine’s eyes, dilated with terror, strove to meet Robespierre’s steady gaze, which was charged with sinister mockery.

===Chinese===
桌子另一边，德·塞尔瓦尔夫人（Mme. de Serval）带着她的三个孩子坐着——不如说是蜷缩着，紧紧偎作一团。约瑟芬（Joséphine）攀着母亲，雅克（Jacques）攀着雷吉娜（Régine）。此刻他们身上那股昂扬的劲头没了，那种当着威胁的人群痛斥嗜血暴君的豪情也没了。仿佛随着一只巨掌狠狠一击、放倒了他们这场疯狂冒险的首领贝特朗（Bertrand），对死亡的森然恐惧，便已降临到他们的灵魂之上。两张年轻的脸，连同德·塞尔瓦尔夫人那张脸，都显得扭曲而憔悴；雷吉娜那双吓得睁大的眼睛，则竭力迎住罗伯斯庇尔凝定不动的注视——那目光里满含阴毒的嘲弄。

===Original===
And for one short interval of time the crowd was silent; and the everlasting stars looked down from above on the doings of men. To these trembling, terrified young creatures, suddenly possessed with youth’s passionate desire to live, with a passionate horror of death, these few seconds of tense silence must have seemed like an eternity of suffering. Then Robespierre’s thin face lighted up in a portentous smile﻿—a smile that caused those pale cheeks yonder to take on a still more ashen hue.

“And where is our eloquent orator of a while ago?” the great man asked quietly. “I heard my name, for I sat at my window looking with joy on the fraternisation of the people of France. I caught sight of the speaker, and came down to hear more clearly what he had to say. But where is he?”

===Chinese===
有短短一阵子，人群鸦雀无声；永恒的星辰在天上俯瞰着人间的作为。对这几个战战兢兢、魂飞魄散的年轻人来说——青春那股要活下去的炽烈欲望、那股对死亡的炽烈恐惧，此刻突然攫住了他们——这紧张的沉默中的几秒钟，必定像无穷无尽的受难。随后，罗伯斯庇尔那张瘦脸泛起一丝不祥的笑——这一笑，使那边那几张苍白的面颊愈发罩上一层死灰。

“方才我们那位能言善辩的演说家哪儿去了？”这位伟人不动声色地问道。“我听见了自己的名字，因为我正坐在窗前，欣然望着法兰西人民的友爱同乐。我瞧见了那位发言的人，便下来听他要说什么，听个真切。可他在哪儿？”

===Original===
His pale eyes wandered slowly along the crowd; and such was the power exercised by the extraordinary man, so great the terror that he inspired, that everyone there﻿—men, women and children, workers and vagabonds﻿—turned their eyes away, dared not meet his glance lest in it they read an accusation or a threat.

Indeed, no one dared to speak. The young rhetorician had disappeared, and everyone trembled lest they should be implicated in his escape. He had evidently got away under cover of the confusion and the noise. But his companions were still there﻿—four of them; the woman and the boy and the two girls, crouching like frightened beasts before the obvious fury, the certain vengeance of the people. The murmurs were ominous. “Death! Guillotine! Traitors!” were words easily distinguishable in the confused babbling of the sullen crowd.

===Chinese===
他那双苍白的眼睛缓缓扫过人群；这个非凡人物所施的威权如此之大，他身上激发的恐怖如此之深，以致在场每一个人——男女老少、工人和游民——都把眼睛挪开，不敢与他的目光相接，唯恐从那里面读出控诉或者威胁。

的确，谁也不敢作声。那年轻的雄辩家已经无影无踪，人人都发着抖，唯恐自己被牵连进他的逃脱里。他显然是趁着混乱与喧闹脱身的。可他的同伴们还在那儿——一共四个：那妇人、那少年和两个姑娘，像吓坏了的野兽一般蜷缩着，面对人群明摆着的狂怒、笃定要来的报复。低低的嗡嗡声凶险不祥。在阴沉的人群纷乱的嘈杂里，“死！断头台！卖国贼！”这样的字眼清晰可辨。

===Original===
Robespierre’s cruel, appraising glance rested on those four pathetic forms, so helpless, so desperate, so terrified.

“Citizens,” he said coldly, “did you not hear me ask where your eloquent companion is at this moment?”

Régine alone knew that he lay like a log under the table, close to her feet. She had seen him fall, struck by that awful blow from a brutal fist; but at the ominous query she instinctively pressed her trembling lips close together, whilst Joséphine and Jacques clung to her with the strength of despair.

===Chinese===
罗伯斯庇尔那冷酷的、估量般的目光，落在这四个可怜的人影上——那样无助，那样绝望，那样惊惶。

“公民们，”他冷冷地说，“难道没听见我问，你们那位能言善辩的伙伴此刻在哪儿？”

只有雷吉娜一个人知道，他正像段木头似的躺在桌子底下，紧挨着她的脚边。她亲眼看见他倒下，被那一记残暴的重拳打中；可听到这句凶险的问话，她本能地把自己颤抖的双唇紧紧抿住，而约瑟芬和雅克则凭着绝望中的气力，死死攀住她不放。

===Original===
“Do not parley with the rabble, citizen,” St. Just whispered eagerly. “This is a grand moment for you. Let the people of their own accord condemn those who dared to defame you.”

And even Couthon, the prudent, added sententiously:

“Such an opportunity may never occur again.”

===Chinese===
“别跟这帮贱民多费口舌，公民，”圣茹斯特急切地低声道。“这是您的辉煌时刻。让人民自发地惩处那些胆敢诽谤您的人吧。”

连素来谨慎的库东，也拿腔拿调地补了一句：

“这样的机会，往后再也不会有了。”

===Original===
The people, in truth, were over-ready to take vengeance into their own hands.

“*Á la lanterne, les aristos!*”

Gaunt, bedraggled forms leaned across the table, shook begrimed fists in the direction of the four crouching figures. With the blind instinct of trapped beasts, they retreated into the shadows step by step, as those threatening fists appeared to draw closer, clutching at the nearest table and dragging it with them, in an altogether futile attempt at a barricade.

===Chinese===
说实在的，人民早就急不可耐，要亲手报仇。

“把贵族吊上灯杆！（Á la lanterne, les aristos!）”

形容枯槁、蓬头垢面的人影，隔着桌子探过身来，朝那四个蜷缩的身影挥舞污黑的拳头。那四个人凭着落网野兽般的盲目本能，一步步退进暗影里，眼见那些威吓的拳头步步逼近，便抓住离得最近的一张桌子，拖着它一起退——全然徒劳地想拿它当一道街垒。

===Original===
“Holy Mother of God, protect us!” murmured Mme. de Serval from time to time.

Behind them there was nothing but the rows of houses, no means of escape even if their trembling knees had not refused them service; whilst vaguely, through their terror, they were conscious of the proximity of that awful asthmatic creature with the wheezy cough and the hideous, toothless mouth. At times he seemed so close that they shut their eyes, almost feeling his grimy hands around their throat, his huge, hairy arms dragging them down to death.

===Chinese===
“上帝的圣母啊，保佑我们！”德·塞尔瓦尔夫人一遍又一遍地低声祷告。

他们身后只有一排排房屋，就算那两条打颤的腿还肯听使唤，也无路可逃；而在恐惧的迷雾中，他们还隐隐意识到，那个患哮喘的可怖家伙就在近旁——呼哧呼哧地咳着，咧着一张没有牙齿的丑嘴。有时他仿佛近在咫尺，吓得他们闭上眼睛，几乎当真觉出那双污黑的手已经掐上了自己的喉咙，那两条粗大多毛的胳膊正把他们往死里拖。

===Original===
It all happened in the space of a very few minutes, far fewer even than it would take completely to visualise the picture. Robespierre, like an avenging wraith, theatrical yet impassive, standing in the light of a huge resin torch, which threw alternate lights and shadows, grotesque and weird, upon his meagre figure, now elongating the thin, straight nose, now widening the narrow mouth, misshaping the figure till it appeared like some fantastic ghoul-form from the nether world. Behind him, his two friends were lost in the gloom, as were now Mme. de Serval and her children. They were ensconced against a heavy porte-cochère, a rickety table alone standing between them and the mob, who were ready to drag them to the nearest lantern and immolate them before the eyes of their outraged idol.

===Chinese===
这一切前后不过寥寥几分钟，比把这幅图景在心里完整想上一遍还要少。罗伯斯庇尔像个复仇的幽灵，做戏似的，却又漠然无动，立在巨大松脂火把的光里；火光把明明灭灭、光怪陆离的影子投在他单薄的身形上，一时拉长那又细又直的鼻梁，一时撑宽那窄窄的嘴，把整个人形扭曲得仿佛冥府里来的什么离奇食尸鬼。他身后，他的两位朋友已隐入黑暗，德·塞尔瓦尔夫人和她的孩子们此刻也一样。他们背抵着一扇厚重的车马大门，与暴民之间只隔一张摇摇欲坠的桌子；那群暴民已准备把他们拖到最近的灯杆下，在他们被冒犯的偶像眼前，把他们当祭品宰掉。

===Original===
“Leave the traitors alone!” Robespierre commanded. “Justice will deal with them as they deserve.”

“*Á la lanterne!*” the people﻿—more especially the women﻿—demanded insistently.

Robespierre turned to one of his “*tappe-durs*.”

“Take the aristos to the nearest Commissariat,” he said. “I’ll have no bloodshed to mar our Fraternal Supper.”

===Chinese===
“别去动那些叛徒！”罗伯斯庇尔命令道。“正义自会给他们应得的处置。”

“吊上灯杆！（Á la lanterne!）”人们——尤其是女人们——执拗地叫嚷着。

罗伯斯庇尔转向他手下的一名“打手”。

“把这几个贵族押到最近的警务分署（Commissariat）去，”他说。“我不要见血，坏了我们的兄弟晚餐会。”

===Original===
“The Commissariat, forsooth!” a raucous voice positively bellowed. “Who is going to stand between us and our vengeance? Robespierre has been outraged by this rabble. Let them perish in sight of all!”

===Chinese===
“警务分署？亏你说得出口！”一个嘶哑的嗓门简直是吼出来的。“谁来拦着我们报仇？这帮贱民冒犯了罗伯斯庇尔。让他们死在众人眼前！”

===Original===
How it all happened after that, none who were there could in truth have told you. The darkness, the flickering lights, the glow of the braziers, which made the inky blackness around more pronounced, made everything indistinguishable to ordinary human sight. Certain it is that citizen Rateau﻿—who had constituted himself the spokesman of the mob﻿—was at one time seen towering behind the four unfortunates, with his huge arms stretched out, his head thrown back, his mouth wide open, screaming abuse and vituperation, demanding the people’s right to take the law into its own sovereign hands.

At that moment the light of the nearest resin torch threw his hulking person into bold relief against a heavy porte-cochère which was immediately behind him. The mob acclaimed him, cheered him to the echoes, agreed with him that summary justice in such a case was lone satisfying. The next instant a puff of wind blew the flame of the torch in a contrary direction, and darkness suddenly enveloped the ranting colossus and the cowering prey all ready to his hand.

===Chinese===
这之后事情究竟是怎生经过的，在场的人实在谁也说不清。黑暗、摇曳的光、火盆的红焰——红焰把四周墨似的黑衬得愈发浓重——使寻常人的眼睛什么也分辨不清。可以确定的是：公民拉托（Rateau）——他自封为暴民的代言人——曾一度被人瞥见，高塔般耸在那四个倒霉人身后，巨大的双臂张开，头向后仰，嘴巴大张，嘶声叫骂不休，声言人民有权以主权者的身份，亲手执掌法律。

就在那一刻，最近一支松脂火把的光，把他那庞大的身躯，赫然映在他身后一扇厚重的车马大门上。暴民为他喝彩，欢声雷动、久久回荡，都赞同他的话：这样的案子，唯有当场快意处决才解恨。可下一瞬，一阵风把火把的火焰吹向另一边，黑暗陡然罩住了那咆哮的巨人，和他唾手可得、瑟缩成一团的猎物。

===Original===
“Rateau!” shouted someone.

“Hey, there! citizen Rateau! Where art thou?” came soon from every side.

No answer came from the spot where Rateau had last been seen, and it seemed as if just then a strong current of air had slammed a heavy door to somewhere in the gloom. Citizen Rateau had disappeared, and the four traitors along with him.

It took a few seconds of valuable time ere the mob suspected that it was being robbed of its prey. Then a huge upheaval occurred, a motion of the human mass densely packed in the Rue St. Honoré, that was not unlike the rush of water through a narrow gorge.

“Rateau!” People were yelling the name from end to end of the street.

===Chinese===
“拉托！”有人喊道。

“喂，喂！拉托公民！你在哪儿呀？”转眼间，四面八方都响起了这声呼喊。

拉托最后露面的地方没有半点回应，而黑暗深处，仿佛恰在那时有一股强风，把哪扇沉重的门砰地关上了。拉托公民不见了，那四个叛徒也随他一同消失。

过了宝贵的几秒钟，暴民才疑心到手的猎物被人劫走了。随即掀起一场大骚动——挤挤挨挨塞满圣奥诺雷街（Rue St. Honoré）的人潮猛地一涌，很像洪水灌过狭窄的峡谷。

“拉托！”人们从街头到街尾，扯着嗓子喊这个名字。

### II / II

===Original===
Superstition, which was rampant in these days of carnage and of crime, had possession of many a craven soul. Rateau had vanished. It seemed as if the Evil One, whose name had been so freely invoked during the course of the Fraternal Supper, had in very truth spirited Rateau away.

On the top of the tumult came a silence as complete as that of a graveyard at midnight. The “*tappe-durs*,” who at their chief’s command had been forging their way through the crowd, in order to reach the traitors, ceased their hoarse calls of “Make way there, in the name of the Convention!” whilst St. Just, who still stood close to his friend, literally saw the cry stifled on Robespierre’s lips.

===Chinese===
在这杀戮与罪孽的年月里甚嚣尘上的迷信，攫住了不知多少怯懦的灵魂。拉托消失了。那个在兄弟晚餐会席间被人们随口咒来骂去的恶魔，仿佛当真把拉托摄走了。

大骚动过后，继之而来的是一片死寂，静得如同午夜的坟场。那些“打手”奉首领之命，正在人群中硬开一条路、要去抓那几个叛徒，这时也住了口，不再嘶声喊“以国民公会的名义，让开！”；而仍旧站在朋友身边的圣茹斯特，简直亲眼看见那声呼叱，活生生憋死在了罗伯斯庇尔的唇边。

===Original===
Robespierre himself had not altogether realised what had happened. In his innermost heart he had already yielded to his friends’ suggestion, and was willing to let mob-law run its course. As St. Just had said: what a triumph for himself if his detractors were lynched by the mob! When Rateau towered above the four unfortunates, hurling vituperation above their heads, the tyrant smiled, well satisfied; and when the giant thus incontinently vanished, Robespierre for a moment or two remained complacent and content.

===Chinese===
罗伯斯庇尔自己也还没完全弄明白出了什么事。在他内心深处，他早已依了朋友们的怂恿，乐得任暴民私刑自行其是。正像圣茹斯特说的：倘若诋毁他的人被暴民私刑处死，对他本人是何等的胜利！当拉托高塔般耸立在四个倒霉人头顶、劈头盖脑地叫骂时，暴君笑了，心满意足；及至那巨人就这么没来头地消失，罗伯斯庇尔有那么一两分钟工夫，依然自得而惬意。

===Original===
Then the whole crowd oscillated in the direction of the mysterious porte-cochère. Those who were in the front ranks threw themselves against the heavy panels, whilst those in the rear pushed with all their might. But the porte-cochères of old Paris are heavily constructed. Woodwork that had resisted the passage of centuries withheld the onslaught of a pack of half-starved caitiffs. But only for awhile.

The mob, fearing that it was getting foiled, broke into a howl of execration, and Robespierre, his face more drawn and grey than before, turned to his companions, trying to read their thoughts.

“If it should be﻿—” St. Just murmured, yet dared not put his surmise into words.

===Chinese===
于是整个人潮朝那扇神秘的车马大门荡了过去。前排的人扑在厚重的门板上，后排的人则使出全身力气往前推。然而老巴黎的车马大门构造厚重。历经数百年沧桑的木作，硬是顶住了一伙半饥不饱的恶徒的冲击。不过也只顶得了一时。

暴民眼看要扑空，爆出一阵咒骂的嚎叫；罗伯斯庇尔的脸比先前更加紧绷、更加灰败，他转向同伴，想从他们脸上读出心思来。

“万一要是——”圣茹斯特嗫嚅道，却终究不敢把自己的猜测说出口。

===Original===
Nor had he time to do so, or Robespierre the leisure to visualise his own fears. Already the massive oak panels were yielding to persistent efforts. The mighty woodwork began to crack under the pressure of this living battering ram; when suddenly the howls of those who were in the rear turned to a wild cry of delight. Those who were pushing against the porte-cochère paused in their task. All necks were suddenly craned upwards. The weird lights of torches and the glow of braziers glinted on guant necks and upturned chins, turned heads and faces into phantasmagoric, unearthly shapes.

===Chinese===
他也来不及说出口，罗伯斯庇尔也来不及把自己心里的恐惧描摹成形。厚实的橡木门板已经在不停的推撞下渐渐吃不消了。巨大的木作开始在这根血肉攻城槌的重压下迸出裂纹；就在这时，后排人群的嚎叫忽然转成一阵狂喜的欢呼。推门的人停下了手里的活计。所有脖颈猛然一齐仰起。火把诡谲的光与火盆的红焰，在干瘦的颈项和仰起的下巴上闪烁明灭，把一颗颗脑袋、一张张面孔，照成了幻影幢幢、不似人间的形状。

===Original===
Robespierre and his two companions instinctively looked up too. There, some few métres lower down the street, on the third-floor balcony of a neighbouring house, the figure of Rateau had just appeared. The window immediately behind him was wide open and the room beyond was flooded with light, so that his huge person appeared distinctly silhouetted﻿—a black and gargantuan mass﻿—against the vivid and glowing background. His head was bare, his lank hair fluttered in the breeze, his huge chest was bare and his ragged shirt hung in tatters from his brawny arms. Flung across his left shoulder, he held an inanimate female form, whilst with his right hand he dragged another through the open window in his wake. Just below him, a huge brazier was shedding its crimson glow.

===Chinese===
罗伯斯庇尔和他的两个同伴，也不由自主地抬起头来。就在沿街往下几米远，隔壁一座房子的三楼阳台上，拉托的身影刚刚出现。他身后的窗子敞开着，窗后的房间灯火通明，把他庞大的身躯清晰地衬成一道黑影——一团黑色的、巨人般的庞然大物——映在那明亮灼热的背景上。他光着头，稀疏的头发在微风里飘动，宽阔的胸膛袒露着，破烂的衬衫成条地挂在粗壮的臂膀上。他左肩上横扛着一个不省人事的女人，右手又从敞开的窗子里拖出另一个，拽在自己身后。就在他脚下，一只巨大的火盆正吐着猩红的光。

===Original===
The sight of him﻿—gaunt, weird, a veritable tower of protean revenge﻿—paralysed the most ebullient, silenced every clamour. For the space of two seconds only did he stand there, in full view of the crowd, in full view of the almighty tyrant whose defamation he had sworn to avenge. Then he cried in stentorian tones:

“Thus perish all conspirators against the liberty of the people, all traitors to its cause, by the hands of the people and for the glory of their chosen!”

===Chinese===
他这副模样——瘦骨嶙峋、诡奇骇人，当真是一座千形万变的复仇之塔——让最沸腾的人也僵住了，让一切喧嚷都哑了。他只在原处站了两秒钟，任全场观看，也任那位全能的暴君观看——他曾立誓要为他所受的诽谤复仇。随后，他扯开洪亮的嗓门高呼：

“凡阴谋反对人民自由者、背叛人民大业者，都当这样死去——死于人民之手，为他们所选之人的荣光！”

===Original===
And, with a mighty twist of his huge body, he picked up the inanimate form that lay lifeless at his feet. For a moment he held the two in his arms, high above the iron railing of the balcony; for a moment those two lifeless, shapeless forms hung in the darkness in midair, whilst an entire crowd of fanatics held their breath and waited, awed and palpitating, only to break out into frantic cheering as the giant hurled the two lifeless bodies down, straight into the glowing brazier.

“Two more to follow!” he shouted lustily.

===Chinese===
说罢，他魁伟的身躯猛地一拧，抱起脚边那具没有声息的躯体。有一瞬，他把两具身躯一同擎在臂弯里，高高举过阳台的铁栏；有一瞬，那两个没有生命、不成形状的身影，悬在半空的黑暗里，而整群狂徒屏住呼吸等着，又敬又惧、心头突突乱跳——直到巨人把那两具死气沉沉的身子掷下去，径直扔进烧得通红的火盆，他们才轰然爆出狂热的欢呼。

“还有两个，随后就到！”他扯着嗓子中气十足地喊道。

===Original===
There was pushing and jostling and cheering. Women screamed, men blasphemed and children cried. Shouts of “*Vive* Rateau!” mingled with those of “*Vive* Robespierre!” a circle was formed, hands holding hands, and a wild saraband danced around the glowing brazier. And this mad orgy of enthusiasm lasted for full three minutes, until the foremost among those who, awestruck and horrified, had approached the brazier in order to see the final agony of the abominable traitor, burst out with a prolonged “Malediction!”

===Chinese===
人群推挤着、欢腾着。女人尖叫，男人咒天骂神，孩子哭喊。“拉托万岁！（Vive Rateau!）”的呼声与“罗伯斯庇尔万岁！（Vive Robespierre!）”的呼声混作一处；人们手拉手围成一圈，围着烧红的火盆，疯狂地跳起萨拉班德舞（saraband）。这场狂热的疯魔欢宴足足持续了三分钟，直到那几个又惊又惧、凑近火盆想看一看可憎叛徒临终惨状的人里，走在最前面的几位，爆出一声拖得长长的“该死！（Malediction!）”

===Original===
Beyond that exclamation, they were speechless﻿—pointed with trembling hands at the shapeless bundles on which the dull fire of the braziers had not yet obtained a purchase.

The bundles were shapeless indeed. Rags hastily tied together to represent human forms; but rags only! No female traitors, no aristos beneath! The people had been fooled, hideously fooled by a traitor all the more execrable, as he had seemed one of themselves.

“Malediction! Death to the traitor!”

===Chinese===
除了这一声惊呼，他们说不出话来——只用颤抖的手，指着火盆上那几团不成形状的包袱，火盆暗红的火，还没能烧上它们的身。

那几团东西确实不成形状。那是些草草捆起来、扎成人形的破布；可只有破布！底下没有女叛徒，没有什么贵族！人民被愚弄了，被骇人听闻地愚弄了——愚弄他们的是一个叛徒，而这叛徒正因看上去是自己人，才愈发可恨。

“该死！处死那叛徒！”

===Original===
Aye, death indeed! The giant, whoever he might be, would have to bear a charmed life if he were to escape the maddened fury of a foiled populace.

“Rateau!” they shouted hoarsely.

They looked up to that third-floor balcony which had so fascinated them awhile ago. But now the window was shut and no light from within chased the gloom that hung over the houses around.

“Rateau!” the people shouted.

===Chinese===
是啊，真得处死才行！那巨人，不管他究竟是谁，若想从被挫败的民众那疯狂的暴怒底下脱身，除非他真有一副刀枪不入的天佑之躯。

“拉托！”他们声音嘶哑地喊。

他们仰头望向方才令他们看得出神的那座三楼阳台。可现在窗子已经关上，屋里没有一点光亮，来驱散笼罩在四周房屋上的昏暗。

“拉托！”人们喊。

===Original===
But Rateau had disappeared. It all seemed like a dream, a nightmare. Had Rateau really existed, or was he a wraith, sent to tease and to scare those honest patriots who were out for liberty and for fraternity? Many there were who would have liked to hold on to that theory﻿—men and women whose souls, warped and starved by the excesses and the miseries of the past five years, clung to any superstition, any so-called supernatural revelations, that failed to replace the old religion that had been banished from their hearts.

===Chinese===
可拉托已经无影无踪。这一切恍如一场梦、一场噩梦。拉托真的存在过吗？还是他本是一缕幽魂，专来捉弄、惊吓那些为自由、为博爱奔走出力的老实爱国者？不少人倒真愿意抱住这个说法不放——这些男男女女，灵魂早被过去五年的恣纵与苦难扭曲、饿瘪了，便死死抓住一切迷信、一切所谓超自然的启示不放，尽管这些东西终究填补不了那被从他们心中放逐掉的旧宗教。

===Original===
But in this case not even superstition could be allowed free play. Rateau had vanished, it is true. The house from whence he had thus mocked and flouted the people was searched through and through by a mob who found nothing but bare boards and naked walls, empty rooms and disused cupboards on which to wreak its fury.

But down there, lying on the top of the brazier, were those two bundles of rags slowly being consumed by the smouldering embers, silent proofs of the existence of that hulking creature whose size and power had, with that swiftness peculiar to human conceptions, already become legendary.

===Chinese===
只是这一回，连迷信也由不得它自便了。拉托确实消失了。他就在那座房子里，如此这般地嘲弄、奚落了人民；暴民把房子里里外外、上上下下搜了个遍，却只找到光秃的地板、赤裸的墙壁、空空的房间和废弃不用的柜橱，连个泄愤的地方都没有。

可就在下面，火盆顶上还摊着那两团破布，正被焖燃的余烬慢慢烧蚀——它们无声地证明着那个庞然大物确曾存在；而他的身量与神力，已凭着人类想象力特有的飞快速度，化作了传奇。

===Original===
And in a third-floor room, a lamp that had recently been extinguished, a coil of rope, more rags, male and female clothes, a pair of boots, a battered hat, were mute witnesses to the swift passage of the mysterious giant with the wheezy cough﻿—the trickster who had fooled a crowd and thrown the great Robespierre himself into ridicule.

===Chinese===
而在三楼的一个房间里，一盏刚熄不久的灯、一卷绳索、更多的破布、一套男装和一套女装、一双靴子、一顶压瘪的帽子，都在无言地作证：那个咳嗽呼哧呼哧的神秘巨人，曾从此飞快经过——就是这个骗子，愚弄了一群人，连伟大的罗伯斯庇尔本人，也被他变成了笑柄。

## VII. Two Interludes / VII. 两段插曲

### I / I

===Original===
Two hours later the Rue St. Honoré had resumed its habitual graveyard-like stillness. The stillness had to come at last. Men in their wildest passions, in their most ebullient moods, must calm down sooner or later, if only temporarily. Blood aglow with enthusiasm, or rage, or idolatry, cannot retain its fever-pitch uninterruptedly for long. And so silence of that turbulent scene of awhile ago.

Here, as in other quarters of Paris, the fraternal suppers had come to an end; and perspiring matrons, dragging weary children at their skirts, wended their way homewards, whilst their men went to consummate the evening’s entertainment at one of the numerous clubs or cabarets where the marvellous doings in the Rue St. Honoré could be comfortably lived over again or retailed to those, less fortunate, who had not been there to see.
===Chinese===
两个钟头以后，圣奥诺雷街（Rue St. Honoré）又恢复了它惯常的、坟场一般的死寂。这份寂静，终究是迟早要来的。人在最狂热的激情之中、最沸腾的兴头之上，迟早总得平息下来，哪怕只是暂时的。因狂热、因义愤、因偶像崇拜而发烫的血，是不能没完没了地一直高烧不退的。于是，方才那一片沸反盈天的场面，就此哑然无声。

这里同巴黎别的街区一样，兄弟晚餐会已经散了场。汗涔涔的主妇们拖着黏在裙边的困乏孩子，一路回家去了；她们的男人则到为数众多的俱乐部或酒馆中的一家，去给这个晚上的余兴做个收梢——在那儿，圣奥诺雷街上那番惊天动地的作为，尽可舒舒服服地重温一遍，或者转述给那些无此眼福、运气稍差的人们听。

===Original===
In the early morning the “*nettoyeurs publiques*” would be coming along, to clear away the debris of the festivities and to gather up the tables and benches which were the property of the several Municipal sections, and put them away for the next occasion.

But these “*nettoyeurs*” were not here yet. They, too, were spending an hour or two in the nearest cabarets, discussing the startling events that had rendered notorious one corner of the Rue St. Honoré.
===Chinese===
一早，那些“公共清洁工”（nettoyeurs publiques）就要来了，来清扫宴席的残渣，收拢各张桌子板凳——那是各市政分区的公物——归置妥当，留待下回的场合再使唤。

可这些“清洁工”们眼下还没有到。他们也正在就近的酒馆里消磨一两个钟头，议论着那桩惊人的变故——正是它，叫圣奥诺雷街的一角名声大噪。

===Original===
And so the streets were entirely deserted, save here and there for the swift passage of a furtive form, hugging the walls, with hands in pockets and a crimson cap pulled over the eyes, anxious only to escape the vigilance of the night-watchman, swift of foot and silent of tread; and anon, in the Rue St. Honoré itself, when even these nightbirds had ceased to flutter, the noiseless movement of a dark and mysterious form that stirred cautiously upon the greasy cobblestones. More silent, more furtive than any hunted beast creeping out of its lair, this mysterious form emerged from under one of the tables that was standing nearly opposite the house where Robespierre lived and close to the one where the superhuman colossus had wrought his magic trick.
===Chinese===
于是街道上彻底空了，只偶尔有一条鬼祟的人影贴墙飞快掠过：双手插在兜里，红便帽压到眼上，一门心思只求躲过守夜人的警觉，脚程飞快，落脚无声；再过一阵，待连这些夜鸟儿也扑腾够了、歇了翅，圣奥诺雷街上便只剩一团黑黢黢的神秘影子，在油垢的鹅卵石路面上小心翼翼地蠕动。这团影子无声无息、鬼鬼祟祟，赛过任何一头蹑出巢穴的受猎困兽——它从一张桌子底下钻了出来。那桌子立的地方，差不多正对着罗伯斯庇尔居住的宅子，又紧挨着那位超凡巨灵变他那套魔法戏法的另一所屋子。

===Original===
It was Bertrand Moncrif. No longer a fiery Desmosthenes now, but a hunted, terror-filled human creature, whom a stunning blow from a giant fist had rendered senseless, even whilst it saved him from the consequences of his own folly. His senses still reeling, his limbs cramped and aching, he had lain stark and still under the table just where he had fallen, not sufficiently conscious to realise what was happening beyond his very limited range of vision or to marvel what was the ultimate fate of his companions.

His only instinct throughout this comatose condition was the blind one of self-preservation. Feeling rather than hearing the tumult around him, he had gathered his limbs close together, lain as still as a mouse, crouching within himself in the shelter of the table above. It was only when the silence around had lasted an eternity of time that he ventured out of his hiding-place. With utmost caution, hardly daring to breathe, he crept on hands and knees and looked about him, up and down the street. There was no one about. The night fortunately was moonless and dark; nature had put herself on the side of those who wished to pass unperceived.
===Chinese===
那是贝特朗·蒙克里夫（Bertrand Moncrif）。此刻他不再是那个烈焰般慷慨的德摩斯梯尼，只是一头遭到追捕、满心惊惶的可怜活物——巨人的一记拳头打得他失了知觉，却也正因这一拳，把他从自己荒唐行径的后果里救了出来。他神志犹自天旋地转，四肢麻木酸疼，就这么直挺挺、一动不动地躺在桌底倒下的老地方，神思不够清明，既辨不清自己那一眼望不出去的视野之外正发生着什么，也顾不上诧异同伴们最终落得了什么下场。

在这半昏迷的状态里，支配他的只有一条盲目的本能：保全性命。周遭的喧腾，他与其说是听见，不如说是觉出的；他把四肢紧紧拢作一团，一动不动躺在那里，像只耗子，缩着身子，藏在上面临近那张桌子的荫蔽底下。直到四下的寂静已经熬满了一个永恒那么久，他才敢从藏身之处探出来。他万分小心，大气儿也不敢出，手脚并用地爬了一程，四下里张望，把那条街从这头到那头打量了一遍。附近一个人影也没有。所幸这夜无月而漆黑；造化亲自站到了那些想悄然而过、不惹人眼的人一边。

===Original===
Bertrand struggled to his feet, smothering a cry of pain. His head ached furiously, his knees shook under him; but he managed to crawl as far as the nearest house, and rested for awhile against its wall. The fresh air did him good. The April breeze blew across his burning forehead.

For a few minutes he remained thus, quite still, his eyes gradually regaining their power of vision. He recollected where he was and all that had happened. An icy shiver ran down his spine, for he also remembered Régine and Mme. de Serval and the two children. But he was still too much dazed, really only half conscious, to do more than vaguely marvel what had become of them.
===Chinese===
贝特朗挣扎着站起身，死死捂住一声痛呼。他头疼得凶，两条膝盖在腿上直打颤；可他还是撑着挪到了最近一处屋子跟前，倚着墙歇了一会儿。清新的空气让他好受了些。四月的夜风，拂过他滚烫的额头。

有好几分钟，他就这样一动不动，两眼渐渐恢复了视物的气力。他记起了自己身在何处，记起了发生过的这一切。一道冰冷的寒颤顺着脊梁骨溜下去，因为他也想起了雷吉娜（Régine），想起了德·塞尔瓦尔夫人（Mme. de Serval），还有那两个孩子。可他仍旧昏沉得厉害，实在只算半个清醒人，除了恍恍惚惚地纳闷他们不知落得了什么下场，旁的一概无从做起。

===Original===
He ventured to look fearfully up and down the street. Tables scattered pell-mell, the unsavoury remnants of fraternal suppers, a couple of smouldering braziers, collectively met his gaze. And at one point, sprawling across a table, with head lost between outstretched arms, a figure, apparently asleep, perhaps dead.

Bertrand, now nothing but a bundle of nerves, could hardly suppress a cry of terror. It seemed to him as if his life depended on whether that sprawling figure was alive or dead. But he dared not approach in order to make sure. For awhile he waited, sinking more and more deeply into the shadows, watching that motionless form on which his life depended.
===Chinese===
他壮着胆子，惶惶地朝街两头望去。横七竖八散着的桌子，兄弟晚餐会不堪入目的残渣余滓，两只兀自阴燃着的火盆，一齐撞进他眼里。而在一处，一张桌面上摊着一具人形，脑袋埋进张开的双臂之间——瞧着像是睡熟了，兴许是死了。

贝特朗这会儿整个人是一捆绷到极限的神经，险些压不住一声惊叫。在他觉来，自己这条性命，就吊在那摊着的人形是活是死上头。可他不敢凑近去看个真切。他等了一阵，把身子一寸一寸愈发深地隐进黑影里，盯住那具一动不动的形体——他的性命，正悬在它上头。

===Original===
The figure did not move, and gradually Bertrand nerved himself up to confidence and then to action. He buried his head in the folds of his coat-collar and his hands in the pockets of his breeches, and with silence, stealthy footsteps he started to make his way down the street. At first he looked back once or twice at the immobile figure sprawling across the table. It had not moved, still appeared as if it might be dead. Then Bertrand took to his heels and, no longer looking either behind him or to the right or left, with elbows pressed close to his side, he started to run in the direction of the Tuileries.

A minute later, the motionless figure came back to life, rose quickly and with swift, noiseless tread, started to run in the same direction.
===Chinese===
那人影一动不动，贝特朗这才渐渐壮起胆来，先有了胆气，继而起了行动。他把脑袋埋进外套领子的褶子里，两手插进套裤的裤兜，蹑着无声的脚步，悄悄沿街走去。起先他还回头望过一两眼那摊在桌上的僵直人影。它没有动，瞧着仍像是个死人。于是贝特朗撒腿便跑，再不住后看，也不左右张望，两条胳膊肘紧紧贴住身侧，朝杜伊勒里宫（Tuileries）的方向奔去了。

一分钟后，那具不动的形体还了魂，霍然起身，迈出迅疾无声的脚步，朝同一个方向奔去。

### II / II

===Original===
In the cabarets throughout the city, the chief topic of conversation was the mysterious events of the Rue St. Honoré. Those who had seen it all had marvellous tales to tell of the hero of the adventure.

“The man was eight or else nine feet high; his arms reached right across the street from house to house. Flames spurted out of his mouth when he coughed. He had horns on his head; cloven feet; a forked tail!”
===Chinese===
全城上下的酒馆里，头一号的谈资，就是圣奥诺雷街那桩神秘的变故。凡是亲眼见过全本的，讲起这场冒险的主角，个个都有一车神乎其神的故事。

“那人身量足有八英尺高，再不然就是九英尺；两条胳膊一伸，从街这边房子的墙面直抵到街那边的墙面。咳嗽一声，嘴里就往外蹿火苗。头上生角；脚是分瓣的蹄子；尾巴还带着分叉！”

===Original===
These were but a few of the asseverations which rendered the person of the fictitious citizen Rateau a legendary one in the eyes of those who had witnessed his amazing prowess. Those who had not been thus favoured listened wide-eyed and open-mouthed.
