“I would you had, citoyenne,” he retorted. “You would have realised that to refuse your help to unmask an abominable spy after such an episode is tantamount to treason.”

Her gaiety dropped from her like a mantle. In a moment she was serious, puzzled. A frown appeared between her brows. Her dark eyes flashed, rapidly inquiring, suspicious, fearful, upon Robespierre.

===Chinese===
“那位来无影去无踪的猩红石榴红，”她欢快地插嘴道。“扮成一个气喘吁吁的小伙计（gossoon），谁都认不出来——除了罗伯斯庇尔公民。啊，真愿我当时在场！”

“我倒也但愿您在场，女公民，”他反唇相讥。“您就会明白，出了这样一场事之后还拒绝协助揭穿一个可恶的奸细，无异于叛国。”

她的欢快像一件斗篷，从身上滑落了。转眼间，她变得严肃、困惑起来。眉间蹙起一道细纹。她那双黑眼睛飞快地向罗伯斯庇尔闪出探询的、猜疑的、惶惧的光。

===Original===
“To refuse my help?” she asked slowly. “My help in unmasking a spy? I do not understand.”

She looked from one man to the other. Chauvelin was the only one who would not meet her gaze. No, not the only one. Tallien, too, appeared absorbed in contemplating his finger nails.

“Citizen Tallien,” she queried harshly. “What does this mean?”

===Chinese===
“拒绝我的帮助？”她缓缓地问。“要我的帮助去揭穿一个奸细？我不明白。”

她把目光从一个人身上移到另一个人身上。肖夫兰是唯一不肯同她对视的人。不，还不是唯一的一个。塔利安呢，也仿佛正专心致志地端详着自己的手指甲。

“塔利安公民，”她厉声质问，“这是什么意思？”

===Original===
“It means just what I said,” Robespierre intervened coldly. “That abominable English spy has fooled us all. You said yourself that ’tis a woman’s wit that will bring that elusive adventurer to his knees one day. Why not yours?”

Theresia gave no immediate reply. She was meditating. Here, then, was this other means to her hand, whereby she was to propitiate the man-eating tiger, turn his snarl into a purr, obtain immunity for herself and her future lord, but what a prospect!

“I fear me, citizen Robespierre,” she said after awhile, “that you overestimate the keenness of my wits.”

===Chinese===
“意思就是我方才说的话，”罗伯斯庇尔冷冷地插进来。“那个可恶的英国奸细把我们全都耍了。您自己说过，终有一天，是女人的心智会叫那位来去无踪的冒险家跪下臣服。为什么就不能是您的？”

特蕾莎没有立刻作答。她在沉思。瞧，这另一条门路就这样送到了她手边：顺着它，她可以安抚那头吃人的老虎，把他喉咙里的咆哮化作呼噜，为她自己和她未来的夫君讨得一纸赦免——只是，这是何等的前景啊！

“我怕您是把我的心智估量得太锋利了，罗伯斯庇尔公民，”她过了一会儿才说。

===Original===
“Impossible!” he retorted drily.

And St. Just, ever the echo of his friend’s unspoken words, added with a great show of gallantry:

===Chinese===
“不可能！”他干巴巴地回了一句。

而圣茹斯特，永远是他朋友没说出口的话的回声，煞有介事地献着殷勤补上一句：

===Original===
“The citoyenne Cabarrus, even from her prison in Bordeaux, succeeded in snaring our friend Tallien, and making him the slave of her beauty.”

“Then why not the Scarlet Pimpernel?” was Couthon’s simple conclusion.

“The Scarlet Pimpernel!” Theresia exclaimed with a shrug of her handsome shoulders. “The Scarlet Pimpernel, forsooth! Why, meseems that no one knows who he is! Just now you all affirmed that he was a coal-heaver named Rateau. I cannot make love to a coal-heaver, can I?”

===Chinese===
“卡巴吕斯女公民当年身在波尔多（Bordeaux）的监狱里，尚且能诱住我们的朋友塔利安，叫他做了她美貌的奴隶。”

“那为什么就不能收服猩红石榴红呢？”库东得出他直截了当的结论。

“猩红石榴红！”特蕾莎喊着，耸了耸她那好看的肩膀。“猩红石榴红，好一个猩红石榴红！嗐，依我看，根本没有人知道他是谁！方才你们大家还一口咬定，他是个叫拉托的运煤工来着。总不能叫我对一个运煤工谈情说爱吧，啊？”

===Original===
“Citizen Chauvelin knows who the Scarlet Pimpernel is,” Couthon went on deliberately. “He will put you on the right track. All that we want is that he should be at your feet. It is so easy for the citoyenne Cabarrus to accomplish that.”

“But if you know who he is,” she urged, “why do you need my help?”

===Chinese===
“肖夫兰公民就知道猩红石榴红是谁，”库东不紧不慢地接着说。“他会替您指对路子。我们要的，不过是叫他拜倒在您的脚下。这一层，卡巴吕斯女公民办起来还不容易。”

“可你们既然知道他是谁，”她追问道，“又何必要我的帮助？”

===Original===
“Because,” St. Just replied, “the moment that he lands in France he sheds his identity, as a man would a coat. Here, there, everywhere﻿—he is more elusive than a ghost, for a ghost is always the same, whilst the Scarlet Pimpernel is never twice alike. A coal-heaver one day; a prince of dandies the next. He has lodgings in every quarter of Paris and quits them at a moment’s notice. He has confederates everywhere: concierges, cabaret-keepers, soldiers, vagabonds. He has been a public letter-writer, a sergeant of the National Guard, a rogue, a thief! ’Tis only in England that he is always the same, and citizen Chauvelin can identify him there. ’Tis there that you can see him, citoyenne, there that you can spread your nets for him; from thence that you can lure him to France in your train, like you lured citizen Tallien to obey your every whim in Bordeaux. Once a man hath fallen a victim to the charms of beautiful Theresia Cabarrus,” added the young demagogue gallantly, “she need only to beckon and he will follow, as does citizen Tallien, as did Bertrand Moncrif, as do so many others. Bring the Scarlet Pimpernel to your feet, here in Paris, citoyenne, and we will do the rest.”

===Chinese===
“因为，”圣茹斯特答道，“他一踏上法兰西的土地，就把自己的身份抖落个干净，就像男人脱掉一件外套。这儿，那儿，到处——他比鬼魂还难捉摸，因为鬼魂好歹总是同一副模样，猩红石榴红却从来没有两回相像。今天是个运煤工，明天就是一位纨绔子弟中的王子。他在巴黎每个区都有落脚的寓所，说走就走，片刻不留。他到处有同党：门房、酒馆老板、士兵、游民。他当过街头代书先生，当过国民自卫军的军士，当过骗子、小偷！只有在英格兰，他才是始终如一的那一个，也只有在那儿，肖夫兰公民才认得出他。也只有在英格兰，女公民，您才见得着他，才张得开捉他的网；从那儿，您才能诱他随行，把他一路引回法兰西来，就像当年在波尔多，您诱得塔利安公民对您百依百顺那样。一个男人一旦做了美丽的特蕾莎·卡巴吕斯魅力的俘虏，”这位年轻的煽动家献着殷勤补上一句，“她只消招一招手，他就会跟上来——塔利安公民是这样，贝特朗·蒙克里夫是这样，许许多多别人也是这样。把猩红石榴红引到您脚下，就在这儿，就在巴黎，女公民，余下的事交给我们。”

===Original===
While his young devotee spoke thus vehemently, Robespierre had relapsed into his usual pose of affected detachment. His head was bent, his arms were folded across his chest. He appeared to be asleep. When St. Just paused, Theresia waiting awhile, her dark eyes fixed on the great man who had conceived this monstrous project. Monstrous, because of the treachery that it demanded.

Theresia Cabarrus had in truth identified herself with the Revolutionary government. She had promised to marry Tallien, who outwardly at least was as bloodthirsty and ruthless as was Robespierre himself; but she was a woman and not a demon. She had refused to sell Bertrand Moncrif in order to pander to Tallien’s fear of Robespierre. To entice a man﻿—whoever he was﻿—into making love to her, and then to betray him to his death, was in itself an abhorrent idea. What she might do if actual danger of death threatened her, she did not know. No human soul can with certainty say, “I would not do this or that, under any circumstances whatever!” Circumstance and impulse are the only two forces that create cowards or heroes. Principles, willpower, virtue, are really subservient to those two. If they prove the stronger, everything in man must yield to them.

And Theresia Cabarrus had not yet been tried by force of circumstance or driven by force of impulse. Self-preservation was her dominant law, and she had not yet been in actual fear of death.

===Chinese===
他的这位年轻信徒说得这般激昂的时候，罗伯斯庇尔又恢复了往常那副佯装超然的姿态。他低着头，双臂交叠在胸前，像是睡着了。圣茹斯特停下来后，特蕾莎等了一会儿，一双黑眼睛凝望着这位构想出如此骇人计划的伟人。骇人，是因为它所要的是背信弃义。

特蕾莎·卡巴吕斯其实早已同革命政府认同为一体。她答应了嫁给塔利安——此人至少在表面上，同罗伯斯庇尔本人一样嗜血、一样冷酷；可她是个女人，不是恶魔。她曾拒绝出卖贝特朗·蒙克里夫，去迎合塔利安对罗伯斯庇尔的畏惧。去诱一个男子——不管他是谁——向自己求爱，再把他出卖到死地，这主意本身就令人憎恶。倘若死亡的真正威胁临到她自己头上，她会做出什么来，她并不知道。没有哪个人的灵魂能斩钉截铁地说：“无论在什么情况之下，我都不会这样、不会那样！”境遇和冲动，是仅有的两股造就懦夫或英雄的力量。原则、意志力、美德，实际都要听命于这两者。它们若是占了上风，人身上的一切就都得低头。

而特蕾莎·卡巴吕斯，还不曾受过境遇之力的考验，也不曾被冲动之力驱使过。自保是她至高无上的法则，而她还不曾真正惧怕过死亡。

===Original===
This is not a justification on the part of this veracious chronicle of Theresia’s subsequent actions; it is an explanation. Faced with this demand upon her on the part of the most powerful despot in France, she hesitated, even though she did not altogether dare to refuse. Womanlike, she tried to temporise.

She appeared puzzled; frowned. Then asked vaguely:

“Is it then that you wish me to go to England?”

St. Just nodded.

===Chinese===
这部信史在此并非要为特蕾莎日后的行为辩护；这只是作一番解释。面对法兰西最有权势的暴君向她提出的这项要求，她犹豫了，尽管她又不敢断然拒绝。出于女人的天性，她试图拖延周旋。

她作出困惑的样子；皱起眉，随后含含糊糊地问：

“这么说，您是要我到英格兰去？”

圣茹斯特点了点头。

===Original===
“But,” she continued, in the same indeterminate manner, “meseems that you talk very glibly of my﻿—what shall I say?﻿—my proposed dalliance with the mysterious Englishman. Suppose he﻿—he does not respond?”

“Impossible!” Couthon broke in quickly.

“Oh!” she protested. “Impossible? Englishmen are known to be prudish﻿—moral﻿—what? And if the man is married﻿—what then?”

===Chinese===
“可是，”她继续说着，还是那副不置可否的腔调，“照我看，你们把我同那位神秘的英国人之间的——叫我怎么说呢？——那场设想的调情，讲得未免太轻巧了。假如他——他偏偏不上钩呢？”

“不可能！”库东急忙插嘴道。

“哦！”她提出异议。“不可能？谁不知道英国人假正经——讲究道德——不是吗？要是这个人已经有了妻室——那又怎么办？”

===Original===
“The citoyenne Cabarrus underrates her powers,” St. Just riposted glibly.

“Theresia, I entreat!” Tallien put in dolefully.

He felt that the interview, from which he had hoped so much, was proving a failure﻿—nay, worse! For he realised that Robespierre, thwarted in this desire, would bitterly resent Theresia’s positive refusal to help him.

===Chinese===
“卡巴吕斯女公民把自己的本事估低了，”圣茹斯特伶牙俐齿地回敬道。

“特蕾莎，我求求你！”塔利安哭丧着脸插进来说。

他觉出，这场他原本寄予厚望的会晤正落得个失败——不，比失败还糟！因为他明白，罗伯斯庇尔这桩心愿一旦受挫，必会对特蕾莎断然拒绝相助怀恨在心。

===Original===
“Eh, what?” she riposted lightly. “And it is you, citizen Tallien, who would push me into this erotic adventure? I’ faith, your trust in me is highly flattering! Have you not thought that in the process I might fall in love with the Scarlet Pimpernel myself? He is young, they say, handsome, adventurous; and I am to try and capture his fancy﻿ ﻿… the butterfly is to dance around the flame﻿ ﻿… No, no! I am too much afraid that I may singe my wings!”

===Chinese===
“嗳，怎么？”她轻快地回敬。“原来推我去赴这场风流历险的，竟是您吗，塔利安公民？说真的，您对我的这份信任，可真叫人受宠若惊！您就没想到过，这过程中我自己或许会爱上猩红石榴红吗？据说他年轻、英俊、爱冒险；而我呢，就要去试着俘获他的心……蝴蝶要绕着火焰跳舞了……不，不！我可太害怕烧焦了自己的翅膀！”

===Original===
“Does that mean,” Robespierre put in coldly, “that you refuse us your help, citoyenne Cabarrus?”

“Yes﻿—I refuse,” she replied calmly. “The project does not please me, I confess﻿—”

“Not even if we guaranteed immunity to your lover, Bertrand Moncrif?”

She gave a slight shudder. Her lips felt dry, and she passed her tongue rapidly over them.

===Chinese===
“这是不是说，”罗伯斯庇尔冷冷地插话，“您拒绝帮助我们，卡巴吕斯女公民？”

“是——我拒绝，”她平静地答道。“这计划不合我的意，我承认——”

“哪怕我们保证饶过您的情人贝特朗·蒙克里夫，也不答应吗？”

她轻轻打了个寒噤。嘴唇发干，她飞快地用舌头舔了舔。

===Original===
“I have no lover, except citizen Tallien,” she said steadily, and placed her fingers, which had suddenly become ice-cold, upon the clasped hands of her future lord. Then she rose, thereby giving the signal for the breaking-up of the little party.

In truth, she knew as well as Tallien that the meeting had been a failure. Tallien was looking sallow and terribly worried. Robespierre, taciturn and sullen, gave her one threatening glance before he took his leave.

“You know, citoyenne,” he said coldly, “that the nation has means at its disposal for compelling its citizens to do their duty.”

===Chinese===
“除了塔利安公民，我没有情人，”她沉着地说，一面把突然变得冰凉的指尖，搁在她未来夫君交握的双手上。随后她站起身来，就此发出信号，这小小的聚会散了。

说实话，她同塔利安一样心里明白，这场会面是失败了。塔利安面色蜡黄，忧心如焚。罗伯斯庇尔闷声不响、阴沉着脸，临走之前向她投来威胁的一瞥。

“您是知道的，女公民，”他冷冷地说，“国家自有办法，强制它的公民尽自己的本分。”

===Original===
“Ah, bah!” retorted the fair Spaniard, shrugging her shoulders. “I am not a citizen of France. And even your unerring Public Prosecutor would find it difficult to frame an accusation against me.”

Again she laughed, determined to appear gay and inconsequent through it all.

“Think how the accusation would sound, citizen Robespierre!” she went on mockingly. “ ‘The citoyenne Cabarrus, for refusing to make amorous overtures to the mysterious Englishman known as the Scarlet Pimpernel, and for refusing to administer a love-philtre to him as prepared by Mother Théot at the bidding of citizen Robespierre!’ Confess! Confess!” she added, and her rippling laugh had a genuine note of merriment in it at last, “that we none of us would survive such ridicule!”

===Chinese===
“得了吧（Ah, bah!）！”美丽的西班牙女子耸了耸肩，回敬道。“我可不是法兰西的公民。就连您那位从不出错的公共检察官（Public Prosecutor），要给我编派一纸罪名，怕也要犯难。”

她又笑了起来，打定主意，自始至终都要显得快活，显得没心没肺。

“您想想，这罪名念出来会是什么腔调，罗伯斯庇尔公民！”她继续嘲弄道。“‘卡巴吕斯女公民，因拒不向人称猩红石榴红的神秘英国人作出求爱的挑逗，并拒不服从罗伯斯庇尔公民之命、将泰奥妈妈（Mother Théot）配制的爱情迷药喂与该英国人服用！’承认吧！承认吧！”她补了一句，那串银铃般的笑声里，终于有了一丝真正欢愉的调子，“这样的荒唐官司，咱们谁也经不起它这样取笑啊！”

===Original===
Theresia Cabarrus was a clever woman, and by speaking the word “ridicule,” she had touched the one weak chink in the tyrant’s armour. But it is not always safe to prod a tiger, even with a child’s cane, or even from behind protecting bars. Tallien knew this well enough. He was on tenterhooks, longing to see the others depart so that he might throw himself once again at Theresia’s feet and implore her to obey the despot’s commands.

But Theresia appeared unwilling to give him such another chance. She professed intense fatigue, bade him “good night” with such obvious finality, that he dared not outstay his welcome. A few moments later they had all gone. Their gracious hostess accompanied them to the door, since Pepita had by this time certainly gone to bed. The little procession was formed, with St. Just and Chauvelin supporting their palsied comrade, Robespierre detached and silent, and finally Tallien, whose last appealing look to his beloved would have melted a heart of stone.

===Chinese===
特蕾莎·卡巴吕斯是个聪明女人，说出“荒唐可笑”这个字眼，她正戳中了暴君甲胄上唯一的裂缝。可是，戳老虎未必回回安全，哪怕拿的是一根孩子的手杖，哪怕隔着护身的栅栏。这一点，塔利安再清楚不过。他如坐针毡，只盼着旁人快些离去，好让他再一次拜倒在特蕾莎脚下，哀求她依从暴君的命令。

可特蕾莎像是无意再给他这样的机会。她口口声声说乏透了，同他道“晚安”道得那样明白决绝，他不敢再赖着不走。片刻之后，人全都走了。他们那位和蔼可亲的女主人一路送他们到门口——佩皮塔这时候自然早已上床睡了。小小的一行队伍排开：圣茹斯特和肖夫兰一边一个架着他们那位瘫了的同伴，罗伯斯庇尔超然物外、一言不发，最后是塔利安——他投向心上人的最后一瞥哀恳的目光，就是铁石心肠也要被融化。

## XI. Strange Happenings / XI. 怪事迭起

### I / I

===Original===
Now the dingy little apartment in the Rue Villedot was silent and dark. The elegant little lamp with its rose-coloured shade was turned down in the withdrawing-room, leaving only a tiny glimmer of light, which failed to dispel the gloom around. The nocturnal visitors had departed more than a quarter of an hour ago; nevertheless, the beautiful hostess had not yet gone to bed. In fact, she had hardly moved since she bade the final adieu to her timorous lover. The enforced gaiety of the last few moments still sat like a mask upon her face. All that she had done was to sink with a sigh of weariness upon the settee.

And there she remained, with neck craned forward, listening, straining every nerve to listen, even though the heavy, measured footsteps of the five men had long since ceased to echo up and down the stone passages and stairs. Her foot, in its quaint small sandal, beat now and then an impatient tattoo upon the threadbare carpet. Her eyes at intervals cast anxious looks upon the old-fashioned clock above the mantelpiece.

It struck half-past two. Whereupon Theresia rose and went out into the vestibule. Here a tallow candle flickered faintly in its pewter sconce and emitted an evil-smelling smoke, which rose in spirals to the blackened ceiling.

===Chinese===
此刻，维尔多街那套幽暗的小公寓里一片沉寂、一片昏黑。起居室里，那盏罩着玫瑰色灯罩的雅致小灯已经拧暗，只余一星微光，驱不散四下的昏沉。夜访的宾客们离去已有一刻多钟；然而，美丽的女主人仍未上床就寝。说实在的，自打同她那怯生生的恋人最后道别以来，她几乎没有挪动过。方才那片刻强撑出来的欢容，还像一副面具似的罩在她脸上。她所做的，不过是长叹一声，带着一身倦意，颓然陷进长榻里。

她就那么待着，脖颈前探，侧耳谛听，绷住每一根神经去谛听——虽说那五个男人沉重而匀整的脚步声，早已不再顺着石砌的过道与楼梯上上下下地回响。她那只穿着精巧小便鞋的脚，不时在磨得露了线的地毯上敲出急不可耐的嗒嗒声。她的眼睛，时不时朝壁炉架上那座老式时钟投去焦灼的一瞥。

时钟敲响两点半。特蕾莎（Theresia）于是起身，走进外面的门厅。这里，一支油脂蜡烛在白镴烛台上幽幽地闪着，冒出一股难闻的烟，一缕缕盘旋着升向熏黑的天花板。

===Original===
Theresia paused, glanced inquiringly down the narrow passage which gave access to the little kitchen beyond. Between the kitchen and the corner of the vestibule where she was standing, two doors gave on the passage: her bedroom, and that of her maid Pepita. Theresia was vividly conscious of the strange silence which reigned in the whole apartment. The passage was pitch dark save at its farthest end, where a tiny ray of light found its way underneath the kitchen door.

The silence was oppressive, almost terrifying. In a hoarse, anxious voice, Theresia called:

“Pepita!”

But there came no answer. Pepita apparently had gone to bed, was fast asleep by now. But what had become of Bertrand?

===Chinese===
特蕾莎驻了足，朝那条通往里端小厨房的窄过道探询地望了一眼。在厨房与她此刻所站的门厅一角之间，有两扇门开向这条过道：一扇是她的卧室，另一扇是女仆佩皮塔（Pepita）的。整套公寓里笼罩着一种异样的寂静，特蕾莎对此再敏感不过。过道黑得伸手不见五指，唯独最尽头处，一线细光从厨房门底下漏了出来。

这寂静沉甸甸地压下来，几乎叫人胆寒。特蕾莎用沙哑而焦灼的声音唤道：

“佩皮塔！”

可是没有回音。佩皮塔想必已经上了床，这会儿睡得正沉。可是贝特朗（Bertrand）怎么样了呢？

===Original===
Full of vague misgivings, her nerves tingling with a nameless fear, Theresia picked up the candle and tiptoed down the passage. Outside Pepita’s door she paused and listened. Her large dark eyes looked weird in their expression of puzzlement and of awe, the flickering light of the candle throwing gleams of orange-coloured lights into the depths of the widely dilated pupils.

“Pepita!” she called; and somehow the sound of her own voice added to her terror. Strange that she should be frightened like this in her own familiar apartment, and with a faithful, sturdy maid sleeping the other side of this thin partition wall!

“Pepita!” Theresia’s voice was shaking. She tried to open the door, but it was locked. Why had Pepita, contrary to her habit, locked herself in? Had she, too, been a prey to some unexplainable panic? Theresia knocked against the door, rattled the handle in its socket, called more loudly and more insistently, “Pepita!” and, receiving no reply, fell, half-swooning with fear, against the partition wall, whilst the candle slipped out of her trembling grasp and fell with a clatter to the ground.

===Chinese===
满心说不清的不祥预感，神经被一种无名的恐惧刺得发麻，特蕾莎端起蜡烛，蹑手蹑脚地沿过道走去。到了佩皮塔门外，她停住脚听了听。烛光摇曳，把一抹抹橙黄的光投进她那双睁得大大的瞳孔深处；那双又大又黑的眼睛里满含惶惑与骇异，瞧上去怪瘆人的。

“佩皮塔！”她又唤了一声；不知怎的，自己的嗓音反倒添重了她的恐怖。也真怪，在自己这所熟门熟路的公寓里，一板之隔就有个忠心耿耿、筋骨结实的老女仆睡着，她竟会吓成这样！

“佩皮塔！”特蕾莎的声音发起颤来。她试着开门，门却锁上了。佩皮塔为什么一反常态，把自己锁在屋里？莫非她也着了某种说不清道不明的恐慌的道儿？特蕾莎敲着门，把门把手在窝座里摇得咯咯作响，一声比一声高、一遍比一遍急地唤着“佩皮塔！”——始终没有回音，她怕得几欲昏厥，跌靠在板壁上；蜡烛从她发抖的手中滑脱，哐啷一声摔在地上。

===Original===
She was now in complete darkness, with senses reeling and brain paralysed. How long she remained thus, in a state bordering on collapse, she did not know; probably not more than a minute or so. Consciousness returned quickly, and with it the cold sweat of an abject fear; for through this returning consciousness she had perceived a groan issuing from behind the locked door. But her knees were still shaking; she felt unable to move.

“Pepita!” she called again; and to her own ears her voice sounded hoarse and muffled. Straining her ears and holding her breath, she once more caught the sound of a smothered grown.

Whereupon, driven into action by the obvious distress of her maid, Theresia recovered a certain measure of self-control. Pulling herself vigorously together, she began by groping for the candle which had dropped out of her hand a while ago. Even as she stooped down for this she contrived to say in a moderately clear and firm voice:

===Chinese===
此刻她置身一片漆黑，只觉天旋地转，脑子也瘫了。她这样近乎虚脱地撑了多久，自己全然不知；恐怕不过一分钟上下。知觉很快恢复，随之而来的是一阵寒彻心底的冷汗——因为就在知觉回转之际，她察觉那扇锁着的门后发出了一声呻吟。可她的双膝仍在打颤，只觉得动弹不得。

“佩皮塔！”她又唤了一声；在她自己耳中，这声音嘶哑而发闷。她屏住呼吸、竖起双耳，又一次捕捉到一声被捂住的呻吟。

于是，女仆那显而易见的危困驱使她行动起来，特蕾莎找回了几分自制。她奋力定住心神，头一件事便是去摸方才脱手掉落的蜡烛。她一面俯下身去，一面还设法用相当清晰而坚定的口吻说：

===Original===
“Courage, Pepita! I’ll find the light and come back.” Then she added: “Are you unable to unlock the door?”

To this, however, she received no reply save another muffled groan.

Theresia now was on her hands and knees, groping for the candlestick. Then a strange thing happened. Her hands, as they wandered vaguely along the flagged floor, encountered a small object, which proved to be a key. In an instant she was on her feet again, her fingers running over the door until they encountered the keyhole. Into this she succeeded, after further groping, in inserting the key; it fitted and turned the lock. She pushed open the door, and remained paralysed with surprise upon the threshold.

===Chinese===
“撑住，佩皮塔！我找到灯就回来。”随后又补上一句：“你没办法自己开门吗？”

然而，她得到的回答只有又一声闷闷的呻吟。

特蕾莎这会儿手脚并用地趴在地上，摸索那只烛台。这时，一件怪事发生了。她的双手沿着石板地面茫然游移，碰着一个小物件——竟是一把钥匙。转瞬之间她一跃而起，手指在门板上细细摸索，直到摸着锁孔。又摸索了一阵，她总算把钥匙插进了锁孔；钥匙严丝合缝，一转，锁舌开了。她推开门，随即惊得僵立在门槛上。

===Original===
Pepita was reclining in an armchair, her hands tied behind her, a woollen shawl wound loosely around her mouth. In a distant corner of the room, a small oil-lamp, turned very low, cast a glimmer of light upon the scene. For Theresia to run to the pinioned woman and undo the bonds that held her was but the work of a few seconds.

“Pepita!” she cried. “What in heaven’s name has happened?”

The woman seemed not much the worse for her enforced duress. She groaned, and even swore under her breath, and indeed appeared more dazed than hurt. Theresia, impatient and excited, had to shake her more than once vigorously by the shoulder before she was able to gather her scattered wits together.

===Chinese===
佩皮塔歪在一把扶手椅里，双手被反绑在身后，一条羊毛披肩松松地缠在嘴上。房间远处一角，一盏拧得极暗的小油灯给这一幕投下一痕微光。特蕾莎抢到被捆缚的女人跟前，解开捆着她的绳结，前后不过是几秒钟的事。

“佩皮塔！”她失声道。“老天爷在上，到底出了什么事？”

这女人受了一场无妄的拘禁，看去倒没吃多大亏。她哼哼着，还低声咒骂了几句；说实在的，与其说她伤了，不如说她懵了。特蕾莎又急又躁，只得攥住她的肩膀，使劲摇了好几回，才把她七零八落的神智摇拢回来。

===Original===
“Where is M. Bertrand?” Theresia asked repeatedly, ere she got a reply from her bewildered maid.

At last Pepita was able to speak.

“In very truth, Madame,” she said slowly, “I do not know.”

“How do you mean, you do not know?” Theresia queried, with a deepened frown.

===Chinese===
“贝特朗先生在哪儿？”特蕾莎问了一遍又一遍，好半天才从晕头转向的女仆口中讨出一句话来。

佩皮塔总算能开口了。

“说真的，夫人，”她慢吞吞地说，“我不知道。”

“你说不知道，这是什么意思？”特蕾莎问道，眉头蹙得更深了。

===Original===
“Just what I say, my pigeon,” Pepita retorted with marked acerbity. “You ask me what has happened, and I say I do not know. You want to know what has become of M. Bertrand. Then go and look for yourself. When I last saw him, he was in the kitchen, unfit to move, the poor cabbage!”

“But, Pepita,” Theresia insisted, and stamped her foot with impatience, “you must know how you came to be sitting here, pinioned and muffled. Who did it? Who has been here? God preserve the woman, will she never speak!”

===Chinese===
“就是这意思，我的小鸽子（my pigeon），”佩皮塔回敬道，话说得相当尖刻。“你问我出了什么事，我说我不知道。你想知道贝特朗先生的下落，那就自己去找。我最后一回见他时，他在厨房里，动弹不得，可怜的小宝贝！”

“可是，佩皮塔，”特蕾莎不肯罢休，急得直跺脚，“你怎么会坐在那儿，手被捆着、嘴被捂着，这你总该知道。是谁干的？有谁来过？老天爷保佑，这女人莫非永远不肯开口！”

===Original===
Pepita by now had fully recovered her senses. She had struggled to her feet, and went to take up the lamp, then led the way toward the door, apparently intent on finding out for herself what had become of M. Bertrand and in no way sharing her mistress’s unreasoning terror. She halted on the threshold and turned to Theresia, who quite mechanically started to follow her.

“M. Bertrand was sitting in the armchair in the kitchen,” she said simply. “I was arranging a cushion for his head, to make him more comfortable, when suddenly a shawl was flung over my head without the slightest warning. I had seen nothing; I had not heard the merest sound. And I had not the time to utter a scream before I was muffled up in the shawl. Then I was lifted off the ground as if I were a sack of feathers, and I just remember smelling something acrid which made my head spin round and round. But I remember nothing more after until I heard voices in the vestibule when thy guests were going away. Then I heard thy voice and tried to make thee hear mine. And that is all!”

===Chinese===
这会儿佩皮塔已完全定下神来。她挣扎着爬起身，走去端起油灯，随后在前头引路朝门口走去，分明是打定主意要亲自弄清贝特朗先生的下落，全然不像女主人那样没来由地惊惶。她在门槛上顿住，回身朝向特蕾莎——后者正木然抬步，要跟她出去。

“贝特朗先生本来坐在厨房那把扶手椅里，”她平平实实地说。“我正给他头底下安放靠垫，想叫他舒坦些，冷不防一条披肩兜头罩了下来，半点征兆也没有。我什么也没看见，一丝声息也没听见；还没来得及喊出一声，嘴已被披肩捂了个严实。跟着我被人从地上拎起来，像拎一袋羽毛似的；只记得闻见一股辛呛的气味，熏得我脑袋一圈圈直打转。再往后的事，我什么都不记得了，直到听见门厅里你那些客人告辞的人声。后来听见你唤我，我就拼命想让你听见我的声音。就这些！”

===Original===
“When did that happen, Pepita?”

“Soon after the last of thy guests had arrived. I remember I looked at the clock. It must have been half an hour after midnight.”

While the woman spoke, Theresia had remained standing in the middle of the room, looking in the gloom like an elfin apparition, with her clinging, diaphanous draperies. A frown of deep puzzlement lay between her brows and her lips were tightly pressed together as if in wrath; but she said nothing more, and when Pepita, lamp in hand, went out of the room, she followed.

===Chinese===
“那是几时的事，佩皮塔？”

“你末一位客人到后没多久。我记得我还看了钟，准是午夜过后半个钟头。”

这女人讲述的当儿，特蕾莎一直站在房间中央；幽暗里，裹着她那一身贴体的轻薄纱衣，恰似精灵的幻影。她眉宇间凝着深深的困惑，两片嘴唇紧紧抿住，仿佛含着怒气；可她什么也没再说，待佩皮塔端着灯出了房门，便跟了出去。

### II / II

===Original===
When, the kitchen door being opened, that room was found to be empty, Theresia was no longer surprised. Somehow she had expected this. She knew that Bertrand would be gone. The windows of the kitchen gave on the ubiquitous wrought-iron balcony, as did all the other windows of the apartment. That those windows were unfastened, had only been pushed to from the outside, appeared to her as a matter of course. It was not Bertrand who had thrown the shawl over Pepita’s head; therefore someone had come in from the outside and had kidnapped Bertrand﻿—someone who was peculiarly bold and daring. He had not come in from the balcony and through the window, because the latter had been fastened as usual by Pepita much earlier in the evening. No! He had gone that way, taking Bertrand with him; but he must have entered the place in some other mysterious manner, like a disembodied sprite bent on mischief or mystery.

Whilst Pepita fumbled and grumbled, Theresia started on a tour of inspection. Still deeply puzzled, she was no longer afraid. With Pepita to speak and the lamps all turned on, her habitual courage and self-possession had quickly returned to her. She had no belief in the supernatural. Her materialistic, entirely rational mind at once rejected the supposition, hinted at by Pepita, that magical powers had been at work to take Bertrand Moncrif to a place of safety.

===Chinese===
厨房门打开，屋里果然空空如也——特蕾莎已不再惊讶。不知怎的，她早料着了这一步。她知道贝特朗必定已经走了。厨房的窗子开向那无处不在的锻铁阳台，正如这套公寓里其余所有的窗子一样。那些窗没上闩、只从外头虚掩着，在她看来更是理所当然。往佩皮塔头上罩披肩的不是贝特朗；可见是有人从外面进来，劫走了贝特朗——一个胆气格外豪壮、格外泼天的人。那人也不是从阳台翻窗进来的，因为窗子早在晚间就被佩皮塔照例闩好了。不！他是打那条路走的，随身带走了贝特朗；可他进这宅子，必定另有什么神秘的门道，仿佛一个无形无影的精灵，一门心思要作祟弄玄。

佩皮塔摸摸索索、嘟嘟囔囔的当儿，特蕾莎已开始巡视起来。她仍是大惑不解，却不害怕了。有佩皮塔在旁说话，灯又全都点了起来，她素来的胆气与从容便很快回了身。她不信鬼神。她那讲实际、全凭理智的头脑，当下就推翻了佩皮塔话里点到的那个想头——说是有什么法术显灵，把贝特朗·蒙克里夫（Bertrand Moncrif）摄去了安全之地。

===Original===
Something was going on in her brain, certain theories, guesses, conjectures, which she was passionately eager to set at rest. Nor did it take her long. Candle in hand, she had gone round to explore. No sooner had she entered her own bedroom than the solution of the mystery lay revealed before her, in a shutter, forced open from the outside, a broken pane of glass which had allowed a hand to creep in and surreptitiously turn the handle of the tall French window to allow of easy ingress. It had been quickly and cleverly done; the splinters of glass had made no noise as they fell upon the carpet. But for the disappearance of Bertrand, the circumstances suggested a nimble housebreaker rather than a benevolent agency for the rescue of young rashlings in distress.

The frown of puzzlement deepened on Theresia Cabarrus’s brow, and her mobile mouth with the perfectly arched if somewhat thin lips expressed a kind of feline anger, whilst the hand that held the pewter candlestick trembled perceptibly.

===Chinese===
她脑子里正翻腾着些东西——几套理论、几样猜测、几种臆想，她心急火燎，非要弄个水落石出不可。这也没费她多久。她端着蜡烛挨处探看，刚踏进自己的卧室，谜底便赫然摊在眼前：一扇从外面强行撬开的百叶窗，一扇破碎的玻璃——有一只手正是打那儿探进来，神不知鬼不觉地拧开了高大落地窗的把手，好从容入室。手脚做得又快又巧，玻璃碎片落在地毯上，竟不曾出一点声响。只是就贝特朗的失踪而论，这情形与其说出自什么大发慈悲、专搭救落难冒失鬼（rashlings）的善心神通，倒更像一个身手伶俐的入室窃贼所为。

特蕾莎·卡巴吕斯眉间的困惑愈发深锁，那张口齿灵动的嘴——唇弓完美、却略嫌单薄的两片唇——透出一股猫儿似的怒气；握着白镴烛台的那只手，也分明在颤。

===Original===
Pepita’s astonishment expressed itself by sundry exclamations: “Name of a name!” and “Is it possible?” The explanation of the mystery had loosened her tongue, and while she set stolidly to work to clear up the debris of glass in her mistress’s bedroom, she allowed free rein to her indignation against the impudent marauder, who in doubt had only been foiled in his attempt at wholesale robbery by some lucky circumstance which would presently come to light.

The worthy old peasant absolutely refused to connect the departure of M. Bertrand with so obvious an attempt at housebreaking.

“M. Bertrand was determined to go, the poor cabbage!” she said decisively; “since thou didst make him understand that his staying here was a danger to thee. He no doubt took an opportunity to slip out the front door whilst thou wast engaged in conversation with that pack of murderers, whom may the good God punish one of these days!”

===Chinese===
佩皮塔的惊诧化作一连串叫嚷：“活见鬼！”“这怎么可能？”谜底一揭开，她的舌头也跟着松了。她一面在女主人的卧室里闷头打扫玻璃碎屑，一面任着性子痛骂那个不知天高地厚的强盗——说这家伙八成是奔着满载而归来的，只是撞上了什么走运的岔子才没能得手，而这岔子眼下便会水落石出。

这位心地厚道的老村妇，死活不肯把贝特朗先生的离去，同这起明摆着的入室盗窃联系到一处。

“贝特朗先生是横了心要走的，可怜的小宝贝！”她斩钉截铁地说；“既然你叫他想明白了，他留在这儿便是你的祸患。不用问，他是趁你陪着那帮杀人犯说话的工夫，打前门溜出去的——那帮杀人犯，愿仁慈的上帝早一天收拾了他们！”

===Original===
From which remark we may gather that Pepita had not imbibed revolutionary ideals with the air of her native Andalusia.

Theresia Cabarrus, wearied beyond endurance by all the events of this night, as well as by her old servant’s incessant gabble, finally sent her, still muttering and grumbling, to bed.

===Chinese===
由这一句话，我们便可以掂出：佩皮塔当年呼吸着故乡安达卢西亚（Andalusia）的空气长大，可不曾把革命理想也一并吸进肺腑里去。

这一夜的种种变故，加上老仆人没完没了的聒噪，把特蕾莎·卡巴吕斯折腾到了忍无可忍的地步；末了，她打发她上床去——那老妇人嘴里兀自嘟囔个不停。

## XII. Chauvelin / XII. 肖夫兰

===Original===
Theresia had opposed a stern refusal to Pepita’s request that she might put her mistress to bed before she herself went to rest. She did not want to go to bed: she wanted to think, and now that that peculiar air of mystery, that silence and semidarkness no longer held their gruesome sway in her apartment, she did not feel afraid.

Pepita went to bed. For awhile, Theresia could hear her moving about, with ponderous, shuffling footsteps; then, presently everything was still. The clock of old St. Roch struck three. Not much more than half an hour had gone by since her guests had been departed. To Theresia it seemed like an infinity of time. The sense of a baffling mystery being at work around her had roused her ire and killed all latent fear.
===Chinese===
佩皮塔（Pepita）请求让她先伺候女主人安寝、自己再去歇息，特蕾莎（Theresia）却断然回绝了。她不想上床：她要想一想事情；如今那股异样的神秘气氛、那片沉寂与半明半暗，已不再在她寓所里逞着阴森的威势，她也就不觉得害怕了。

佩皮塔上床去了。有一阵子，特蕾莎还听得见她挪来挪去的动静，脚步沉重拖沓；随后不久，一切归于寂静。老圣罗歇教堂（St. Roch）的钟敲了三下。客人们告辞而去，至今不过才过去半个多钟头，可在特蕾莎的感觉里，这却像是无穷无尽的一段光阴。眼下周遭正有一种令人无从捉摸的神秘在暗中作祟——这份感觉激起了她的怒火，也扼杀了她心底潜伏的恐惧。

===Original===
But what was the mystery?

And was there a mystery at all? Or was Pepita’s rational explanation of the occurrence of this night the right one after all?
===Chinese===
可这桩神秘事，究竟是什么呢？

再者，当真有什么神秘事吗？还是说到底，佩皮塔对今夜这桩变故那番合乎情理的解释，才是正解？

===Original===
Citoyenne Cabarrus, unable to sit still, wandered up and down the passage, in and out of the kitchen; in and out of her bedroom, and thence into the vestibule. Then back again. At one moment, when standing in the vestibule, she thought she heard someone moving on the landing outside the front door. Her heart beat a little more rapidly, but she was not afraid. She did not believe in housebreakers and she felt that Pepita, who was a very light sleeper, was well within call.

So she went to the front door and opened it. The quick cry which she gave was one of surprise rather than of fear. In her belated visitor she had recognised citizen Chauvelin; and somehow, by a vague process of reasoning, his presence just at this moment seemed quite rational﻿—in keeping with the unsolved mystery that was so baffling to the fair Theresia.
===Chinese===
卡巴吕斯女公民坐立不安，在过道里踱来踱去，一会儿进厨房，一会儿出厨房；又走进卧室，再从卧室踱进门厅，然后又踱回来。有一会儿，她正站在门厅里，仿佛听见前门外楼梯平台上有动静。她的心跳得略快了些，可她并不害怕。她不信会有撬门入室的窃贼，况且佩皮塔睡觉极轻、稍有动静便醒，就在近旁，一唤即应。

于是她走到前门，把门打开。她脱口而出的那一声短促的惊呼，与其说是出于害怕，不如说是出于诧异。她认出这位深夜来客是肖夫兰（Chauvelin）公民；也不知怎的，凭着一种模模糊糊的推想，他偏偏在此时此刻出现，倒显得顺理成章——同那桩令美丽的特蕾莎百思不解的悬案正相吻合。

===Original===
“May I come in, citoyenne?” Chauvelin said in a whisper. “It is late, I know; but there is urgency.”

He was standing on the threshold, and she, a few paces away from him in the vestibule. The candle, which now burned low in its socket, was behind her. Its light touched with a weird, flickering glow on the pale face of the once noted Terrorist, with its pale eyes and sharply hooked nose, which gave him the air of a gaunt bird of prey.
===Chinese===
“我能进来吗，女公民（citoyenne）？”肖夫兰压着嗓音说。“我知道，时候不早了；可事情紧急。”

他站在门槛上，她则在离他几步远的门厅里。那支蜡烛这时在烛台上已燃得将尽，搁在她身后。烛光幽幽地、忽明忽暗地照在这位当年赫赫有名的恐怖党徒（Terrorist）那张苍白的脸上——那双淡白的眼睛，那只尖利钩起的鹰钩鼻，使他活脱脱像一只瘦骨嶙峋的猛禽。

===Original===
“It is late,” she murmured vaguely. “What do you want?”

“Something has happened,” he replied, still speaking below his breath. “Something which concerns you. And, before speaking of it to citizen Robespierre﻿—”

At the dread name Theresia stepped farther back into the vestibule.
===Chinese===
“是晚了，”她恍恍惚惚地低声道。“您想要什么？”

“出事了，”他答道，依旧压着嗓音。“一件与您有关的事。而在向罗伯斯庇尔（Robespierre）公民提起之前——”

一听这可怕的名字，特蕾莎便朝门厅深处又退了一步。

===Original===
“Enter!” she said curtly.

He came in, and she closed the door carefully behind him. Then she led the way into the withdrawing room and turned up the wick of the lamp under its rosy shade. She sat down and motioned to him to do the same.
===Chinese===
“进来吧！”她生硬地说。

他走了进来，她小心地随手把门在他身后关严。然后她引路进了客厅，把玫瑰色灯罩下那盏灯的灯芯捻亮。她坐下来，又示意他也坐。

===Original===
“What is it?” she asked.

Before replying, Chauvelin’s finger and thumb﻿—thin and pointed like the talons of a vulture﻿—went fumbling in the pocket of his waistcoat. From it he extracted a small piece of neatly folded paper.
===Chinese===
“什么事？”她问。

还未答话，肖夫兰的拇指和食指——细而尖，像秃鹫的爪子——先在马甲口袋里摸索了一阵，从里面摸出一张叠得整整齐齐的小纸片。

===Original===
“When we left your apartment, citoyenne﻿—my friend St. Just and I supporting poor palsied Couthon, and Robespierre following close behind us﻿—I spied this scrap of paper, which St. Just’s careless foot had just kicked to one side when he was stepping across the threshold. Some unknown hand must have insinuated it underneath the door. Now, I never despise stray bits of paper. I have had so many through my hands that proved after examination to be of paramount importance. So, whilst the others were busy with their own affairs I, unseen by them, had already stooped and picked the paper up.”
===Chinese===
“我们离开您寓所的时候，女公民——我的朋友圣茹斯特（St. Just）和我一边一个架着可怜的瘫子库东（Couthon），罗伯斯庇尔紧随在我们身后——我瞥见了这小片纸，是圣茹斯特迈过门槛时，叫他那只漫不经心的脚刚刚踢到一边去的。必定是哪只不相识的手，把它从门缝底下塞了进来。而我呢，从来不小看任何散落的纸片。经我手过的这种纸片多得很，事后一查验，不少都关系重大。所以，趁旁人各自忙着自己的事、谁也没留意我，我早已弯腰把纸片拾了起来。”

===Original===
He paused for a moment or two, then, satisfied that he held the beautiful woman’s undivided attention, he went on in his habitual dry, urbane monotone:

“Now, though I was quite sure in my own mind, citoyenne, that this billet-doux was intended for your fair hands, I felt that, as its finder, I had some sort of lien upon it﻿—”
===Chinese===
他停顿了片刻，随后，确信这位美人的心思已全然在他身上，便用他惯常那种干巴巴、文绉绉的平板腔调，接着说了下去：

“话虽如此，女公民，我心里十拿九稳，这封情书（billet-doux）本是要送到您那双玉手里的；可我又觉得，我既是拾到它的人，对它总多少有几分权利——”

===Original===
“To the point, citizen, I pray you!” Theresia broke in harshly, tried by a show of impatience and of fatigue to hide the anxiety which had once more taken possession of her heart. “You found a letter addressed to me; you read it. As you have brought it here, I presume that you wish me to know its contents. So get on, man, get on!” she added more vehemently. “It is not at three in the morning that one cares for dalliance.”

By way of reply, Chauvelin slowly unfolded the note and began to read:
===Chinese===
“说正题吧，公民，我求您了！”特蕾莎厉声打断他，拿一副不耐烦和倦怠的姿态，遮掩那重新攫住她心头的焦虑。“您拾到一封写给我的信；您读了它。既然您把它带到这儿来，想必是要我知道信里写了什么。那就往下说呀，伙计，往下说！”她添了一句，语气愈发激烈。“清早三点钟，可不是跟人调情逗趣的时候。”

算作回答，肖夫兰慢条斯理地展开那纸条，念了起来：

===Original===
“ ‘Bertrand Moncrif is a young fool, but he is too good to be the plaything of a sleek black pantheress, however beautiful she might be. So I am taking him away to England where, in the arms of his long-suffering and loyal sweetheart, he will soon forget the brief madness which so nearly landed him on the guillotine and made of him a tool to serve the selfish whims of Theresia Cabarrus.’ ”
===Chinese===
“‘贝特朗·蒙克里夫（Bertrand Moncrif）是个年轻的小傻瓜，可他心性太良善，不该给一只油光水滑的黑母豹当玩物——任凭她生得多么美丽。所以我这就带他远走英格兰，在他那位久经苦候、忠贞不渝的心上人怀抱里，他很快就会忘却这场短暂的痴狂——正是这场痴狂，险些把他送上了断头台，还把他变成一件工具，供特蕾莎·卡巴吕斯（Theresia Cabarrus）发泄她自私的奇想。’”

===Original===
Theresia had listened to the brief, enigmatic epistle without displaying the slightest sign of emotion or surprise. Now, when Chauvelin had finished reading, and with his strange, dry smile had handed her the tiny note, she took it and for awhile contemplated it in silence, her face perfectly placid save for a curious and ominous contraction of the brows and a screwing-up of the fine eyes, which gave her a curious, snakelike expression.

“You know, of course, citoyenne,” Chauvelin said after awhile, “who the writer of this﻿—shall we say?﻿—impudent epistle happens to be?”

She nodded.
===Chinese===
特蕾莎听着这封简短而扑朔迷离的短信，没有流露出一丝一毫动容或惊诧的迹象。这时肖夫兰念完了，带着他那古怪干涩的微笑，把那小小的纸条递给她；她接过来，默然端详了片刻，面色十分平静，只是眉头有一种古怪而不祥的紧蹙，一双秀目微微眯起，使她脸上透出一种奇异的、蛇一般的神情。

“您当然是知道的，女公民，”过了一会儿，肖夫兰开口道，“写这封——我们不妨这么说吧？——无礼短信的人，究竟是哪一位？”

她点了点头。

===Original===
“The man,” he went on placidly, “who goes by the name of the Scarlet Pimpernel. The impudent English adventurer whom citizen Robespierre has asked *you*, citoyenne, to lure into the net which we may spread for him.”

Still Theresia was silent. She did not look at Chauvelin, but kept her eyes fixed upon the scrap of paper, which she had folded into a long, narrow ribbon and was twining in and out between her fingers.
===Chinese===
“就是那个人，”他不动声色地接着说，“人称猩红石榴红（Scarlet Pimpernel）的那位。就是那个无礼的英国冒险家——罗伯斯庇尔公民请的偏偏是您，女公民，请您去把他诱进我们不妨为他撒下的那张罗网。”

特蕾莎依旧不作声。她不看肖夫兰，只把目光钉在那片纸条上——她已把它折成一条又长又窄的细带，正让它在指间穿来穿去。

===Original===
“A while ago, citoyenne,” Chauvelin continued, “in this very room, you refused to lend us a helping hand.”

Still no reply from Theresia. She had just smoothed out the mysterious epistle, carefully folded it into four, and was in the act of slipping it into the bosom of her gown. Chauvelin waited quite patiently. He was accustomed to waiting, and patience was an integral part of his stock in trade. Opportunism was another.
===Chinese===
“方才，女公民，”肖夫兰继续道，“就在这个房间里，您可是拒绝过助我们一臂之力的。”

特蕾莎还是不答。她刚把那封神秘的短信抚平，仔细折成四折，正要把它塞进裙衫的胸口里去。肖夫兰极有耐心地等着。他向来等惯了，耐心本是他吃饭家什里不可缺的一件。另一件，便是相机行事。

===Original===
Theresia was sitting on her favourite settee, leaning forward with her hands clasped between her knees, her head was bent, and the tiny rose-shaded lamp failed to throw its glimmer of light upon her face. The clock on the mantelshelf behind her was ticking with insentient monotony. Anon, a distant chime struck the quarter after three. Whereupon Chauvelin rose.

“I think we understand one another, citoyenne,” he said quietly, and with a sigh of complete satisfaction. “It is late now. At what hour may I have the privilege of seeing you alone?”
===Chinese===
特蕾莎坐在她心爱的那张长榻上，身子前俯，双手交握夹在两膝之间，头低垂着；那盏小小的玫瑰罩灯，一点微光竟照不到她的脸上。她身后壁炉架上的钟，木然而单调地滴答走着。不多时，远处一阵钟声敲响了三点一刻。于是肖夫兰站起身来。

“我想，咱们彼此心里都有数了，女公民，”他轻声说，带着一声完全心满意足的叹息。“现在晚了。几点钟我能有幸单独拜见您呢？”

===Original===
“At three in the afternoon?” she replied tonelessly, like one speaking in a dream. “Citizen Tallien is always at the Convention then, and my door will be denied to everybody else.”

“I’ll be here at three o’clock,” was Chauvelin’s final word.
===Chinese===
“下午三点吧？”她声调平板地答道，像人在梦里说话。“塔利安（Tallien）公民那时候总是在国民公会（Convention）里，我的门对其他任何人都闭门不纳。”

“三点钟我到这儿来。”肖夫兰丢下这最后一句。

===Original===
Theresia had not moved. He made her a deep bow and went out of the room. The next moment, the opening and shutting of the outer door proclaimed that he had gone.

After that, Theresia Cabarrus went to bed.
===Chinese===
特蕾莎没有动。他朝她深深一躬，走出了房间。转眼间，外头大门一开一关，宣告他已经走了。

打这以后，特蕾莎·卡巴吕斯便上床睡了。

## XIII. The Fisherman’s Rest / XIII. 渔人客栈

### I / I

===Original===
And whilst the whole of Europe was in travail with the repercussion of the gigantic upheaval that was shaking France to its historic foundations, the last few years had seen but very little change in this little corner of England.

The Fisherman’s Rest stood where it had done for two centuries and long before thrones had tottered and anointed heads fallen on the scaffold. The oak rafters, black with age, the monumental hearth, the tables and high-backed benches, seemed like mute testimonies to good order and to tradition, just as the shiny pewter mugs, the foaming ale, the brass that glittered like gold, bore witness to unimpaired prosperity and an even, well-regulated life.

===Chinese===
当整个欧洲还在为那场把法兰西摇撼得历史根基动摇的巨大剧变的余波而阵痛时，英格兰的这个小小角落里，近几年却几乎没有起过什么变化。

渔人客栈（The Fisherman’s Rest）两百年间一直矗立在老地方——早在王座倾覆、涂过圣油的头颅纷纷滚落断头台之前许久，它就在那里了。年代熏黑的橡木房梁、厚重壮观的大壁炉、一张张桌子、一圈圈高背长凳，仿佛是良序与传统的无言见证；正如那锃亮的白镴大酒杯、泛着泡沫的麦芽酒、金子般熠熠放光的黄铜器皿，见证着未曾受损的富足与平稳安定的生活。

===Original===
Over in the kitchen yonder, Mistress Sally Waite, as she now was, still ruled with a firm if somewhat hasty hand, the weight of which, so the naughty gossips averred, even her husband, Master Harry Waite, had experienced more than once. She still queened it over her father’s household, presided over his kitchen, and drove the young scullery wenches to their task with her sharp tongue and an occasional slap. But The Fisherman’s Rest could not have gone on without her. The copper saucepans would in truth not have glittered so, nor would the home-brewed ale have tasted half so luscious to Master Jellyband’s faithful customers, had not Mistress Sally’s strong brown hands drawn it for them, with just the right amount of creamy foam on the top and not a bit too much.

And so it was still many a “Ho, Sally! ’Ere Sally! ’Ow long’ll you be with that there beer!” or “Say, Sally! A cut of your cheese and homebaked bread; and look sharp about it!” that resounded from end to end of the long, low-raftered coffee-room of The Fisherman’s Rest, on this fine May day of the year of grace 1794.

Sally Waite, her muslin cap set at a becoming angle, her kerchief primly folded over her well-developed bosom, and her kirtle neatly raised above a pair of exceedingly shapely ankles, was in and out of the room, in and out of the kitchen, tripping it like a benevolent if somewhat substantial fairy, bandying chaff here, administering rebuke there, hot, panting and excited.

===Chinese===
那边厨房里，如今已为人妇的萨莉·韦特太太（Mistress Sally Waite）依旧用一只坚定却略嫌急躁的手掌管一切——那巴掌的分量，照那些促狭鬼嚼舌根的说法，连她丈夫哈里·韦特（Master Harry Waite）都不止一次领教过。她仍在她父亲的家里当女王，主持着父亲的厨房，用尖利的舌头外加偶尔一记耳光，驱赶着年轻的小洗碗女工们干活。可是渔人客栈少了她还真是转不动。说实在的，若不是萨莉太太那双有力的棕色巧手替杰利班德老板（Master Jellyband）忠实的主顾们打酒——顶上那层奶油似的泡沫恰到好处、不多一分——那些铜汤锅断不会那样锃亮，那家酿的麦芽酒，在主顾们口中也不会有那般一半的甘醇。

于是在公元 1794 年 5 月这个晴好的日子里，渔人客栈那房梁低矮的深长咖啡室里，从这头到那头，依旧回荡着一声声“嗬，萨莉！萨莉哎！那啤酒你还要磨蹭多久！”或是“喂，萨莉！切一份你的奶酪、来些自家烤的面包——手脚麻利点儿！”

萨莉·韦特头上那顶细布软帽戴得俏皮合宜，一方头巾端端正正叠掩在丰盈的胸前，裙裾利落地撩起，露出一双格外出挑的脚踝；她屋里屋外、厨房内外进进出出，像一位心肠虽好、体态却颇丰腴的仙女似的脚步轻盈，这边打趣两句，那边训斥一声，浑身热烘烘、气喘吁吁、兴头十足。

### II / II

===Original===
The while mine host, Master Jellyband﻿—perhaps a shade more portly of figure, a thought more bald of pate, these last two years﻿—stood with stubby legs firmly planted upon his own hearth, wherein, despite the warmth of a glorious afternoon, a log fire blazed away merrily. He was giving forth his views upon the political situation of Europe generally with the self-satisfied assurance born of complete ignorance and true British insular prejudice.

Believe me, Mr. Jellyband was in no two minds about “them murderin’ furriners over yonder” who had done away with their King and Queen and all their nobility and quality, and whom England had at last decided to lick into shape.

===Chinese===
与此同时，店主杰利班德老板——这两年下来，身量兴许胖了一圈，头顶兴许又荒了一分——叉开两条短粗的腿，稳稳站在自家的壁炉跟前；虽说外面是个风和日丽的暖和午后，炉膛里却有一棚柴火欢快地烧得正旺。他正向人发表自己对欧洲大局的高见，那份踌躇自满的笃定，出自十足的无知与地道的英国岛民偏见。

相信我，对于“海外那帮杀人的外国佬”，杰利班德先生心里绝无半点含糊——就是那伙害死了自家国王和王后、除尽了全部贵族与上流人物的人；对这伙人，英国如今总算决定出手整治了。

===Original===
“And not a moment too soon, hark’ee, Mr. ’Empseed,” he went on sententiously. “And if I ’ad my way, we should ’ave punished ’em proper long before this﻿—blown their bloomin’ Paris into smithereens and carried off the pore Queen afore those murderous villains ’ad ’er pretty ’ead off ’er shoulders!”

Mr. Hempseed, from his own privileged corner in the inglenook, was not altogether prepared to admit that.

“I am not for interfering with other folks’ ways,” he said, raising his quaking treble so as to stem effectually the torrent of Master Jellyband’s eloquence. “As the Scriptures say﻿—”

===Chinese===
“一点儿也不早了，你听着，亨普西德先生，”他一本正经地接着说。“要是由我做主，早就好好惩治过他们了——把他们那倒灶的巴黎炸个稀巴烂，赶在那帮杀人不眨眼的恶棍砍下可怜王后那颗俏脑袋之前，把她抢出来！”

亨普西德先生（Mr. Hempseed）在壁炉角他那个享有特权的座位上坐着，倒不打算全然认可这话。

“我可不赞成去插手别人家的活法，”他说着，提高他那打颤的尖细嗓音，好一举截住杰利班德老板滔滔雄辩的洪流。“正如《圣经》上说的——”

===Original===
“Keep your dirty fingers from off my waist!” came in decisive tones from Mistress Sally Waite, whilst the shrill sound made by the violent contact of a feminine hand against a manly cheek froze the Scriptural quotation on Mr. Hempseed’s lips.

“Now then, now then, Sally!” Mr. Jellyband thought fit to say in stern tones, not liking his customers to be thus summarily dealt with.

“Now then, father,” Sally retorted, with a toss of her brown curls, “you just attend to your politics, and Mr. ’Empseed to ’is Scriptures, and leave me to deal with them impudent jackanapes. You wait!” she added, turning once more with a parting shot directed against the discomfited offender. “If my ’Arry catches you at them tricks, you’ll see what you get﻿—that’s all!”

===Chinese===
“把你那脏爪子从我腰上拿开！”萨莉·韦特太太斩钉截铁地喝道；与此同时，一只女人的手掌狠狠掴在一个男人的脸颊上，那声脆响把亨普西德先生嘴边的经文生生冻住了。

“好了，好了，萨莉！”杰利班德先生觉得有必要板起面孔说上一句——他可不喜欢自家的客人这样干脆利落地挨收拾。

“得了，爹，”萨莉把一头棕色鬈发一甩，回嘴道，“您只管操心您的政治，亨普西德先生只管念他的《圣经》，那些无礼的猴崽子交给我来治。你给我等着！”她又回过身，朝那个败下阵来的冒失鬼补上一句。“要是叫我哈里撞见你搞这些把戏，有你好看的——就这么回事！”

===Original===
“Sally!” Mr. Jellyband admonished, more sternly this time. “You’ll ’ave my lord Hastings ’ere before ’is dinner is ready.”

Which suggestion so overawed Mistress Sally that she promptly forgot the misdoings of the forward swain and failed to hear the sarcastic chuckle which greeted the mention of her husband’s name. With an excited little cry, she ran quickly out of the room.

Mr. Hempseed, loftily unaware of interruption, concluded his sententious remark:

===Chinese===
“萨莉！”杰利班德先生这一回更加声色俱厉。“你再闹下去，黑斯廷斯勋爵（Hastings）就要赶在他饭菜还没备好的当口到这儿了。”

这一提把萨莉太太镇得不轻，她登时忘了那个放肆登徒子的轻薄勾当，也没听见人们提到她丈夫名字时响起的那阵讪笑。她激动地轻呼一声，快步跑出了屋子。

亨普西德先生高高地端着架子，全然不觉方才被打断过，从容说完了他那句一本正经的高论：

===Original===
“As the Scriptures say, Mr. Jellyband: ‘ ’Ave no fellowship with the unfruitful work of darkness.’ I don’t ’old not with interfering. Remember what the Scriptures say: ‘ ’E that committeth sin is of the devil, and the devil sinneth from the beginning,’ ” he concluded with sublime irrelevance, sagely nodding his head.

But Mr. Jellyband was not thus lightly to be confounded in his argument﻿—no, not by any quotation, relevant or otherwise!

===Chinese===
“正如《圣经》上说的，杰利班德先生：‘那暗昧无益的事，不要与人同行。’我可不赞成插手别人的事。记着《圣经》上的话：‘犯罪的是属魔鬼，因为魔鬼从起初就犯罪。’”他煞有介事地点着头，用一句文不对题到极处的话收了尾。

可要这样轻易就把杰利班德先生驳倒在争论里，没门——任你引什么经据什么典，切题也罢，不切题也罢，统统不行！

===Original===
“All very fine, Mr. ’Empseed,” he said, “and good enough for them ’oo, like yourself, are willin’ to side with them murderin’ reprobates﻿ ﻿…”

“Like myself, Mr. Jellyband?” protested Mr. Hempseed, with as much vigour as his shrill treble would allow. “Nay, but I’m not for them children of darkness﻿—”

“You may be or you may not,” Mr. Jellyband went on, nothing daunted. “There be many as are, and ’oo’d say ‘Let ’em murder,’ even now, but I say that them as ’oo talk that way are not true Englishmen; for ’tis we Englishmen ’oo can teach the furriner just what ’e may do and what ’e may not. And as we’ve got the ships and the men and the money, we can just fight ’em as are not of our way o’ thinkin’. And let me tell you, Mr. ’Empseed, that I’m prepared to back my opinions ’gainst any man as don’t agree with me!”

===Chinese===
“说得倒轻巧，亨普西德先生，”他说，“像你这样甘愿替那帮杀人的恶棍帮腔的人，听着正合适……”

“把我扯进去，杰利班德先生？”亨普西德先生提出抗议，那尖细的嗓门里能挣出多少劲就挣出多少劲。“不不，我可不站在那些黑暗之子一边——”

“你也许是，也许不是，”杰利班德先生毫不气馁，接着往下说。“这种人多着呢，直到这会儿还会说‘让他们杀去吧’；可我告诉你说，讲这种话的人就不是真正的英国人——因为正是我们英国人，才教训得了外国佬什么能做、什么不能做。何况我们有船、有人、有钱，凡是跟我们不是一个想法的，我们就同他们干。我告诉你，亨普西德先生，谁不服我的见解，我随时奉陪到底！”

===Original===
For the nonce Mr. Hempseed was silent. True, a Scriptural text did hover on his thin, quivering lips; but as no one paid any heed to him for the moment its appositeness will forever remain doubtful. The honours of victory rested with Mr. Jellyband. Such lofty patriotism, coupled with so much sound knowledge of political affairs, could not fail to leave its impress upon the more ignorant and the less fervent amongst the frequenters of The Fisherman’s Rest.

Indeed, who was more qualified to pass an opinion on current events than the host of that much-frequented resort, seeing that the ladies and gentlemen of quality who came to England from over the water, so as to escape all them murtherin’ reprobates in their own country, did most times halt at The Fisherman’s Rest on their way to London or to Bath? And though Mr. Jellyband did not know a word of French﻿—no furrin lingo for him, thank ’ee!﻿—he nevertheless had mixed with all that nobility and gentry for over two years now, and had learned all that there was to know about the life over there, and about Mr. Pitt’s intentions to put a stop to all those abominations.

===Chinese===
眼下亨普西德先生哑了声。不错，一段经文确曾在他那两片薄薄的、哆嗦着的嘴唇边上打转；可那一刻谁也没理会他，那段经文到底切不切题，只好永远存疑了。胜利的荣冠归了杰利班德先生。这般崇高的爱国心肠，配上这等扎实深厚的政局学问，断不能不在渔人客栈常客中那些更无知、更不热心的人心里刻下印子。

再说，对时局发表高见，谁比得上这处宾客盈门的客店的店主？要知道，那些为躲避本国杀人恶棍而渡海来英国的上流仕女，在去伦敦（London）或巴斯（Bath）的路上，十有八九要在渔人客栈歇歇脚。虽说杰利班德先生一个法文词也不识——外国话他可不学，谢谢您啦！——可他到底同那班贵族缙绅厮混了两年有余，海那边的生活是什么光景、皮特（Pitt）先生打算怎样根除那些丑行，该知道的他是全知道了。

### III / III

===Original===
Even now, hardly had mine hosts conversation with his favoured customers assumed a more domestic turn, than a loud clatter on the cobblestones outside, a jingle and a rattle, shouts, laughter and bustle, announced the arrival of guests who were privileged to make as much noise as they pleased.

Mr. Jellyband ran to the door, shouted for Sally at the top of his voice with a “Here’s my lord Hastings!” to add spur to Sally’s hustle. Politics were forgotten for the nonce, arguments set aside, in the excitement of welcoming the quality.

===Chinese===
恰在此刻，店主同他那些受宠的主顾的闲谈刚转上家常，外面鹅卵石街上便响起一片喧闹——蹄声哒哒、铃铛叮当、车声辚辚，夹着呼喊、欢笑与忙乱——宣告一批客人驾到；这批客人有的是特权，爱多吵便可多吵。

杰利班德先生奔向门口，扯开嗓门喊萨莉，喊一声“黑斯廷斯勋爵到啦！”好给萨莉的腿脚再抽上一鞭。一时政治忘了，争论撂下了，都忙着迎接贵客，好不兴奋。

===Original===
Three young gallants in travelling clothes, smart of appearance and debonair of mien, were ushering a party of strangers﻿—three ladies and two men﻿—into the hospitable porch of The Fisherman’s Rest. The little party had walked across from the inner harbour, where the graceful masts of an elegant schooner lately arrived in port were seen gently swaying against the delicately coloured afternoon sky. Three or four sailors from the schooner were carrying luggage, which they deposited in the hall of the inn, then touched their forelocks in response to a pleasant smile and nod from the young lords.

“This way, my lord,” Master Jellyband reiterated with jovial obsequiousness. “Everything is ready. This way! Hey, Sallee!” he called again; and Sally, hot, excited, blushing, came tripping over from the kitchen, wiping her hot plump palms against her apron in anticipation of shaking hands with their lordships.

===Chinese===
三位一身旅行装的年轻公子，衣着漂亮，风度潇洒，正引着一批陌生客人——三位女士、两位男士——走进渔人客栈好客的门廊。这一小行人是从内港步行过来的；港里一艘新近进港的雅致纵帆船，那秀美的桅杆映着色调轻柔的午后天空，微微摇曳。船上的三四个水手扛着行李，把行李安放在客栈大厅里，随后碰了碰额发，答谢年轻爵爷们愉快的一笑与颔首。

“这边请，勋爵大人，”杰利班德老板满面堆欢、点头哈腰地又说了一遍。“样样都备妥了。这边走！嘿，萨莉！”他又喊了一声；萨莉热烘烘、兴冲冲，红着脸，从厨房那边碎步跑来，在围裙上擦着滚热丰润的手掌，一心要同各位爵爷拉手。

===Original===
“Since Mr. Waite isn’t anywhere about,” my lord Hastings said gaily, as he put a bold arm round Mistress Sally’s dainty waist, “I’ll e’en have a kiss, my pretty one.”

“And I, too, by gad, for old sake’s sake!” Lord Tony asserted, and planked a hearty kiss on mistress Sally’s dimpled cheek.

“At your service, my lords, at your service!” Master Jellyband rejoined, laughing. Then added more soberly: “Now then, Sally, show the ladies up into the blue room, the while their lordships ’ave a first shake down in the coffee-room. This way, gentlemen﻿—your lordships﻿—this way!”

The strangers in the meanwhile had stood by, wide-eyed and somewhat bewildered in face of this exuberant hilarity which was so unlike what they had pictured to themselves of dull, fog-ridden England﻿—so unlike, too, the dreary moroseness which of late had replaced the erstwhile lighthearted gaiety of their own countrymen. The porch and the narrow hall of The Fisherman’s Rest appeared to them seething with vitality. Everyone was talking, nobody seemed to listen; everyone was merry, and everyone knew everybody else and was pleased to meet them. Sonorous laughter echoed from end to end along the solid beams, black and shiny with age. It all seemed so homely, so happy. The deference paid to the young gallants and to them as strangers by the sailors and the innkeeper was so genuine and hearty without the slightest sign of servility, that those five people who had left behind them so much class-hatred, enmity and cruelty in their own country, felt an unaccountable tightening of the heart, a few hot tears rise to their eyes, partly of joy, but partly too of regret.

===Chinese===
“既然韦特先生不在跟前，”黑斯廷斯勋爵快活地说，一面大着胆子伸出胳膊，搂住萨莉太太纤细的腰肢，“那我不妨讨个香吻了，我的小美人。”

“看在旧日情分的份上，我也来一个，老天作证！”托尼勋爵（Lord Tony）宣称道，跟着在萨莉太太带酒窝的脸颊上响亮地亲了一口。

“听各位爵爷差遣，听差遣！”杰利班德老板笑着应道。随即又添上一句，语气郑重了些：“好了，萨莉，领太太小姐们上楼到蓝房间去，让各位爵爷先在咖啡室里安顿安顿。这边请，各位先生——各位爵爷——这边请！”

这当儿，那几位陌生客人一直站在一旁，面对这般奔放的热闹，个个睁圆了眼睛，有些发懵——这同他们自己想象中那个沉闷多雾的英格兰太不一样了；同他们同胞近来取代了往日轻松欢快的那种阴郁愁苦，更是天差地别。渔人客栈的门廊和窄窄的大厅，在他们眼里简直沸腾着生气。人人都在说话，似乎谁也不听谁的；人人都快活，人人彼此相识，见了面个个欢喜。浑厚的笑声顺着结结实实的房梁从这头滚到那头，梁木年深日久，黑亮黑亮的。这一切显得那么自在，那么快活。水手们和店主对年轻公子们、对他们这些生客所表示的敬意，那么真挚、那么热诚，又没有半点奴颜婢膝的影子——这五个人在本国抛下了那么多的阶级仇恨、敌意与残忍，此刻只觉得心头没来由地一紧，几滴热泪涌上眼眶，一半是欢喜，一半也是怅惘。

### IV / IV

===Original===
Lord Hastings, the youngest and merries of the English party, guided the two Frenchmen toward the coffee-room, with many a jest in atrocious French and kindly words of encouragement, all intended to put the strangers at their ease.

Lord Anthony Dewhurst and Sir Andrew Ffoulkes﻿—a trifle more serious and earnest, yet equally happy and excited at the success of their perilous adventure and at the prospect of reunion with their wives﻿—lingered a moment longer in the hall, in order to speak with the sailors who had brought the luggage along.

===Chinese===
黑斯廷斯勋爵是英国一行中最年轻、最快活的一位；他引着两位法国人往咖啡室去，一路上用糟糕透顶的法语说了不少笑话，又讲了许多和气的宽慰话，全是想让这几位客人安下心来的。

安东尼·杜赫斯特勋爵（Lord Anthony Dewhurst）和安德鲁·福尔克斯爵士（Sir Andrew Ffoulkes）——两位略嫌严肃、略嫌郑重，却同样为这趟凶险冒险的功成、为即将同妻子团聚而欢喜激动——在大厅里又多待了一会儿，好同搬运行李来的水手们说话。

===Original===
“Do you know aught of Sir Percy?” Lord Tony asked.

“No, my lord,” the sailor gave answer; “not since he went ashore early this morning. ’Er Ladyship was waitin’ for ’im on the pier. Sir Percy just ran up the steps and then ’e shouted to us to get back quickly. ‘Tell their lordships,’ ’e says, ‘I’ll meet them at The Rest.’ And then Sir Percy and ’er ladyship just walked off and we saw naun more of them.”

“That was many hours ago,” Sir Andrew Ffoulkes mused, with an inward smile. He too saw visions of meeting his pretty Suzanne very soon, and walking away with her into the land of dreams.

===Chinese===
“可有珀西爵士的消息？”托尼勋爵问。

“不知道，爵爷，”水手答道；“打他今儿一大早上了岸，就再没见着影儿了。夫人正在码头上候着他呢。珀西爵士一溜烟跑上台阶，接着就冲我们喊，叫我们快回去。‘告诉各位爵爷，’他说，‘我到客栈去会他们。’然后珀西爵士和夫人就那么走掉了，我们再没见着他们。”

“那已是好几个钟头前的事了。”安德鲁·福尔克斯爵士喃喃自语，唇边泛起一丝会心的微笑。他也望见了不日便将见到他美丽的苏珊（Suzanne）、同她双双走进梦之乡的光景。

===Original===
“ ’Twas just six o’clock when Sir Percy ’ad the boat lowered,” the sailor rejoined. “And we rowed quick back after we landed ’im, but the *Daydream*, she ’ad to wait for the tie. We wurr a long while gettin’ into port.”

Sir Andrew nodded.

“You don’t know,” he said, “if the skipper had any further orders?”

“I don’t know, sir,” the man replied. “But we mun be in readiness always. No one knows when Sir Percy may wish to set sail again.”

===Chinese===
“珀西爵士吩咐放小艇那会儿，刚敲六点，”水手接着说。“送他上了岸，我们便飞快划回来，可‘白日梦’号（Daydream）得候着潮水。我们磨了好半天才进的港。”

安德鲁爵士点点头。

“你可知道，”他说，“船长还领没领别的吩咐？”

“不知道，先生，”那人答道。“不过我们得时刻备着。谁也说不准珀西爵士什么时候又要扬帆出海。”

===Original===
The two young men said nothing more, and presently the sailors touched their forelocks and went away. Lord Tony and Sir Andrew exchanged knowing smiles. They could easily picture to themselves their beloved chief, indefatigable, like a boy let out from school, exhilarated by the deadly danger through which he had once more passed unscathed, clasping his adored wife in his arms and wandering off with her, heaven knew whither, living his life of joy and love and happiness during the brief hours which his own indomitable energy, his reckless courage, accorded to the sentimental side of his complex nature.

Far too impatient to wait until the tide allowed the *Daydream* to get into port, he had been rowed ashore in the early dawn, and his beautiful Marguerite﻿—punctual to the assignation conveyed to her by one of those mysterious means of which Percy alone knew the secret﻿—was ready there to receive him, to forget in the shelter of his arms the days of racking anxiety and of cruel terror for her beloved through which she had again and again been forced to pass.

===Chinese===
两位年轻人没再多说什么；不多时，水手们碰了碰额发，自去了。托尼勋爵和安德鲁爵士相视一笑，心照不宣。他们不难在心中勾勒出他们爱戴的首领：不知疲倦，活像个放了学的小男孩，又一次毫发未损地闯过致命的险境，正自兴奋难抑，把挚爱的妻子搂在怀中，同她信步远去——天晓得去了哪儿——趁着他不屈不挠的精力、亡命的胆魄施舍给他复杂天性中多情一面的这短短几个时辰，过他充满欢乐、爱情与幸福的日子。

他实在等不及潮水送“白日梦”号进港，天方破晓便让人划小艇送上了岸；他美丽的玛格丽特——凭着只有珀西一人知晓底细的那类神秘途径之一接到幽会的传讯，分毫不差地如约而至——早已候在那里迎他，要在他的怀抱的荫庇里，把那些为挚爱之人撕心裂肺的焦灼、那些残忍的恐怖日子统统忘掉；那样的日子，她被迫一遭又一遭地熬了过来。

===Original===
Neither Lord Tony nor Sir Andrew Ffoulkes, the Scarlet Pimpernel’s most faithful and devoted lieutenants, begrudged their chief these extra hours of bliss, the while they were left in charge of the party so lately rescued from horrible death. They knew that within a day or two﻿—within a few hours, perhaps﻿—Blakeney would tear himself away once more from the clinging embrace of his exquisite wife, from the comfort of luxury of an ideal home, from the adulation of friends, the pleasures of wealth and of fashion, in order mayhap to grovel in the squalor and filth of some outlandish corner of Paris, where he could be in touch with the innocents who suffered﻿—the poor, the terror-stricken victims of the merciless revolution. Within a few hours, mayhap, he would be risking his life again every moment of the day, in order to save some poor hunted fellow-creature﻿—man, woman or child﻿—from death that threatened them at the hands of inhuman monsters who knew neither mercy nor compunction.

And for the nineteen members of the League, they took it in turns to follow their leader where danger was thickest. It was a privilege eagerly sought, deserved by all, and accorded to those who were most highly trusted. It was invariably followed by a period of rest in happy England, with wife, friends, joy and luxury. Sir Andrew Ffoulkes, Lord Anthony Dewhurst and my lord Hastings had been of the expedition which brought Mme. de Serval with her three children and Bertrand Moncrif safely to England, after adventures more perilous, more reckless of danger, than most. Within a few hours they would be free to forget in the embrace of clinging arms every peril and every adventure save the eternal one of love, free to forswear everything outside that, save their veneration for their chief and their loyalty to his cause.

===Chinese===
托尼勋爵和安德鲁·福尔克斯爵士——猩红石榴红最忠实、最赤忱的两员副将——谁也不眼红首领多享这几个钟头的福气；他们只管照看这一行方才从可怕的死亡里搭救出来的客人。他们知道，过不了一两天——兴许只消几个钟头——布莱克尼便会再次挣脱娇妻依依不舍的怀抱，挣脱理想家园的舒适奢华，挣脱朋友的奉承、财富与时尚的欢乐，没准儿又钻进巴黎哪个穷街陋巷的肮脏污秽里去，同受苦的无辜者——那些穷人、那场无情革命吓破了胆的受害者——通气接头。兴许只消几个钟头，他又要一天到晚时时刻刻拿性命去冒险，好把某个可怜的、被追猎的同类——无论是男是女是孩子——从那些既不知怜悯、也不知悔恨的吃人魔鬼伸来的死亡威胁里救出来。

至于同盟的十九名成员，则是轮番跟随首领，奔赴险情最炽的地方。这是人人争求、人人当得的权利，只授予最受信任的人。轮过一遭，照例便回到幸福的英格兰歇上一阵，有娇妻，有朋友，有欢乐，有奢华。安德鲁·福尔克斯爵士、安东尼·杜赫斯特勋爵和黑斯廷斯勋爵都参加了那趟远征，把德·塞尔瓦尔夫人连同她的三个孩子和贝特朗·蒙克里夫平安送到了英国；一路的历险，比寻常更多凶险，更加不惜性命。只消几个钟头，他们便可自由自在地偎在依依环抱的柔臂里，把一切险境、一切冒险统统忘掉——唯独那永恒的爱情之险除外；也可把爱情之外的一切统统弃绝——唯独对首领的崇敬、对首领事业的忠诚，不在此列。

## XIV. The Castaway / XIV. 漂流者

### I / I

===Original===
An excellent dinner served by Mistress Sally and her attendant little wenches put everybody into rare good-humour. Madame de Serval﻿—pale, delicate, with gentle, plaintive voice and eyes that had acquired a pathetically furtive look﻿—even contrived to smile, her heart warmed by the genuine welcome, the rare gaiety that irradiated this fortunate corner of God’s earth. Wars and rumours of war reached it only as an echo of great things that went on in the vast outside world; and though more than one of Dover’s gallant sons had perished in one or the other of the Duke of York’s unfortunate incursions into Holland, or in one of the numerous naval engagements off the Western shores of France, on the whole, the war, intermittent and desultory, had not yet cast its heavy gloom over the entire country.

Joséphine and Jacques de Serval, whose enthusiasm for martyrdom had received so severe a check in the course of the Fraternal Supper in the Rue. St. Honoré, had at first with the self-consciousness of youth adopted an attitude of obstinate and irreclaimable sorrow, until the antics of Master Harry Waite, pretty Sally’s husband﻿—jealous as a young turkey-cock of every gallant who dared to ogle his buxom wife﻿—brought laughter to their lips. My Lord Hastings’ comical attempts at speaking French, the droll mistakes he made, easily did the rest; and soon their lively, high-pitched Latin voices mingled with unimpaired gaiety with the more mellow sound of Anglo-Saxon tongues.

===Chinese===
萨莉太太（Mistress Sally）带着她手下那帮小丫头端上了一顿上好的晚餐，人人被哄得心里罕有地畅快。德·塞尔瓦尔夫人（Madame de Serval）——面色苍白，身子单薄，嗓音温柔哀婉，一双眼已带上一种凄惶躲闪的神气——居然也强自绽出了笑容：这一方蒙天眷顾的角落里流溢着真诚的欢迎、难得的欢快，暖了她的心。战争及战争的传闻传到这儿，不过是外面大千世界里种种大事的一缕回声；虽说约克公爵（Duke of York）几度进兵荷兰，回回都不走运，多佛尔（Dover）英勇的儿郎已有不止一个殒命在这几役的这一场或那一场里，或是殒命在法兰西西面海岸外无数次海战之中，但总的说来，这场时断时续、零敲碎打的战争，还不曾把它沉重的阴霾整个儿罩到全国头上。

约瑟芬（Joséphine）和雅克·德·塞尔瓦尔（Jacques de Serval）——在圣奥诺雷街（Rue St. Honoré）那场兄弟晚餐会（Fraternal Supper）上，两人那股殉道的豪兴挨过狠狠的一记挫折——起初还带着青年人特有的那份自觉，摆出一副执拗到底、无可救药的愁苦姿态；直到哈里·韦特小哥（Master Harry Waite）——就是漂亮的萨莉的丈夫，活像一只年轻的雄火鸡，凡有风流客胆敢朝他丰腴的老婆多瞟一眼，他都要妒火中烧——作出种种滑稽腔来，才把他们逗得笑了出来。黑斯廷斯勋爵（Lord Hastings）学说法语时的那些滑稽努力、他闹出的种种逗笑的差错，又轻而易举地包办了余下的功夫；不多时，他们那活泼尖亮的拉丁嗓子，便带着不减的欢快，同更为圆浑低回的盎格鲁-撒克逊腔调融到了一处。

===Original===
Even Régine de Serval had smiled when my lord Hastings had asked her with grave solemnity whether Mme. de Serval would wish “*le fou de descendre*”﻿—the lunatic to come downstairs﻿—meaning all the while whether she wanted the fire in the big hearth to be let down, seeing that the atmosphere in the coffee-room was growing terribly hot.

The only one who seemed quite unable to shake off his moroseness was Bertrand Moncrif. He sat next to Régine, silent, somewhat sullen, a look that seemed almost one of dull resentment lingering in his eyes. From time to time, when he appeared peculiarly moody or when he refused to eat, her little hand would steal out under the table and press his with a gentle, motherly gesture.

===Chinese===
连雷吉娜·德·塞尔瓦尔（Régine de Serval）也失笑了：方才黑斯廷斯勋爵一本正经地问她，德·塞尔瓦尔夫人是不是想要“*le fou de descendre*”（把疯子放下楼来）——他满心想问的原是：咖啡室里热得吓人，大壁炉里的火要不要压一压。

唯一似乎怎么也甩不掉那身郁色的，是贝特朗·蒙克里夫（Bertrand Moncrif）。他挨着雷吉娜坐，一言不发，有些阴沉，眼里滞留着一抹近乎麻木怨怼的神情。时不时地，每逢他显得格外郁郁寡欢，或是干脆不肯进食，她那只小手便会从桌子底下悄悄伸过去，带着一个温柔的、慈母般的动作，握一握他的手。

### II / II

===Original===
It was when the merry meal was over and while Master Jellyband was going the round with a fine bottle of smuggled brandy, which the young gentlemen sipped with unmistakable relish, that a commotion arose outside the inn; whereupon Master Harry Waite ran out of the coffee-room in order to see what was amiss.

Nothing very much apparently. Waite came back after a moment or two and said that two sailors from the barque *Angela* were outside with a young French lad, who seemed more dead than alive, and whom it appears the barque had picked up just outside French waters, in an open boat, half perished with terror and inanition. As the lad spoke nothing but French, the sailors had brought him along to The Fisherman’s Rest, thinking that maybe some of the quality would care to interrogate him.

===Chinese===
就在这顿欢宴将近尾声、杰利班德（Jellyband）老板正捧着一瓶上好的走私白兰地挨桌巡敬、年轻绅士们也津津有味地小口品着的时候，客栈外面起了一阵骚动；于是哈里·韦特小哥跑出咖啡室，去看看出了什么乱子。

看样子倒没什么大不了的。韦特过了一忽儿便回来，说“安杰拉号（Angela）”三桅帆船上的两名水手正在外面，带着一个法国少年，那少年瞧上去已只剩半口气了。据说是这条船刚出法兰西领海，就在一条敞舱小船里把他搭救了上来——人已经吓得半死，又饿得只剩一口气。只因这少年除了法语什么都不会说，水手们便把他一路领到渔人客栈（The Fisherman’s Rest）来，心想兴许店里哪位体面贵人乐意盘问盘问他。

===Original===
At once Sir Andrew Ffoulkes, my Lord Tony and Lord Hastings were on the qui vive. A lad in distress, coming from France, found alone in an open boat, suggested one of those tragedies in which the League of the Scarlet Pimpernel was wont to play a role.

“Let the lad be taken into the parlour, Jellyband,” Sir Andrew commanded. “You’ve got a fire in there, haven’t you?”

“Yes, yes, Sir Andrew! We always keep fires going here until past the 15th of May.”

“Well then, get him in there. Then give him some of your smuggled brandy first, you old dog! then some wine and food. After that we’ll find out something more about him.”

===Chinese===
安德鲁·福弗克斯爵士（Sir Andrew Ffoulkes）、托尼勋爵（Lord Tony）和黑斯廷斯勋爵登时都警觉起来。一个落难的少年，来自法兰西，孤身一人被人发现漂在一条敞舱小船上——这情形便叫人想到某一类惨剧，而猩红石榴红（Scarlet Pimpernel）的同盟向来惯于在这类惨剧里充当一个角色。

“把这少年带进雅间去，杰利班德，”安德鲁爵士吩咐道。“你们那儿生着火呢，是不是？”

“是，是，安德鲁爵士！我们这儿的炉火向来不熄，一直要生到5月15日以后呢。”

“那就把他弄进去。先灌他一点儿你那走私白兰地，你这老家伙！再给他些酒和吃食。过后咱们再来打听打听他的底细。”

===Original===
He himself went along in order to see that his orders were carried out. Jellyband, as usual, had already deputed his daughter to do the necessary, and in the hall there was Mistress Sally, capable and compassionate, supporting, almost carrying, a youth who in truth appeared scarce able to stand.

She led him gently into the small private parlour, where a cheerful log-fire was blazing, sat him down in an armchair beside the hearth, after which Master Jellyband himself poured half a glass of brandy down the poor lad’s throat. This revived him a little, and he looked about him with huge, scared eyes.

===Chinese===
他自己也跟着过去，好亲眼看着吩咐一一照办。杰利班德呢，照例早把这桩必要的事差给了女儿；大厅里，能干而慈悲的萨莉太太正搀着——几乎是抱着——一个青年，那青年瞧上去当真连站都站不大稳了。

她把他轻轻搀进那间小小的独用雅间，里面一炉劈柴火烧得正旺；她扶他在壁炉旁的扶手椅里坐下，随后杰利班德老板亲自把小半杯白兰地灌进这可怜少年的喉咙。这一下他缓过来一点儿，睁着一双惊惶的大眼睛，四下里张望。

===Original===
“*Sainte Mére de Dieu!*” he murmured feebly. “Where am I?”

“Never mind about that now, my lad,” replied Sir Andrew, whose knowledge of French was of a distinctly higher order than that of his comrades. “You are among friends. That is enough. Have something to eat and drink now. Later we’ll talk.”

===Chinese===
“上帝的圣母啊（*Sainte Mére de Dieu*）！”他有气无力地喃喃道。“我这是在哪儿？”

“这个眼下先别管，小伙子，”安德鲁爵士答道——他的法文造诣，比他几位同伴明显高出一筹。“你如今是在朋友们中间了。这就够了。先喝点什么、吃点什么。回头咱们再谈。”

===Original===
He was eyeing the boy keenly. Contact with suffering and misery over there in France, under the leadership of the most selfless, most understanding man of this or any time, had intensified his powers of perception. Even the first glance had revealed to him the fact that here was no ordinary waif. The lad spoke with a gentle, highly refined voice; his skin was delicate, and his face exquisitely beautiful; his hands, though covered with grime, and his feet, encased in huge, coarse boots, were small and daintily shaped, like those of a woman. Already Sir Andrew had made up his mind that if the oilskin cap which sat so extraordinarily tightly on the boy’s head were to be removed, a wealth of long hair would certainly be revealed.

However, all these facts, which threw over the young stranger a further veil of mystery, could not in all humanity be investigated now. Sir Andrew Ffoulkes, with the consummate tact born of kindliness, left the lad alone as soon as he appeared able to sit up and eat, and himself rejoined his friends in the coffee-room.

===Chinese===
他正锐利地打量着这少年。在法兰西那边的种种苦难惨状里历练过来——在那位古往今来最无私、最善体人意的伟人麾下历练过来——他的洞察力早已磨砺得愈发敏锐。只消第一眼，他便看出这绝非一个寻常的流浪儿。这少年开口嗓音温软、极见教养；肌肤细嫩，眉目精致得出奇；一双手虽蒙着污垢，一双脚虽套在又大又粗的靴子里，却都生得小巧秀气，像女人的一般。安德鲁爵士心里早已认定：那顶在少年头上箍得出奇紧的油布帽子底下，必定藏着一头浓密的长发。

不过，这些给这位年轻陌生人又罩上一层神秘纱幕的种种迹象，此刻出于人情道理，终究不便深究。安德鲁·福弗克斯爵士怀着仁厚者天生的那种周全得体的分寸，一见少年坐得起来、吃得下东西，便任他独自待着，自己回咖啡室与朋友们会合去了。

## XV. The Nest / XV. 爱巢

### I / I

===Original===

No one, save a very few intimates, knew of the little nest wherein Sir Percy Blakeney and his lady hid their happiness on those occasions when the indefatigable Scarlet Pimpernel was only able to spend a few hours in England, and when a journey to their beautiful home in Richmond could not be thought of. The house﻿—it was only a cottage, timbered and creeper-clad﻿—lay about a mile and a half outside Dover, off the main road, perched up high on rising ground over a narrow lane. It had a small garden round it, which in May was ablaze with daffodils and bluebells, and in June with roses. Two faithful servants, a man and his wife, looked after the place, kept the nest cosy and warm whenever her ladyship wearied of fashion, or else, actually expecting Sir Percy, would come down from London for a day or two in order to dream of that elusive and transient happiness for which her soul hungered, even while her indomitable spirit accepted the inevitable.

===Chinese===

除了极少数心腹，没有人知道这座小小的爱巢。每逢那不知疲倦的猩红石榴红（Scarlet Pimpernel）只能在英国消磨几个钟头、根本谈不上赶回里士满（Richmond）美丽宅邸的时节，珀西·布莱克尼爵士（Sir Percy Blakeney）和他的夫人便把他们的幸福藏在这巢里。这座宅子——其实只是一栋小屋，木骨墙上爬满藤蔓——坐落在多佛尔（Dover）城外约一英里半的地方，离开大路，高踞在一条窄巷上方隆起的坡地上。屋子四周围着一座小花园，五月里水仙与风铃草开得一片绚烂，六月里又是满园玫瑰。一对忠仆——一个男仆和他的妻子——照管着这地方：每当夫人厌倦了上流社会的浮华，或是果真盼着珀西爵士要来，从伦敦（London）下来住上一两天，好去梦她灵魂所渴慕的那份缥缈而易逝的幸福——纵使她那不屈的精神早已听天由命——两人总把爱巢收拾得温暖舒适。

===Original===

A few days ago the weekly courier from France had brought her a line from Sir Percy, together with the promise that she should rest in his arms on the 1st of May. And Marguerite had come down to the creeper-covered cottage knowing that, despite obstacles which might prove insuperable to others, Percy would keep his world.

She had stolen out at dawn to wait for him on the pier; and sure enough, as soon as the May-day sun, which had risen today in his glory as if to crown her brief happiness with warmth and radience, had dissipated the morning mist, her yearning eyes had spied the smart white gig which had put off from the *Daydream* leaving the graceful ship to await the turn of the tide before putting into port.

===Chinese===

几天前，每周一班从法兰西来的信使给她捎来珀西爵士的几行字，还捎来一句许诺：5月1日，她准能偎进他的怀抱。于是玛格丽特（Marguerite）来到这座爬满藤蔓的小屋；她知道，纵然那些阻碍在旁人看来或许万难逾越，珀西也必定言而有信。

她天没亮便悄悄溜出门，到码头上去等他；果然，五一节的太阳今天升得光彩夺目，仿佛要用温暖与光辉为她这短暂的幸福加冕；晨雾刚被它驱散，她那双望眼欲穿的眼睛便望见了从“白日梦”号（Daydream）上放下的一艘漂亮白色小艇，那艘秀美的船只得候着转潮，才能进港。

===Original===

Since then, every moment of the day had been one of rapture. The first sight of her husband in his huge caped coat, which seemed to add further inches to his great height, his call of triumph when he saw her, his arms outstretched, there, far away in the small boat, with a gesture of such infinite longing that for a second or two tears obscured Marguerite’s vision. Then the drawing up of the boat against the landing-stage; Percy’s spring ashore; his voice, his look; the strength of his arms; the ardour of his embrace. Rapture, in truth, to which the thought of its brief duration alone lent a touch of bitterness.

But of parting again Marguerite would not think﻿—not today, while the birds were singing a deafening paean of joy; not while the scent of growing grass, of moits, travailing earth, was in her nostrils; not while the sap was in the trees, and the gummy crimson buds of the chestnuts were bursting into leaf. Not while she wandered up the narrow lane between hedges of blackthorn in bloom, with Percy’s arm around her, his loved voice in her ear, his merry laughter echoing through the sweet morning air.

===Chinese===

打那以后，这一天的每一刻都令人心醉神迷。头一眼望见丈夫裹在那件带披肩的硕大外套里——那外套仿佛让他本就魁伟的身量又平添了几寸——他望见她时那一声得意的呼喊，他远远地在那条小艇里张开双臂，那姿势怀着无穷无尽的渴念，教玛格丽特的眼睛有那么一两秒钟被泪水模糊了。接着是小艇拢上栈桥；珀西一跃上岸；他的嗓音，他的眼神；他双臂的力气；他拥抱的热烈。实在是心醉神迷，唯独一想到这陶醉何其短促，才给它添了一丝苦味。

可是再别离的事，玛格丽特偏不去想——今天不想，鸟儿正唱着震耳欲聋的欢乐颂歌；生长着的青草、湿润而劳作不息的大地的气息，正沁在她鼻端；树身浆液正满，栗树上黏润的绛红芽苞正绽出叶来。她正沿着窄窄的小巷、在开花的黑刺李树篱之间漫步，珀西的胳膊搂着她，他可爱的嗓音响在她耳边，他快活的笑声在甜美的晨风里回荡——这样的时候，她不想。

===Original===

After that, breakfast in the low, raftered room﻿—the hot, savoury milk, the home-backed bread, the home-churned butter. Then the long, delicious, intimate talk of love, and of yearnings, of duty and of gallant deeds. Blakeney kept nothing secret from his wife; and what he did not tell her, that she easily guessed. But it was from the members of the League that she learned all there was to know of heroism and selflessness in the perilous adventures through which her husband passed with so lighthearted a gaiety.

===Chinese===

之后，便在那房梁低矮的屋子里用早餐——滚热喷香的牛奶，自家烤的面包，自家打的黄油。再往后，便是那场绵绵长谈，甜蜜而贴心，谈爱情，谈渴念，谈责任，谈侠义功勋。布莱克尼（Blakeney）对妻子无所隐瞒；他不曾告诉她的，她也一猜便着。不过，关于她丈夫以何等轻松欢快的心情穿过的那些险象环生的历险，其中种种英勇与无私，她倒都是从同盟（the League）的成员们那里听来的。

===Original===

“You should see me as an asthmatic reprobate, m’dear,” he would say, with his infectious laugh. “And hear that cough! Lud love you, but I am mightily proud of that cough! Poor old Rateau does not do it better himself; and he is genuinely asthmatic.”

He gave her an example of his prowess; but she would not allow him to go on. The sound was too weird, and conjured up visions which today she would fain forget.

===Chinese===

“你真该瞧瞧我扮成气喘的老无赖那副模样，亲爱的，”他总是带着他那富有感染力的笑声说。“再听听那咳嗽！老天作证，我可着实为那声咳嗽得意呢！可怜的老拉托（Rateau）亲口咳嗽起来，也强不到哪儿去；他是真害着气喘呢。”

他当场给她演示了一番自己的绝技；她却不许他再演下去。那声音太瘆人，勾起的种种景象，她今天巴不得统统忘掉。

===Original===

“Rateau was a real find,” he went on more seriously; “because he is three parts an imbecile and as obedient as a dog. When some of those devils are on my track, lo! the real Rateau appears and yours truly vanishes where no one can find him!”

“Pray God,” she murmured involuntarily, “they never may!”

“They won’t, m’dear, they won’t!” he asserted with lighthearted conviction. “They have become so confused now between Rateau the coalheaver, the mysterious Scarlet Pimpernel, and the problematic English milor, that all three of these personalities can appear before their eyes and they will let ’em all escape! I assure you that the confusion between the Scarlet Pimpernel who was in the antechamber of Mother Théot on that fateful afternoon, and again at the Fraternal Supper in the Rue St. Honoré, and the real Rateau who was at Mother Théot’s while that same exciting supper party was going on, was so great that not one of those murdering reprobates could trust his own eyes and ears, and that we got away as easily as rabbits out of a torn net.”

===Chinese===

“拉托可真是个难得的发现，”他接着说，语气郑重了些；“他十分里倒有七分是低能儿，又像狗一样听话。每逢那帮魔鬼盯上我的踪迹，瞧！真拉托便露面了，而鄙人就此遁形，任谁也找不着！”

“求上帝，”她不由自主地喃喃道，“千万别叫他们找着！”

“找不着，亲爱的，找不着的！”他满心笃定、轻快地断言。“他们如今叫运煤工拉托、神秘的猩红石榴红，还有那个说不清道不明的英国爵爷（milor）搅得晕头转向，这三副面孔就是一齐站到他们眼皮底下，他们也会一个个都放跑！我向你保证，那个凶险的下午身在泰奥妈妈（Mother Théot）候见室里、随后又出现在圣奥诺雷街（Rue St. Honoré）兄弟晚餐会（Fraternal Supper）上的猩红石榴红，同那个当那场热闹晚宴进行时正在泰奥妈妈家的真拉托，两下里把他们搅得天旋地转，那帮杀人不眨眼的恶棍没一个敢信自己的眼睛耳朵，我们脱身便容易得像兔子钻出破网。”

===Original===

Thus did he explain and laugh over the perilous adventure where he had faced a howling mob disguised as Rateau the coalheaver, and with almost superhuman pluck and boldness had dragged Mme. de Serval and her children into the derelict house which was one of the League’s headquarters. That is how he characterised the extraordinary feat of audacity when, in order to give his gallant lieutenants time to smuggle the unfortunates out of the house through a back and secret way, he showed himself on the balcony above the multitude, and hurled dummy figures into the brazier below.

Then came the story of Bertrand Moncrif, snatched half-unconscious out of the apartment of the fair Theresia Cabarrus, whilst Robespierre himself sat not half a dozen yards away, with only the thickness of a wall between him and his arch enemy.

===Chinese===

他便是这样一面讲解、一面笑着回顾那段险象环生的历险：他扮作运煤工拉托，直面一群嚎叫的暴民，凭着近乎神勇的胆魄，硬是把德·塞尔瓦尔夫人（Mme. de Serval）和她的孩子们拖进了那座荒宅——同盟的一处据点。他又是这样讲述那桩胆大包天的奇功：为了给麾下勇悍的副手们争取时间，好把那几个落难的人从宅子后头一条隐秘的路径偷偷送出去，他索性现身在人群头顶的阳台上，朝底下火盆里一个个扔假人。

接下来便是贝特朗·蒙克里夫（Bertrand Moncrif）的故事：他半昏半醒之际，被人从美艳的特蕾莎·卡巴吕斯（Theresia Cabarrus）的寓所里一把抢走，而当时罗伯斯庇尔（Robespierre）本人就坐在不出六码远的地方，同他的死敌之间只隔着一堵墙的厚度。

===Original===

“How the woman must hate you!” Marguerite murmured, with a slight shudder of acute anxiety which she did her best to conceal. “There are things that a woman like the Cabarrus will never forgive. Whether she cares for Bertrand Moncrif or no, her vanity will suffer intensely, and she will never forgive you for taking him out of her clutches.”

===Chinese===

“那女人该多恨你啊！”玛格丽特喃喃道，一阵剧烈的焦灼使她微微一颤，又被她竭力掩饰过去。“有些事，卡巴吕斯那样的女人是永远不会宽恕的。且不论她心里有没有贝特朗·蒙克里夫，她的虚荣心已重重受了伤，你把他从她掌心里夺走，她永远不会饶你。”

===Original===

He laughed.

“Lud, m’dear!” he said lightly. “If we were to take heed of all the people who hate us we should spend our lives pondering rather than doing. And all I want to ponder over,” he added, whilst his glance of passionate earnestness seemed to envelop her like an exquisite warm mantle, “is your beauty, your eyes, the scent of your hair, the delicious flavour of your kiss!”

===Chinese===

他哈哈一笑。

“老天，亲爱的！”他轻描淡写地说。“要是把恨我们的人个个都放在心上，那我们这一辈子就光顾着掂量，什么也别干了。而我满心想掂量的，”他添上一句，那热烈恳切的目光仿佛一件精美的暖披风，把她整个裹住，“是你的美貌，你的眼睛，你发间的香气，还有你那一吻的甜滋味！”

### II / II

===Original===

It was some hours later on that same glorious day, when the shadows of ash and chestnut lay right across the lane and the arms of evening folded the cosy nest in their mysterious embrace, that Sir Percy and Marguerite sat in the deep window-embrasure of the tiny living-room. He had thrown open wide the casements, and hand resting in hand, they watched the last ray of golden light lingering in the west and listened to the twitterings which came like tender “good nights” from the newly-built nests among the trees.

It was one of those perfect spring evenings, rare enough in northern climes, without a breath of wind, when every sound carries clear and sharp through the stillness around. The air was soft and slightly moist, with a tang in it of wakening life and of rising sap, and with the scent of wild narcissus and of wood violets rising like intoxicating incense to the nostrils. It was in truth one of those evening when happiness itself seems rudely out of place, and nature﻿—exquisite, but so cruelly, transient in her loveliness﻿—demands the tribute of gentle melancholy.

===Chinese===

还是在那个光辉灿烂的日子，几小时之后，梣树与栗树的影子已横过小巷，暮色的臂膀把这座温暖的爱巢拢进它神秘的怀抱，珀西爵士和玛格丽特坐在小小起居室深深的窗龛里。他把窗扉推得大开，两人手叠着手，望着西方流连不去的最后一缕金光，听着树间新筑的巢里传来的一声声啁啾，宛如道道温柔的“晚安”。

这是北方难得一遇的完美春夜，没有一丝风，四下静得出奇，每个声响都传得清清楚楚、锐利分明。空气温柔而微潮，带着一丝苏醒的生命、上涨的树液的辛香，野水仙与林中紫罗兰的芬芳如醉人的熏香，冉冉升上鼻端。说实在的，这样的夜晚，连幸福本身都显得莽撞得不合时宜；造化——精妙绝伦，她的美却又残忍地转瞬即逝——此刻索要的，是一缕温柔愁思的贡赋。

===Original===

A thrush said something to its mate﻿—something insistent and tender that lulled them both to rest. After that, Nature became quite still, and Marguerite, with a catch in her throat which she would have given much to suppress, laid her head upon her husband’s breast.

Then it was that suddenly a man’s voice, hoarse but distant, broke in upon the perfect peace around. What it said could not at first be gathered. It took some time ere Marguerite became sufficiently conscious of the disturbing noise to raise her head and listen. As for Sir Percy, he was wrapped in the contemplating of the woman he worshipped, and nothing short of an earthquake would have dragged him back to reality, had not Marguerite raised herself on her knees and quickly whispered:

===Chinese===

一只画眉对它的伴侣说了句什么——一句又执着又温柔的话，把两个人都哄得安恬下来。此后，造化便全然静默了；玛格丽特喉咙里涌上一阵哽咽——她多想把它压下去——把头靠在了丈夫的胸前。

就在此时，忽然一个男人的声音，沙哑而遥远，打破了周遭完美的安宁。起初听不清说的是什么。过了好一会儿，玛格丽特才充分意识到这扰人的声响，抬起头来细听。至于珀西爵士，他正沉浸在对他所膜拜的女人的凝想里，若不是玛格丽特直起身子跪坐着、急促地悄声唤了一句，只怕天塌地陷也拉不回他来：

===Original===

“Listen!”

The man’s voice had been answered by a woman’s raised as if in defiance that seemed both pitiful and futile.

===Chinese===

“听！”

男人的声音得到一个女人声音的回答，那嗓音扬着，像是在抗辩，听来既凄楚又徒劳。

===Original===

“You cannot harm me now. I am in England!”

Marguerite leaned out of the window, tried to peer into the darkness which was fast gathering over the lane. The voices had come from there: first the man’s, then the woman’s, and now the man’s again; both speaking in French, the woman obviously terrified and pleading, the man harsh and commanding. Now it was raised again, more incisive and distinct than before, and Marguerite had in truth some difficulty in repressing the cry that rose to her lips. She had recognised the man’s voice.

===Chinese===

“如今你害不了我了。我是在英格兰！”

玛格丽特探身窗外，想望进那正迅速拢上小巷的夜色里。声音正是从那边来的：先是男人的，继而女人的，如今又是男人的；两个说的都是法语，女人显然吓得魂不附体、声声哀告，男人则粗暴专横、句句命令。这时那男人的声音又扬起来，比先前更尖利、更分明，玛格丽特当真费了好大劲，才压下冲到唇边的惊呼。她听出了那个男人的嗓音。

===Original===

“Chauvelin!” she murmured.

“Aye, in England, citoyenne!” that ominous voice went on drily. “But the arm of justice is long. And remember that you are not the first who has tried﻿—unsuccessfully, let me tell you!﻿—to evade punishment by flying to the enemies of France. Wherever you may hide, I will know how to find you. Have I not found you here, now?﻿—and you but a few hours in Dover!”

===Chinese===

“肖夫兰（Chauvelin）！”她喃喃道。
