I have arranged with the landlord that you shall be left undisturbed in the small room where you found one another, and where the four walls will whisper to you of me. Good night, my beloved! Tomorrow you will go to London with the de Servals. I will follow later. It is better so. In London you will find me at the house of Mme. de Neafchâteau, a friend of my father’s who lives at No. 54 in the Soho Square, and who offered me hospitality in the days when I thought I might visit London for pleasure. She will receive me now that I am poor and an exile. Come to me there. Until then my heart will feed on the memory of your kiss.”

The letter was signed “Theresia.”

===Chinese===

“一位好心人向我动了恻隐之心，”信上这样写道，“客栈里没有我的床位了，她便让我在她的村舍里过夜，就在这附近什么地方。那究竟是什么地方，我也不知道。我已同店主讲妥，让你留在这间小屋里，不受搅扰——你们便是在这间屋里寻着彼此的，这里的四壁，会向你低声说起我。晚安，我亲爱的人！明天你随德·塞尔瓦尔（de Servals）一家去伦敦（London）。我随后就来。这样更好。到了伦敦，你到德·讷夫沙托夫人（Mme. de Neafchâteau）家里便能找到我——她是我父亲的朋友，住在苏豪广场（Soho Square）54 号；从前我还当自己会为着游乐到伦敦来一趟，她那时便请我上她家做客。如今我落魄了、流亡了，她也还是会收留我的。到那里来找我吧。在那之前，我的心，就以你那一吻的回忆为食粮。”

信的落款是“特蕾莎”。


===Original===

Bertrand pressed it time and again to his lips. Never in his wildest dreams had he hoped for this; never even in those early days of rapture had he tasted such perfect bliss. The letter he hid against his breast. He was immeasurably happy, felt as if he were treading on air. The sea, the landscape, no longer looked grey and dreary. This was England, the land of the free, the land wherein he had regained his beloved. Ah, the mysterious Scarlet Pimpernel, while seeking ignoble vengeance against her, for sins which she never had committed, did in truth render him and her a priceless service. Theresia, courted, adulated, over in Paris, had been as far removed from Bertrand Moncrif as the stars; but here, where she was poor and lonely, a homeless refugee like himself, she turned instinctively to the faithful lover, who would gladly die to ensure her happiness.

===Chinese===

贝特朗把它按在唇上，吻了一遍又一遍。他纵然做最狂野的梦，也不曾指望过这个；便在早先那些心醉神迷的日子里，他也从不曾尝过这样圆满的至福。他把信藏进了贴胸的衣襟。他幸福得没有边际，只觉得脚下像踩着云端。大海和四野的景色，不再显得灰暗凄凉。这就是英格兰（England），自由的土地，他重新赢得心上人的土地。啊，那位神秘的猩红石榴红（Scarlet Pimpernel），为着她从未犯下的罪过而向她寻那卑劣的报复，却当真为他和她两人做成了一件无价的功德。在巴黎（Paris）那边的时日，特蕾莎被人追求、被人奉承，同贝特朗·蒙克里夫之间隔着星辰一般遥远的距离；可是在这里，她贫穷、孤单，是个同他一样无家可归的流亡者，便本能地投向了这个忠实的情人——一个为保她幸福、甘愿赴死的情人。


===Original===

With that letter in his possession, Bertrand felt that he could not remain indoors. He was pining for open spaces, the sea, the mountains, God’s pure air﻿—the air which she too was breathing even now. He snatched up his hat and made his way out of the little building. The kitchen wench paused in her scrubbing and looked up smiling as he ran past her, singing and shouting for joy. For Régine﻿—the tender, loving heart that pined for him and for his love﻿—he had not a thought. She was the past, the dull, drabby past wherein he had dwelt before he knew how glorious a thing life could be, how golden the future, how rosy that horizon far away.

===Chinese===

怀里揣着这封信，贝特朗觉得自己再也待不住屋里了。他渴念着开阔的天地、大海、群山、上帝纯净的空气——这空气，此刻她不也在呼吸着么。他一把抓起帽子，出了那栋小屋。他从帮厨丫头身边跑过去时，又唱又喊、欢天喜地；丫头停下手里擦洗的活计，含笑抬眼望他。至于雷吉娜（Régine）——那颗为他、也为他的爱苦苦思恋着的温柔多情的心——他连一个念头也不曾分给她。她是过去，是那段阴沉暗淡的过去；他还不曾知道生命能何等辉煌、未来能何等金黄、那天边的地平线能何等绯红的时候，就一直住在那段过去里。


===Original===

By the time he reached the harbour, the sun had risen in all its glory. Way out against the translucent sky, the graceful silhouette of the schooner swayed gently in the morning breeze, her outspread sails gleaming like wings that are tinged with gold. Bertrand watched her for awhile. He thought of the mysterious Scarlet Pimpernel and the hideous vengeance which he had wrought against his beloved. And the rage which possessed his soul at the thought obscured for a moment the beauty of the morning and the glory of the sky. With a gesture characteristic of his blood and of his race, he raised his fist and shook it in the direction of the distant ship.

===Chinese===

等他到了港口，太阳已经升起，光华万丈。极远处，衬着澄澈半透的天幕，那艘纵帆船秀美的侧影在晨风里轻轻摇曳，张开的帆帆光闪耀，像染上了金色的翅膀。贝特朗望了它好一会儿。他想起那位神秘的猩红石榴红，想起他对他心上人施行的那场可憎的报复。一念及此，攫住他灵魂的怒火，一时间遮蔽了清晨的秀美与天空的壮丽。他做了一个他血脉与他种族所特有的手势，举起拳头，朝远处那艘船的方向狠狠挥了挥。

## XIX. A Rencontre / XIX. 不期而遇

### I / I

===Original===

For Marguerite, that wonderful May-day, like so many others equally happy and equally wonderful, came to an end all too soon. To dwell on those winged hours were but to record sorrow, anxiety, a passionately resentment coupled with an equally passionate acceptance of the inevitable. Her intimate friends often marvelled how Marguerite Blakeney bore the strain of these constantly recurring farewells. Every time that in the early dawn she twined her loving arms round the neck of the man she worshipped, feeling that mayhap she was looking into those dear, lazy, laughing eyes for the last time on earth﻿—every time, it seemed to her as if earth could not hold greater misery.

===Chinese===

对玛格丽特来说，那个美妙的五月天，同许许多多同样幸福、同样美妙的日子一样，结束得未免太快。细数那些插翅而飞的时辰，记录下来的无非是愁苦、是焦灼，是一腔激愤的怨怼，又缠着一腔同样炽烈的听天由命。她的闺中密友常常诧异，玛格丽特·布莱克尼（Marguerite Blakeney）怎么承受得住这一场接一场、周而复始的别离。每当破晓时分，她把恩爱的手臂缠上她所膜拜的男人的脖颈，心想这也许是今生今世最后一眼望进那双可爱的、慵懒含笑的眼睛——每一回，她都觉得人世间再盛不下比这更大的悲苦了。

===Original===

Then after that came that terrible half-hour, whilst she stood on the landing-stage﻿—his kisses still hot upon her lips, her eyes, her throat﻿—and watched and watched that tiny speck, that fast-sailing ship that bore him away on his errand of mercy and self-sacrifice, leaving her lonely and infinitely desolate. And then the days and hours, when he was away and it was her task to smile and laugh, to appear to know nothing of her husband save that he was a society butterfly, the pet of the salons, an exquisite, something of a fool, whose frequent absences were accounted for by deerstalking in Scotland or fishing in the Tweed, or hunting in the shires﻿—anything and everything that would throw dust in the eyes of the fashionable crowd of whom she and he formed an integral part.

===Chinese===

接下来便是那可怕的半个钟头：她站在栈桥上——他的吻还热烙在唇上、眼上、颈上——望了又望，望着那一个小点、那一艘疾驶的船，载着他奔赴那慈悲与自我牺牲的使命，撇下她孤零零一个，凄惶无边。再往后，便是他离去后的那些天、那些钟头：她的差事就是笑脸迎人、谈笑自若，装得对自己的丈夫一无所知，只当他是交际场上的一只花蝴蝶，沙龙里的宠儿，一个讲究入时的公子哥儿、有几分傻气；他频频的外出，自有苏格兰猎鹿、特威德河（the Tweed）垂钓、各郡猎场行猎之类的说法来搪塞——凡是能蒙住那班时髦人物眼睛的说辞，样样都使得上；而她和他，本就是那班人物里不可或缺的一分子。

===Original===

“Sir Percy not with you tonight, dear Lady Blakeney?”

“With me? Lud love you, no! I have not seen him these three weeks past.”

“The dog!”

===Chinese===

“今晚珀西爵士没陪您一起来么，亲爱的布莱克尼夫人？”

“陪着我？老天作证，才不呢！我都有三个礼拜没见着他了。”

“这个家伙！”

===Original===

People would talk and ask questions, throw out suggestions and innuendoes. Society a few months ago had been greatly agitated because the beautiful Lady Blakeney, the most fashionable woman about town, had taken a mad fancy for﻿—you’ll never believe it, my dear!﻿—for her own husband. She had him by her side at routs and river-parties, in her opera-box and on the Mall. It was positively indecent! Sir Percy was the pet of Society, his sallies, his inane laugh, his lazy, delicious, impertinent ways and his exquisite clothes, made the success of every salon in which he chose to appear. His Royal Highness was never so good-tempered as when Sir Percy was by his side. Then, for his own wife to monopolise him was preposterous, abnormal, extravagant! Some people put it down to foreign eccentricity; others to Lady Blakeney’s shrewdness in thus throwing dust in the eyes of her none-too-clever lord, in order to mask some intrigue or secret amour, of which Society had not as yet the key.

===Chinese===

人们总要议论纷纷、问长问短，抛出种种揣测与含沙射影。几个月前，社交界还为此大为骚动：美丽的布莱克尼夫人——全城最时髦的女人——竟突发痴念，迷上了——亲爱的，您说什么也不会相信！——迷上了她自己的丈夫。夜会上、河畔游会上，歌剧院的包厢里、林荫大道（the Mall）上，她总把他带在身边。这简直有伤风化！珀西爵士本是社交界的宠儿，他的俏皮妙语、他的痴傻笑声、他那慵懒而讨喜的放诞做派、他精美绝伦的衣装，成就了每一处他肯赏光的沙龙。亲王殿下（His Royal Highness）也从没有像珀西爵士侍立身侧时那样好脾气。既然如此，他自己的太太偏要把他独占，未免荒唐、反常、出格！有人把这归之于外国的怪脾气；也有人归之于布莱克尼夫人的精明——她就这样蒙住她那位不甚灵光的夫君的眼睛，好遮掩某桩密约或私情，而其中的底细，社交界至今还没拿到钥匙。

===Original===

Fortunately for the feelings of the fashionable world, this phase of conjugal affection did not last long. It had been at its height last year, and had waned perceptibly since. Of late, so it was averred, Sir Percy was hardly ever at home, and his appearances at Blakeney Manor﻿—his beautiful house at Richmond﻿—were both infrequent and brief. He had evidently tired of playing second fiddle to his exquisite wife, or been irritated by her caustic wit, which she was wont to sharpen at his expense; and the ménage of these two leaders of fashion had, in the opinion of those in the know, once more resumed a more normal aspect.

When Lady Blakeney was in Richmond, London or Bath, Sir Percy was shooting or fishing or yachting﻿—which was just as it should be. And when he appeared in society, smiling, elegant, always an exquisite, Lady Blakeney would scarce notice him, save for making him a butt for her lively tongue.

===Chinese===

总算顾全了时髦世界的体面，这股夫妻恩爱的风潮没刮多久。去年它正当极盛，此后便明显地消退了。据称，近来珀西爵士难得在家，他驾临布莱克尼庄园（Blakeney Manor）——他坐落在里士满（Richmond）的美丽宅邸——的次数既少，逗留也短。他显然是厌倦了给他那精致绝伦的夫人当配角，要么就是叫她的刻薄机智惹恼了——她惯于拿他作磨刀石，把口齿磨得锋利；在知情者们看来，这对时尚界领袖的家庭生活（ménage），便又恢复了一副较为正常的样子。

布莱克尼夫人住在里士满、伦敦（London）或巴斯（Bath）的时候，珀西爵士便去打猎、钓鱼或驾帆船出海——这也正合体统。而当他出现在社交场上，笑吟吟、风度翩翩、一如既往地讲究入时，布莱克尼夫人却难得看他一眼，只拿他当自己伶牙俐齿取笑的靶子。

### II / II

===Original===

What it cost Marguerite to keep up this role none but a very few ever knew. The identity of one of the greatest heroes of this or any time was known to his most bitter enemy﻿—not to his friends. So Marguerite went on smiling, joking, flirting, while her heart ached and her brain was at times well-nigh numb with anxiety. His intimates rallied round her, of course: the splendid little band of heroes who formed the League of the Scarlet Pimpernel﻿—Sir Andrew Ffoulkes and his pretty wife; Lord Anthony Dewhurst and his lady, whose great dark eyes still wore the impress of the tragedy which had darkened the first month of her happy wedded life. Then there was my lord Hastings; and Sir Evan Cruche, the young Squire of Holt, and all the others.

===Chinese===

把这台戏撑下去，玛格丽特付出了怎样的代价，只有极少数人知道。古往今来最伟大的英雄之一，他的真面目，他最刻毒的死敌知道——他的朋友们反倒不知道。于是玛格丽特继续笑着、打趣着、逢场作戏地调笑着，心里却在疼痛，脑子有时焦灼得近乎麻木。他的心腹密友们自然聚拢在她身边：那组成猩红石榴红（Scarlet Pimpernel）同盟（the League）的一小队了不起的英雄——安德鲁·福尔克斯爵士（Sir Andrew Ffoulkes）和他美丽的妻子；安东尼·杜赫斯特勋爵（Lord Anthony Dewhurst）和他的夫人，她那双乌黑的大眼睛里，至今印着那段惨剧的痕迹——正是它，给她新婚头一个月的幸福生活蒙上了阴翳。此外还有黑斯廷斯勋爵（Hastings）；有埃文·克鲁奇爵士（Sir Evan Cruche）——年轻的霍尔特（Holt）乡绅；还有其余诸人。

===Original===

And for the Prince of Wales, it is more than surmised by those competent to judge that His Royal Highness did indeed guess at the identity of the Scarlet Pimpernel, even if he had not actually been apprised of it. Certain it is that his tact and discretion did on more than one occasion save a situation which might have proved embarrassing for Marguerite.

In all these friends then﻿—in their conversation, their happy laughter, their splendid pluck and equally splendid gaiety, the echo of the chief whom they adored﻿—Marguerite found just the solace that she needed. With Lady Ffoulkes and Lady Anthony Dewhurst she had everything in common. With those members of the League who happened to be in England, she could talk over and in her mind trace the various stages of the perilous adventure on which her beloved and the others were even then engaged.

===Chinese===

至于威尔士亲王（the Prince of Wales），有资格评判的人们几乎可以断定：纵使不曾有人把真相正式告知，殿下也确实猜到了猩红石榴红是谁。可以肯定的是，不止一回，多亏他的机变与审慎，才把一些本可能叫玛格丽特难堪的局面化解了过去。

正是在这一班朋友身上——在他们的谈笑、他们快活的笑声、他们了不起的胆气与同样了不起的欢快里，在他们所崇敬的那位“首领”的回响里——玛格丽特找到了她正需要的慰藉。同福尔克斯夫人和杜赫斯特勋爵夫人，她有着说不尽的相通之处。同那些恰好身在英格兰的同盟成员，她可以把那桩凶险历险的各个阶段细细谈论、在心里逐一描画——她心爱的人和其他人，此刻正投身其中。

===Original===

And there were always the memories of those all too brief days at Dover or in Richmond, when her loving heart tasted such perfect happiness as is granted only to the elect: the happiness that comes from perfect love, perfect altruism, a complete understanding and measureless sympathy. On those memories her hungering soul could subsist in the intervals, and with them as her unalienable property, she could even bid the grim spectre of unhappiness begone.

===Chinese===

还有那些回忆——在多佛尔（Dover）或里士满度过的、何其短促的时日——那时她这颗深情的心，尝到了唯有天之骄子才配得到的完美幸福：由完美的爱、完美的无私、全然的理解与无量的同情凝成的幸福。靠着这些回忆，她饥渴的灵魂便能在别离的间隙里维生；把它们当作自己不可剥夺的财产，她甚至能把不幸那狰狞的鬼影喝退。

### III / III

===Original===

Of Madame de Fontenay﻿—for as such Marguerite still knew her﻿—she saw but little. Whether the beautiful Theresia had gone to London or no, whether she had succeeded in finding her truant husband, Marguerite did not know and cared less. The unaccountable antipathy which she had felt on that first night of her acquaintance with the lovely Spaniard still caused her to hold herself aloof. Sir Percy, true to his word, had not betrayed the actual identity of Theresia Cabarrus to his wife; but in his light, insouciant manner had dropped a word or two of warning, which had sharpened Marguerite’s suspicions and strengthened her determination to avoid Mme. de Fontenay as far as possible. And since monetary or other material help was apparently not required, she had no reason to resume an intercourse which, in point of fact, was not courted by Theresia either.

===Chinese===

至于德·丰特奈夫人（Madame de Fontenay）——玛格丽特至今仍只以这个名字识得她——她是难得见上一面的。美丽的特蕾莎去了伦敦没有，寻着她那负心出走的丈夫没有，玛格丽特既不知道，也懒得多想。初识那位可爱的西班牙女子的头一晚，她心里生出的那股没来由的反感，至今仍叫她敬而远之。珀西爵士言而有信，不曾向妻子泄露特蕾莎·卡巴吕斯（Theresia Cabarrus）的真实身份；只是以他那副轻描淡写、漫不经心的做派，零星丢下过一两句警语，把玛格丽特的疑心磨得更利了，也叫她打定主意，能避德·丰特奈夫人多远就避多远。何况对方显然并不要金钱或其他物质上的帮助，她也就没有理由重续这段往来——说实在的，特蕾莎也无意于此。

===Original===

But one day, walking alone in Richmond Park, she came face to face with Theresia. It was a beautiful late afternoon in July, the end of a day which had been a comparatively happy one for Marguerite﻿—the day when a courier had come from France with news of Sir Percy; a letter from him, telling her that he was well and hinting at the possibility of another of those glorious days together at Dover.

With that message from her beloved just to hand, Marguerite had felt utterly unable to fulfill her social engagements in London. There was nothing of any importance that claimed her presence. His Royal Highness was at Brighton; the opera and the rout at Lady Portarles’ could well get on without her. The evening promised to be more than ordinarily beautiful, with a radiant sunset and the soft, sweet-scented air of a midsummer’s evening.

===Chinese===

不料有一天，她独自在里士满公园（Richmond Park）散步，竟同特蕾莎撞了个照面。那是七月里一个美丽的午后向晚时分，那一天对玛格丽特来说算是比较快活的——就在那天，一位信使从法兰西捎来了珀西爵士的消息；一封他的亲笔信，说他安好无恙，还暗示说，兴许又能共度一个多佛尔那样的光辉日子。

心上人的书信方才到手，玛格丽特只觉得全然打不起精神，无心去应伦敦的那些社交之约。没有什么要紧场合非她到场不可。亲王殿下去了布莱顿（Brighton）；歌剧院和波塔莱斯夫人（Lady Portarles）家的夜会，缺了她也照样进行。这个傍晚眼见得格外美丽，落霞灿烂，仲夏夜的空气温柔而香甜。

===Original===

After dinner, Marguerite had felt tempted to stroll out alone. She threw a shawl over her head and stepped out on to the terrace. The vista of velvet lawns, of shady paths and rose borders in full bloom, stretched out into the dim distance before her; and beyond these, the boundary wall, ivy-clad, overhung with stately limes, and broken into by the finely wrought iron gates that gave straight into the Park.

The shades of evening were beginning to draw in, and the garden was assuming that subtle veil of mysterious melancholy which perfect beauty always lends. In the stately elms far away, a blackbird was whistling his evensong. The night was full of sweet odours﻿—roses and heliotrope, lime and mignonette﻿—whilst just below the terrace a bed of white tobacco swung ghostlike its perfumed censer into the air. Just an evening to lure a lonely soul into the open, away from the indifferent, the casual, into the heart of nature, always potent enough to soothe and to console.

===Chinese===

晚饭后，玛格丽特动了独自出门走走的心思。她往头上拢了一方披巾，步出到露台上。天鹅绒般的草坪、浓荫的小径和盛放的玫瑰花境，在她眼前铺展开去，延伸进朦胧的远处；再往外，是一道围墙，爬满常春藤，覆着高高的椴树，铸工精美的铁门把它破开，径直通进公园里去。

暮色开始四合，园子正蒙上那一层精微的面纱——绝美的景致总会借来的一层神秘忧郁。远处高贵的榆树间，一只乌鸫啭着它的晚祷曲。夜里盛满了甜香——玫瑰与天芥菜，椴花与木樨草——露台正下方，一畦白烟草花像幽灵似的，把芬芳的香炉摇进空气里。这样的夜晚，正引诱一颗孤寂的灵魂走出门去，离开那些冷淡的、漫不经心的人们，投入造化的怀抱——造化永远有足够的伟力，抚慰人心，宽解人意。

### IV / IV

===Original===

Marguerite strolled through the grounds with a light foot, and anon reached the monumental gates, through which the exquisite peace and leafy solitude of the Park seemed to beckon insistently to her. The gate was on the latch; she slipped through and struck down a woodland path bordered by tangled undergrowth and tall bracken, and thus reached the pond, when suddenly she perceived Mme. de Fontenay.

===Chinese===

玛格丽特迈着轻快的步子穿过园地，不多时便到了那座巍峨的大门前，门里公园那精微的宁静与绿叶幽寂，仿佛在执拗地向她招手。门只是虚掩着；她侧身溜了进去，踏上一条林间小径，两侧蔓生着纠结的灌木和高高的蕨草，就这样走到了池塘边——冷不防，她望见了德·丰特奈夫人。

===Original===

Theresia was dressed in a clinging gown of diaphanous black silk, which gave value to the exquisite creamy whiteness of her skin and to the vivid crimson of her lips. She wore a transparent shawl round her shoulders, which with the new-modish, high-waisted effect of her gown, suited her sinuous grace to perfection. But she wore no jewellery, no ornaments of any kind: only a magnificent red rose at her breast.

The sight of her at this place and at this hour was so unexpected that, to Marguerite’s super-sensitive intuition, the appearance of this beautiful woman, strolling listless and alone beside the water’s edge, seemed like a presage of evil. Her first instinct had been to run away before Mme. de Fontenay was aware of her presence; but the next moment she chided herself for this childish cowardice, and stood her ground, waiting for the other woman to draw near.

===Chinese===

特蕾莎穿一袭紧贴身形的黑纱长裙，轻薄透明，衬得她肌肤那精致的乳白与唇上那鲜明的绛红愈加夺目。她肩上围一条轻纱披肩，配上长裙那高腰的时新款型，把她婀娜的风致衬托到了十足。只是她不戴珠宝，不佩任何饰物：唯有胸前一朵艳丽的红玫瑰。

在这个地方、这个时辰见到她，实在太出意料，以至于在玛格丽特那敏感过人的直觉里，这位美丽的女子恹恹地、孤零零地沿着水边踱步的身影，竟像是一个不祥之兆。她的第一个本能，是在德·丰特奈夫人察觉之前赶紧走开；可下一刻她又责备自己这份孩子气的怯懦，索性立定不动，等那女人走近前来。

===Original===

A minute or two later, Theresia had looked up and in her turn had perceived Marguerite. She did not seem surprised, rather came forward with a glad little cry, and her two hands outstretched.

“Milady!” she exclaimed. “Ah, I see you at last! I have oft wondered why we never met.”

===Chinese===

一两分钟后，特蕾莎抬起头来，也望见了玛格丽特。她似乎并不惊讶，反倒欢欢喜喜地轻呼一声，迎上前来，伸出双手。

“夫人！”她唤道。“啊，总算见着您了！我一直纳闷，咱们怎么从来不曾遇上。”

===Original===

Marguerite took her hands, greeted her as warmly as she could. Indeed she did her best to appear interested and sympathetic.

Mme. de Fontenay had not much to relate. She had found refuge in the French convent of the Assumption at Twickenham, where the Mother Superior had been an intimate friend of her mother’s in the happy olden days. She went out very little, and never in society. But she was fond of strolling in this beautiful Park. The sisters had told her that Lady Blakeney’s beautiful house was quite near. She would have liked to call﻿—but never dared﻿—hoping for a chance rencontre which hitherto had never come.

===Chinese===

玛格丽特握住她的双手，尽了最大的热忱招呼她。她确实竭力装出饶有兴味、满怀同情的样子。

德·丰特奈夫人没有什么可细说的。她在特威克纳姆（Twickenham）的法国圣母升天修女院里寻得了栖身之所，那里的院长嬷嬷，在从前的好时光里，是她母亲的一位闺中密友。她很少出门，也从不去社交场。只爱在这座美丽的公园里漫步。修女们告诉过她，布莱克尼夫人的美丽宅邸就在近旁。她本想登门拜访——却始终不敢——只盼着一场偶然的不期而遇（rencontre），而这场不期而遇，至今不曾来过。

===Original===

She asked kindly after milor, and seemed to have heard a rumour that he was at Brighton, in attendance on his royal friend. Of her husband, Mme. de Fontenay had as yet found no trace. He must be living under an assumed name, she thought﻿—not doubt in dire poverty﻿—Theresia feared it, but did not know﻿—would give worlds to find out.

Then she asked Lady Blakeney whether she knew aught of the de Servals.

“I was so interested in them,” she said, “because I had heard something of them while I was in Paris, and seeing that we arrived in England the same day, though under such different circumstances. But we could not journey to London together, as you, milady, so kindly suggested, because I was very ill the next day﻿ ﻿… Ah, can you wonder?﻿ ﻿… A kind friend in Dover took care of me. But I remember their name, and have oft marvelled if we should ever meet.”

===Chinese===

她亲切地问起爵爷（milor）的近况，像是听到了一点风声，说他正在布莱顿，侍奉在他的王室朋友左右。至于她的丈夫，德·丰特奈夫人至今没有寻到一丝踪迹。他想必正顶着化名度日——多半还穷得厉害——特蕾莎怕是这样，却无从确知——为了弄个明白，她什么代价都肯付。

随后她问布莱克尼夫人，可知道些德·塞尔瓦尔（de Serval）一家的近况。

“我对他们一家实在关切得很，”她说，“因为在巴黎那会儿，我就听到过一些他们的事，何况我们又是同一天踏上英格兰的——虽说境遇是那样天差地别。只是没能照您好心的提议，一道结伴去伦敦，因为第二天我就病得不轻……唉，这还用奇怪么？……多佛尔一位好心的朋友照看了我。可我记得他们家的姓氏，还常常暗自纳闷，不知此生可还有相见之期。”

===Original===

Yes; Marguerite did see the de Servals from time to time. They rented a small cottage not very far from here﻿—just outside of town. One of the daughters, Régine, was employed all day at the fashionable dressmaker’s in Richmond. The younger girl, Joséphine, and the boy, Jacques, was doing work in a notary’s office. It was all very dreary for them, but their courage was marvellous; and though the children did not earn much, it was sufficient for their wants.

Madame de Fontenay was vastly interested. She hoped that Régine’s marriage with the man of her choice would bring a ray of real happiness into the household.

===Chinese===

是的；玛格丽特倒时不时见得到德·塞尔瓦尔一家。他们在离这儿不远的地方租了一座小屋——就在镇外。大女儿雷吉娜（Régine）整日在里士满一家时兴的裁缝铺里做工。小女儿约瑟芬（Joséphine）和男孩雅克（Jacques）在一家公证人事务所里当差。这一切对他们来说未免凄清，可他们的勇气令人惊叹；孩子们挣得虽不多，倒也够用了。

德·丰特奈夫人听了极感兴趣。她盼望雷吉娜同她选中的那人成婚，能给这个家添上一缕真正的幸福之光。

===Original===

“I hope so too,” Lady Blakeney assented.

“Milady has seen the young man﻿—Régine’s fiancé?”

“Oh, yes! once or twice. But he is engaged in business all day, it seems. He is inclined to be morbid and none too full of ardour. It is a pity; for Régine is a sweet girl and deserves happiness.”

===Chinese===

“我也这样盼着，”布莱克尼夫人应道。

“夫人见过那位年轻人——雷吉娜的未婚夫么？”

“哦，见过！见过一两回。不过他似乎整天忙于营生。人有些阴郁，热络劲儿也欠着些。可惜了；雷吉娜是个可爱的姑娘，配得上幸福。”

===Original===

“We have so much sorrow in common,” she said with a pathetic smile. “So many misfortunes. We ought to be friends.”

Then she gave a slight little shiver.

“The weather is extraordinarily cold for July,” she said. “Ah, how one misses the glorious sunshine of France!”

===Chinese===

“我们有这么多共同的苦难，”她带着一丝凄婉的微笑说。“这么多的不幸。我们本该做朋友的。”

接着她轻轻打了个寒噤。

“这样的七月天，真是冷得出奇，”她说。“唉，怎能不想念法兰西那灿烂的阳光！”

===Original===

She wrapped her thin, transparent shawl closer round her shoulders. She was delicate, she explained. Always had been. She was a child of the South, and fully expected the English climate would kill her. In any case, it was foolish of her to stand thus talking, when it was so cold.

After which she took her leave, with a gracious inclination of the head and a cordial au revoir. Then she turned off into a small path under the trees, cut through the growing bracken; and Marguerite watched the graceful figure thoughtfully, until the leafy undergrowth hid her from view.


===Chinese===

她把那条单薄的轻纱披肩往肩上裹紧了些。她身子娇弱，她解释道，从来就是如此。她是南方之女，料定英格兰的气候早晚会要了她的命。无论如何，天气这么冷，她还这样站着说话，未免太傻了。

说罢她便告辞了，优雅地颔一颔首，亲切地道了声再见（au revoir）。随后她折进树下一方小径，从渐渐长高的蕨草间穿了过去；玛格丽特若有所思地目送着那婀娜的身影，直到枝叶蔓密的灌木丛把她遮没为止。

## XX. Departure / XX. 启程

### I / I

===Original===

The next morning’s sun rose more radiant than before. Marguerite greeted it with a sigh that was entirely a happy one. Another round of the clock had brought her a little nearer to the time when she would see her beloved. The next courier might indeed bring a message naming the very day when she could rest once more in his arms for a few brief hours, which were so like the foretaste of heaven.

===Chinese===

第二天早晨，太阳升起来，比先前更加灿烂。玛格丽特（Marguerite）迎着它叹了口气，那一声叹息里全是幸福。时钟又转过一轮，把她朝见到心上人的时辰又带近了一程。下一班信使，说不定真能捎来一封信，把那个日子明白定下——到那时，她又能在他怀抱里安歇几个短暂的钟头；那样的钟头，简直就像天堂滋味的预尝。

===Original===

Soon after breakfast she ordered her coach, intending to go to London in order to visit Lady Ffoulkes and give Sir Andrew the message which was contained for him in Percy’s last letter. Whilst waiting for the coach, she strolled out into the garden, which was gay with roses and blue larkspur, sweet william and heliotrope, alive with a deafening chorus of blackbirds and thrushes, the twittering of sparrows and the last call of the cuckoo. It was a garden brimful of memories, filled in rich abundance with the image of the man she worship. Every birdsong seemed to speak his name, the soughing of the breeze amidst the trees seemed to hold the echo of his voice; the perfume of thyme and mignonette to bring back the savour of his kiss.

===Chinese===

早餐过后不久，她吩咐备车，打算到伦敦（London）去拜访福尔克斯夫人（Lady Ffoulkes），并把珀西（Percy）最后一封信里捎给安德鲁·福尔克斯爵士（Sir Andrew Ffoulkes）的口信转达给他。候车的当儿，她踱进花园。园子里玫瑰开得正欢，还有蓝色的飞燕草、美洲石竹和天芥菜；黑鸫和画眉放声合唱，唱得震耳欲聋，麻雀啁啾不休，杜鹃送出今夏最后几声啼唤，满园一派生机。这座花园盛满了回忆，处处丰盈地映着她所崇拜的那个人的身影。每一声鸟语仿佛都在念他的名字，微风穿过树丛的飒飒声，仿佛藏着他嗓音的回响；百里香和木樨草的芬芳，仿佛又把他一吻的滋味带回她唇边。

===Original===

Then suddenly she became aware of hurrying footsteps on the gravelled path close by. She turned, and saw a young man whom at first she did not recognise, running with breathless haste towards her. He was hatless, his linen crumpled, his coat-collar awry. At sight of her he gave a queer cry of excitement and relief.

“Lady Blakeney! Thank God! Thank God!”

Then she recognised him. It was Bertrand Moncrif.

===Chinese===

突然，她察觉到近旁砂砾小径上响起一阵急促的脚步声。她转过身，只见一个年轻人朝她奔来，跑得连气都喘不上，起初她没认出是谁。他光着头，衬衣皱作一团，外套领子歪在一边。一望见她，他发出一声古怪的喊叫，又是激动，又如释重负。

“布莱克尼夫人（Lady Blakeney）！谢天谢地！谢天谢地！”

这下她认出他了。是贝特朗·蒙克里夫（Bertrand Moncrif）。

===Original===

He fell on his knees and seized her gown. He appeared entirely overwrought, unbalanced, and Marguerite tried in vain at first to get a coherent word out of him. All that he kept on repeating was:

“Will you help me? Will you help us all?”

===Chinese===

他扑通跪倒，一把抓住她的裙裾。他整个人看去完全失了常态、乱了方寸，玛格丽特起先怎么也没能从他嘴里问出一句囫囵话来。他翻来覆去只是念叨：

“您肯帮我吗？您肯帮我们大家吗？”

===Original===

“Indeed I will, if I can, M. Moncrif,” Marguerite said gently. “Do try and compose yourself and tell me what is amiss.”

She persuaded him to rise, and presently to follow her to a garden seat, where she sat down. He remained standing in front of her. His eyes still looked wild and scared, and he passed a shaking hand once or twice through his unruly hair. But he was obviously making an effort to compose himself, and after a little while, during which Marguerite waited with utmost patience, he began more coherently:

===Chinese===

“只要办得到，我自然肯帮，蒙克里夫先生，”玛格丽特温声说。“千万定一定神，告诉我出了什么乱子。”

她说服他站起身，又劝他随自己走到园中一张座椅旁，她在椅上坐了。他依旧站在她面前。他眼里还带着狂乱惊惶的神色，一两次抬起发颤的手，插进那头不驯的乱发里。可他分明正竭力定神；玛格丽特耐着顶大的性子又等了一小会儿，他总算开口说得有条理了些：

===Original===

“Your servants said, milady,” he began more quietly, “that you were in the garden. I could not wait until they called you, so I ran to find you. Will you try and forgive me? I ought not to have intruded.”

“Of course I will forgive you,” Marguerite rejoined with a smile, “if you will only tell me what is amiss.”

===Chinese===

“您府上的仆人说，夫人，”他起头说得平静了些，“说您正在花园里。我等不得他们去请您，便自己跑着来找您了。请您千万原谅。我原不该这样唐突闯进来。”

“我自然原谅你，”玛格丽特含笑应道，“只要你先把出了什么乱子讲给我听。”

===Original===

He paused a moment, then cried abruptly:

“Régine has gone!”

Marguerite frowned, puzzled, and murmured slowly, not understanding:

“Gone? Whither?”

“To Dover,” he replied, “with Jacques.”

“Jacques?” she reiterated, still uncomprehending.

“Her brother,” he rejoined. “You know the boy?”

Marguerite nodded.

===Chinese===

他顿了片刻，突然脱口喊道：

“雷吉娜（Régine）走了！”

玛格丽特蹙起眉头，满心疑惑，还不明白就里，慢慢喃喃道：

“走了？上哪儿去了？”

“去多佛尔（Dover）了，”他答道，“同雅克（Jacques）一道。”

“雅克？”她又问了一遍，仍旧不得要领。

“她弟弟，”他应道。“那孩子您认得的？”

玛格丽特点了点头。

===Original===

“Hotheaded, impulsive,” Moncrif went on, trying to speak calmly. “He and the girl Joséphine always had it in their minds that they were destined to liberate France from her present state of anarchy and bloodshed.”

“Like you yourself, M. Moncrif!” Marguerite put in with a smile.

“Oh, I became sobered, reasonable, when I realised how futile it all was. We all owe our lives to that noble Scarlet Pimpernel. They were no longer ours to throw away. At least, that was my theory, and Régine’s. I have been engaged in business; and she works hard﻿ ﻿… Oh, but you know!” he exclaimed impulsively.

“Yes, I know all your circumstances. But to the point, I pray you!”

===Chinese===

“性子火爆，行事鲁莽，”蒙克里夫接着说，竭力把话说得平稳。“他和约瑟芬（Joséphine）那姑娘两个，心里一向存着一个念头，认定他们命中注定，要把法兰西从如今这遍地无政府、遍地流血的境地里解救出来。”

“就像你自己从前一样，蒙克里夫先生！”玛格丽特含笑插了一句。

“哦，等我明白这一切何其徒劳，我就清醒了、懂道理了。我们这几条性命，全亏那位高贵的猩红石榴红（Scarlet Pimpernel）才保下来，再不由得我们抛掷了。至少，我和雷吉娜抱的是这个想法。我如今经营着一桩营生；她呢，做工做得辛苦……哦，反正这些您都知道！”他按捺不住地喊道。

“是，你们各人的境况我全知道。可是求你了，说正题吧！”

===Original===

“Jacques of late has been very excited, feverish. We did not know what was amiss. Régine and I oft spoke of him. And Mme. de Serval has been distraught with anxiety. She worships the boy. He is her only son. But Jacques would not say what was amiss. He spoke to no one. Went to his work every day as usual. Last night he did not come home. A message came for Mme. de Serval to say that a friend in London had persuaded him to go to the play and spend the night with him. Mme. de Serval thought nothing of that. She was pleased to think that Jacques had some amusement to distract him from his brooding thoughts. But Régine, it seems, was not satisfied. After her mother had gone to bed, she went into Jacque’s room; found some papers, it seems﻿ ﻿… letters﻿ ﻿… I know not﻿ ﻿… proof in fact that the boy was even then on his way to Dover, having made arrangements to take ship for France.”

“*Mon Dieu!*” Marguerite exclaimed involuntarily. “What senseless folly!”

“Ah! but that is not the worst. Folly, you say! But there is worse folly still!”

===Chinese===

“雅克近来一直亢奋得很，像害着热病。我们不知道他到底怎么了。雷吉娜跟我时常说起他。德·塞尔瓦尔夫人（Mme. de Serval）忧急得几乎神魂失常。她把那孩子爱得像命根子。他是她独一的儿子。可雅克什么都不肯说，跟谁也不搭话，每天照旧去上工。昨天夜里他没有回家。有人给德·塞尔瓦尔夫人捎来口信，说伦敦有位朋友劝他去看戏，就在人家家里过了夜。德·塞尔瓦尔夫人一点没起疑。她反倒高兴，想着雅克总算有了个消遣，好把他从愁思苦想里引开去。可雷吉娜呢，看样子并不放心。等她母亲睡下了，她进了雅克的房间；似乎翻出了些什么纸页……几封信……我也说不清……总之是凭据，证明那孩子当时已经在去多佛尔的路上，连搭船去法兰西的安排都已办妥了。”

“我的上帝（*Mon Dieu*）！”玛格丽特不由自主地失声喊道。“这是何等没来由的胡闹！”

“啊！这还算不得最坏的呢。您说胡闹！还有更坏的胡闹呢！”

===Original===

With the same febrile movements that characterised his whole attitude, he drew a stained and crumpled letter from his pocket.

“She sent me this, this morning,” he said. “That is why I came to you.”

“You mean Régine?” Marguerite asked, and took the letter which he was handing to her.

“Yes! She must have brought it round herself﻿ ﻿… to my lodgings﻿ ﻿… in the early dawn. I did not know what to do﻿ ﻿… whom to consult﻿ ﻿… A blind instinct brought me here﻿ ﻿… I have no other friend﻿ ﻿…”

In the meanwhile Marguerite was deciphering the letter, turning a deaf ear to his ramblings.

===Chinese===

他带着那股燥热慌乱的动作——他周身的姿态无不打着这股烙印——从口袋里掏出一封污迹斑斑、皱皱巴巴的信。

“今天早上她把这个送给了我，”他说。“我所以才来求您。”

“你是说雷吉娜？”玛格丽特问，接过他递上来的信。

“是！这信准是她亲自送来的……送到我的寓所……在天蒙蒙亮的时分。我不知道该怎么办……该去请教谁……一股盲目的本能把我引到了您这里……我此外再没有别的朋友了……”

这当儿玛格丽特正低头辨读那封信，对他的絮絮叨叨只作不闻。

===Original===

“My Bertrand,” so the letter ran, “Jacques is going to France. Nothing will keep him back. He says it is his duty. I think that he is mad, and I know that it will kill *maman*. So I go with him. Perhaps at the last﻿—at Dover﻿—my tears and entreaties might yet prevail. If not, and he puts this senseless project in execution, I can watch over him there, and perhaps save him from too glaring a folly. We go by coach to Dover, which starts in an hour’s time. Farewell, my beloved, and forgive me for causing you the anxiety; but I feel that Jacques has more need of me than you.”

Below the signature “Régine de Serval” there were a few more lines, written as if with an afterthought:

===Chinese===

“我的贝特朗，”信上这样写道，“雅克要去法兰西了。什么也拦他不住。他说这是他的本分。我想他是疯了，我也知道，这会要了妈妈（*maman*）的命。所以我随他去。也许到了最后一刻——到了多佛尔——我的眼泪和苦求还能挽回。倘若不能，他执意要把这桩糊涂透顶的打算付诸实行，我也能在那边看护他，也许能救他，不叫他闯出太扎眼的祸事。我们搭驿车去多佛尔，车过一个钟头就开。别了，我的爱人，饶恕我害你担这番惊怕；可我觉得，如今雅克比我更需要我。”

在“雷吉娜·德·塞尔瓦尔”的署名底下，还有寥寥几行字，像是落笔之后又想起补上的：

===Original===

“I have told *maman* that my employer is sending me down into the country about some dresses for an important customer, and that as Jacques can get a few days’ leave from his work, I am taking him with me, for I feel the country air would do him good.

“*Maman* will be astonished and no doubt hurt that Jacques did not send her word of farewell, but it is best that she should not learn the truth too suddenly. If we do not return to Dover within the week, you will have to break the news as gently as you can.”

===Chinese===

“我告诉了妈妈，说是东家派我下乡去，替一位要紧的主顾料理几件衣裳；又说雅克能跟做工的地方告下几天假，我便带他一道去，因为我觉得乡下的空气于他有益。

“妈妈见雅克临行连一句道别的话也没给她捎，准会又惊又怪，心里没有不痛的；可总好过让她过猛地知道实情。倘若一礼拜之内我们回不到多佛尔，那就只得劳你把这消息尽量委婉地说给她听了。”

===Original===

Whilst Marguerite read the letter, Bertrand had sunk upon the seat and buried his head in his hands. He looked utterly dejected and forlorn, and she felt a twinge of remorse at thought how she had been wronging him all this while by doubting his love for Régine. She placed a kindly hand on the young man’s shoulder.

“What was your idea,” she asked, “in coming to me? What can I do?”

===Chinese===

玛格丽特读信的当儿，贝特朗已瘫坐在座椅里，双手把脸深深埋住。他那副神情，颓丧孤苦到了极处；她心头掠过一阵悔疚——这一向她总疑心他对雷吉娜的情意，竟是这般一直错怪了他。她把一只温存的手搭在年轻人肩上。

“你上我这儿来，”她问道，“打的是什么主意？我又能做些什么？”

===Original===

“Give me advice, milady!” he implored. “I am so helpless, so friendless. When I had the letter, I could think of nothing at first. You see, Régine and Jacques started early this morning, by the coach from London, long before I had it. I thought you could tell me what to do, how to overtake them. Régine loves me﻿—oh, she loves me! If I knelt at her feet I could bring her back. But they are marked people, those two. The moment they attempt to enter Paris, they will be recognised, arrested. Oh, my God! have mercy on us all!”

“You think you can persuade Régine, M. Moncrif?”

“I am sure,” he asserted firmly. “And you, milady! Régine thinks the whole world of you!”

“But there is the boy﻿—Jacques!”

“He is just a child﻿—he acted on impulse﻿—and I always had great authority over him. And you, milady! The whole family worship you!﻿ ﻿… they know what they owe you. Jacques has not thought of his mother; but if he did﻿—”

===Chinese===

“给我出个主意吧，夫人！”他哀告道。“我这样无靠无依，这样孤零零没个亲友。刚拿到这封信时，我起初什么主意都想不出来。您想，雷吉娜和雅克是今天一大早从伦敦搭驿车动的身，早在我收到信之前许久许久。我想着，您或许能指点我该怎么办、怎样追上他们。雷吉娜是爱我的——唉，她是爱我的！只要我跪在她脚前，我准能把她劝回来。可他们那两个，是黑名单上挂了号的人。他们一试图踏进巴黎，就会教人认出来、抓起来。哦，我的上帝！怜悯怜悯我们大家吧！”

“你觉得自己劝得动雷吉娜么，蒙克里夫先生？”

“我笃定的，”他斩钉截铁地断言。“还有您呢，夫人！雷吉娜把您瞧得比全世界都重！”

“可还有那孩子——雅克！”

“他到底是个孩子——一时的冲动——我一向管他管得住。再有您呢，夫人！他们一家人把您敬得跟神明一样！……他们知道自己欠您多少。雅克没有想到他母亲；可他若是想到了——”

===Original===

Marguerite rose without another word.

“Very well,” she said simply. “We go together and see what we can do with those two obstinate young folk.”

Bertrand gave a gasp of surprise and of hope. His whole face lighted up and he gazed upon the beautiful woman before him as a worshipper would on his divinity.

“You, milady?” he murmured. “You would﻿ ﻿… really﻿ ﻿… help me﻿ ﻿… like that?”

===Chinese===

玛格丽特一言不发，站起身来。

“好吧，”她简简单单地说。“我们就一道去，看看能拿这两个犟脾气的年轻人怎么办。”

贝特朗又惊又盼，倒抽了一口气。他整张脸豁然亮了起来，望着眼前这位美丽的夫人，那目光恰似信徒仰望他的神明。

“您，夫人？”他喃喃道。“您竟肯……当真……这样……帮我？”

===Original===

Marguerite smiled.

“I really would help you like that,” she said. “My coach is ordered; we can start at once. We’ll get relays at Maidstone and at Ashford, and easily reach Dover tonight, before the arrival of the public coach. In any case, I know every one of any importance in Dover. We could not fail to find the runaways.”

“But you are an angel, milady!” Bertrand contrived to stammer, although obviously he was overwhelemed with gratitude.

“You are ready to start?” Marguerite retorted, gently checking any further display of emotion.

===Chinese===

玛格丽特莞尔一笑。

“我当真肯这样帮你，”她说。“我的马车已经吩咐下去了；咱们立刻便可动身。到梅德斯通（Maidstone）和阿什福德（Ashford）再换驿马，今晚轻轻松松赶在公共驿车到站之前抵达多佛尔。再说，多佛尔城里稍有头脸的人，我没有一个不熟。那两个私逃的人，断没有找不着的道理。”

“您真是一位天使，夫人！”贝特朗好容易才结结巴巴吐出这一句，显然他已感激得不知如何是好。

“你这就能动身么？”玛格丽特应了一声，温和地拦住了他感情的再流露。

===Original===

He certainly was hatless, and his clothes were in an untidy condition; but such trifles mattered nothing at a moment like this. Marguerite’s household, on the other hand, were accustomed to these sudden vagaries and departures of their mistress, either for Dover, Bath, or any known and unknown destination, often at a few minutes’ notice.

In this case the coach was actually at the gates. The maids packed the necessary valise; her ladyship changed her smart gown for a dark travelling one, and less than half an hour after Bertrand Moncrif’s first arrival at the Manor, he was searted beside Lady Blakeney in her coach. The coachman cracked his whip, the postilion swung himself into the saddle, and the servants stood at attention as the vehicle slowly swung out of the gates; and presently, the horses putting on the pace, disappeared along the road, followed by a cloud of dust.

===Chinese===

他确实还光着头，一身衣裳也还是那副凌乱相；可在这样的时刻，这些区区小事算不得什么。倒是玛格丽特府上的人，对他们女主人这种突发的奇兴与出行，早就习以为常——去多佛尔也好，去巴斯（Bath）也好，去一切知名不知名的去处，往往只提前几分钟传下话来。

这一回，马车竟已经停在大门口了。女仆们收拾起必备的旅行提包；夫人把那身漂亮的花梢裙衫换成一件深色行装；贝特朗·蒙克里夫初到布莱克尼庄园（the Manor）还不到半个钟头，人已经坐进布莱克尼夫人的马车、坐在她身旁了。车夫把鞭子甩得一声脆响，骑马引车的驭者翻身跨上马鞍，仆人们肃立两旁，目送马车缓缓驶出大门；不多时马匹放开蹄步，车子扬起一路尘烟，顺着大路消失不见了。

### II / II

===Original===

Bertrand Moncrif, brooding, absorbed in thoughts, said little or nothing while the coach swung along at a very brisk pace. Marguerite, who always had plenty to think about, did not feel in the mood to try and make conversation. She was very sorry for the young man, who in very truth must have suffered also from remorse. His lack of ardour﻿—obviously only an outward lack﻿—toward his fiancée and the members of her family, must to a certain extent have helped to precipitate the present catastrophe. Coolness and moroseness on his part gave rise to want of confidence on the other. Régine, heartsick at her lover’s seeming indifference, was no doubt all the more ready to lavish love and self-sacrifice upon the young brother. Marguerite was sorry enough for the latter﻿—a young fool, with the exalté Latin temperament, brimming over with desires for self-immolation as futile as they were senseless﻿—but her generous heart went out to Régine de Serval, a girl who appeared redestined to sorrow and disappointments, endowed with an exceptionally warm nature and cursed with the inability to draw wholehearted affection to herself. She worshipped Bertrand Moncrif; she idolised her mother, her brother, her sister. But though they, one and all, relied on her, brought her the confidences of their troubles and their difficulties, it never occurred to any one of them to give up something﻿—a distraction, a fancy, an ideal﻿—for the sake of silent, thoughtful Régine.

===Chinese===

马车一路轻快地辚辚疾驰，贝特朗·蒙克里夫兀自出神，满腹心事，几乎一言不发。玛格丽特素来便有想不完的心事，此刻也无意寻话攀谈。她很为这年轻人难过——说实在的，他心里想必还受着悔恨的煎熬。他对未婚妻和她一家人的那份冷淡——明摆着只是表面上的冷淡——多多少少，只怕正是催成眼下这场祸事的根苗。他这边冷淡阴郁，那边便生出疑隙。雷吉娜眼见情人貌似薄情，伤心失意，自然愈发把满腔的爱与舍己之心，尽数倾泻到年幼的弟弟身上。玛格丽特为那弟弟也够惋惜的——一个年轻的傻子，一副热烈奔放的拉丁脾性，满腔满腹都涌着舍身效死的渴望，那份渴望既属徒劳，又属无谓——可她那颗宽厚的心，却更向着雷吉娜·德·塞尔瓦尔（Régine de Serval）：这姑娘仿佛命里注定要饱尝忧愁与失望，天性温热得出奇，偏偏又像中了咒似的，怎么也换不来别人全心全意的爱。她崇拜贝特朗·蒙克里夫；她把母亲、弟弟、妹妹当偶像般敬爱着。可是他们一家人，尽管个个倚重她，有了烦恼为难都来向她倾吐，却从没有哪一个人想起，为这个不出声、事事细心的雷吉娜，舍弃掉一些什么——一场消遣也好，一桩癖好也好，一个理想也好。

===Original===

Marguerite allowed her thoughts thus to dwell on these people, whom her husband’s splendid sacrifice on their behalf had rendered dear. Indeed, she loved them like she loved so many others, because of the dangers which he had braved for their sakes. Their lives had become valuable because of his precious one, daily risked because of them. And at the back of her mind there was also the certainty that if these two young fools did put their mad project in execution and endeavoured to return to Paris, it would again be the gallant Scarlet Pimpernel who would jeopardise his life to save them from the consequences of their own folly.

===Chinese===

玛格丽特任凭自己的思绪这样流连在这几个人身上——她丈夫为他们做出的辉煌牺牲，早已叫他们成了她心尖上挂念的人。说实在的，她爱他们，正如她爱许许多多别的人一样，正因为他曾为他们涉过那些险。他们这些性命之所以贵重，正系于他那条宝贵的性命——那条为他们日日犯险的性命。而在她心底，还存着一层笃定：倘若这两个年轻的傻子当真把他们那疯狂的图谋付诸实行、当真设法折返巴黎，那么，再一次冒着性命之险、把他们从自身蠢行的后果里搭救出来的，必定又是那位豪侠的猩红石榴红。

### III / III

===Original===

Luncheon and a brief halt was taken at Farningham and Maidstone reached by three o’clock in the afternoon. Here Lady Blakeney’s own servants took leave of her, and post-horses were engaged to take her ladyship on to Ashford. Two hours later, at Ashford, fresh relays were obtained. The public coach at this hour was only some nine or ten miles ahead, it seems, and there was now every chance that Dover would be reached by nightfall and the young runaways met by their pursuers on arrival.

===Chinese===

一行人在法宁厄姆（Farningham）用了午餐、略作歇息，下午三点钟抵达梅德斯通。在这里，布莱克尼夫人自家的仆人向她辞别下去，另雇了驿马，送夫人继续上路前往阿什福德。两个钟头之后，到了阿什福德，又换上一批新驿马。看样子，公共驿车此刻也不过走在前头八九英里、十英里的地方；如今大有希望在入夜之前赶抵多佛尔，叫那两个私逃的年轻人一下车，便被追赶他们的人截个正着。

===Original===

All was then for the best. Bertrand, after the coach had rattled out of Ashford, appeared to find comfort and courage. He began to talk, long and earnestly﻿—of himself, his plans and projects, his love for Régine, to which he always found it so difficult to give expression; of Régine herself and the de Servals, mother, son and daughters. His voice was toneless and very even. The monotony of his diction acted after awhile as a soporific on Marguerite’s nerves. The rumble of the coach, the closeness of this long afternoon in July, the rocking of the springs, made her feel drowsy. After a while took, a curious scent pervaded the interior of the coach﻿—a sweet, heady scent that appeared to weigh her eyelids down and gave her a feeling of delicious and lazy beatitude. Bertrand Moncrif droned on, and his voice came to her fast-fading senses as through a thick pulpy veil. She closed her eyes. That sweet, intoxicating scent came, more marked, more insistent, to her nostrils. She laid her head against the cushions, and still she heard the dreary monotone of Bertrand’s voice, quite inarticulate now, like the hum of a swarm of bees﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

于是一切都再顺遂不过。马车辚辚驶出阿什福德之后，贝特朗像是寻回了安慰，也寻回了勇气。他开始说话了，说得又长又恳切——说他自己，说他的种种计划和打算，说他对雷吉娜的那份爱——那份爱，他向来觉得难以宣之于口；又说雷吉娜本人，说德·塞尔瓦尔一家，母亲、儿子和女儿们。他的嗓音平板沉闷，匀匀缓缓。这单调的语调，过了一阵，竟像一剂催眠药，作用在玛格丽特的神经上。马车的辚辚声，七月午后这一路长行的窒闷，弹簧的颠动摇晃，都催得她昏昏欲睡。过了一会儿，又有一股古怪的气味在车厢里弥漫开来——一股甜腻冲脑的香味，仿佛直往下坠她的眼皮，给了她一种又惬意、又懒洋洋的至福之感。贝特朗·蒙克里夫絮絮不休，他的声音传到她渐次消散的知觉里，仿佛隔着一层又厚又绵的纱幕。她阖上了眼睛。那股甜醉的香气，愈发分明、愈发执拗地钻进她的鼻端。她把头靠上软垫，耳朵里依然是贝特朗那乏味的单调语声，此刻已全然辨不出字句，只像一窝蜂群的嗡嗡……

===Original===

Then, all of a sudden she was fully conscious; only just in time to feel the weight of an iron hand against her mouth and to see Bertrand’s face, ghastly of hue, eyes distorted more with fear than rage, quite close to her own. She had not the time to scream, and her limbs felt as heavy as lead, so that she could not struggle. The next moment a thick woollen scarf was wound quickly and tightly round her head, covering her mouth and eyes, only barely giving her room to breathe, and her hands and arms were tied together with cords.

===Chinese===

随后，她陡然间彻底清醒过来——恰恰来得及觉出一只铁钳般的手掌死死压上她的嘴，看清贝特朗的那张脸：血色尽褪，煞白如灰，一双眼睛与其说瞪着怒火，不如说瞪满了恐惧，正紧贴着她自己的脸。她连喊一声的工夫都没有，四肢沉得像灌了铅，挣扎不得。下一刻，一条厚厚的毛线围巾飞快地、紧紧地缠上她的头，蒙住她的嘴和眼睛，只勉强留出一线喘息的空隙；她的双手和双臂，也被绳索捆作了一处。

===Original===

This brutal assault had been so quick and so sudden that at first it seemed to Marguerite like part of a hideous dream. She was not fully conscious, and was half suffocated by the thick folds of the scarf and that persistent odour, which by its sickened sweetness caused her well-nigh to swoon.

===Chinese===

这场暴行来得这般迅疾、这般突兀，起初在玛格丽特感觉里，竟像是一场狰狞噩梦的一截。她神智并不全清，教围巾的厚褶和那股挥之不去的气味闷得半窒息——那气味甜得令人作呕，熏得她险些晕厥过去。

===Original===

Through this semiconsciousness, however, she was constantly aware of her enemy, Bertrand Moncrif﻿—the black-hearted traitor who had carried out this execrable outrage: why and for what purpose, Marguerite was too dazed to attempt to guess. He was there, that she knew. She was conscious of his hands making sure of the cords round her wrists, tightening the scarf around her mouth; then presently she felt him leaning across her body and throwing down the window, and she heard him shouting to the driver:

===Chinese===

然而透过这半昏半醒，她始终觉察着她的仇人贝特朗·蒙克里夫——那个黑心肠的叛徒，正是他下手做下了这桩天怒人怨的暴行：为着什么、图着什么，玛格丽特昏昏沉沉，连猜也无从猜起。他在跟前，这一点她知道。她觉出他的双手在查验她腕上的绳索，把围巾在她嘴上又收紧几分；随后她又觉出他俯身横过她的身子，摇下了车窗，听见他朝车夫高声喊道：

===Original===

“Her ladyship has fainted. Drive as fast as ever you can till you come to that white house yonder on the right, the one with the green shutters and the tall yew at the gate!”

===Chinese===

“夫人晕过去了。拼着你最大的脚力跑，一直跑到右边远处那座白房子跟前——绿百叶窗、大门口一株高高紫杉的就是！”

===Original===

The driver’s reply she could not hear, nor the crack of his whip. Certain it is that, though the coach had rattled on at a great pace before, the horses, as if in response to Bertrand’s commands, now burned the ground under their hoofs. A few minutes went by﻿—an eternity. Then that terrible cloying perfume was again held close to her nostrils; an awful dizziness and nausea seized her; after which she remembered nothing more.


===Chinese===

车夫应了句什么，她听不见，也没有听见鞭花的脆响。千真万确的是：马车先前虽已辚辚疾驰，此刻那些马匹竟像应着贝特朗的号令一般，蹄下像着了火、烧着地皮一般飞奔。几分钟过去了——长得像一个永恒。随后，那股可怕的、甜得腻人的香水又一次紧紧凑上她的鼻端；一阵剧烈的眩晕与恶心攫住了她；此后的事，她再也不记得了。

## XXI. Memories / XXI. 追忆

### I / I

===Original===

When Marguerite Blakeney finally recovered consciousness, the sun was low down in the west. She was in a coach﻿—not her own﻿—which was being whisked along the road at terrific speed. She was alone, her mouth gagged, her wrists and her ankles tied with cords, so that she could neither speak nor move﻿—a helpless log, being taken﻿ ﻿… whither?﻿ ﻿… and by whom?

===Chinese===

玛格丽特·布莱克尼（Marguerite Blakeney）终于恢复知觉的时候，太阳已经西垂，落得低低的了。她身在一辆马车里——不是她自己的那辆——车子正以骇人的速度沿着大路飞驰。她孤身一人，嘴里堵着东西，手腕和脚踝都捆着绳索，说不出话，也动弹不得——一段任人摆布的木头，正被载着……载往何方？……又落在谁的手里？


===Original===

Bertrand was not here. Through the front window of the coach she could perceive the vague outline of two men sitting on the driver’s seat, whilst another was riding the off-leader. Four horses were harnessed to the light coach. It flew along in a southeasterly direction, the while the shades of evening were fast drawing in.

===Chinese===

贝特朗（Bertrand）不在这里。透过马车的前窗，她能辨出坐在车夫座上那两个人的模糊轮廓，另有一个则骑在右前头的梢马上。四匹马套着这辆轻便马车，朝东南方向一路飞驰，而暮色正急速地围拢下来。


===Original===

Marguerite had seen too much of the cruelties and barbarities of this world, too much of the hatred that existed between enemy countries, and too much of the bitter rancour felt by certain men against her husband and indirectly against herself, not to realise at once whence the blow had come that had struck her. Something too in the shape of that back which she perceived through the window in front of her, something in the cut of the threadbare coat, the set of the black bow at the nape of the neck, was too familiar to leave her even for a moment in doubt. Here was no ordinary footpad, no daring abduction with a view to reward or ransom. This was the work of her husband’s enemies, who, through her, were once more striving to get at him.

===Chinese===

玛格丽特见过的世间残忍与暴虐太多了，见过的交战敌国之间的仇恨太多了，某些人对她丈夫、又连带着对她本人怀有的那种刻骨怨毒，她也见得太多了——所以她立刻就明白，这一击来自何方。还有，透过面前的车窗她望见的那副背影的模样，那件磨得露线的旧外套的剪裁，后颈上黑蝴蝶结的系法，也都熟悉得叫她连片刻的疑惑都不曾有。这不是寻常的拦路剪径之贼，也不是为了勒取赎金酬赏而干的胆大绑票。这是她丈夫的仇敌们干下的勾当——他们要借着她，再一次去够到他。


===Original===

Bertrand Moncrif had been the decoy. Whence had come the hatred which prompted him to raise his hand against the very man to whom he owed his life, Marguerite was still too dazed to conjecture. He had gone, and taken his secret of rancour with him, mayhap forever. Lying pinioned and helpless as she was, Marguerite had but the one thought: in what way would those fiends who had her a prisoner use her as a leverage against the life and honour of the Scarlet Pimpernel? They had held her once before﻿—not so very long ago﻿—in Boulogne, and he had emerged unscathed, victorious over them all.

===Chinese===

贝特朗·蒙克里夫（Bertrand Moncrif）就是那诱饵。那股仇恨究竟从何而来，竟驱使他对自己的救命恩人举起手来——玛格丽特还昏沉得很，无从揣度。他走了，把他那腔怨毒的秘密随身带走了，说不定是永远带走了。玛格丽特被捆着躺在那里，全然无助，心里只转着一个念头：那些把她握在掌中的恶魔，要怎样拿她作筹码，去撬动猩红石榴红（the Scarlet Pimpernel）的性命与名誉？他们先前也扣押过她一回——就在不算许久以前——在布洛涅（Boulogne），而他毫发无伤地脱了身，把他们尽数斗败了。


===Original===

Marguerite, helpless and pinioned, forced her thoughts to dwell on that time, when his enemies had filled to the brim the cup of humiliation and of dread which was destined to reach him through her hands, and his ingenuity and his daring dashed the cup to the ground ere it reached her lips. In truth, her plight then, at Boulogne, was in no way less terrible, less seemingly hopeless than now. She was a prisoner then, just as she was now; in the power of men whose whole life and entire range of thought had for the past two years been devoted to the undoing and annihilation of the Scarlet Pimpernel. And there was a certain grim satisfaction for the pinioned, helpless woman in recalling the many instances where the daring adventurer had so completely outwitted his enemies, as well as in the memory of those days at Boulogne when the life of countless innocents was to be the price of her own.

===Chinese===

玛格丽特无助地、被捆着，却强逼自己的思绪流连到那个时候：那时，他的仇敌们把那杯羞辱与惊怖斟得满满，那杯酒本注定要经由她的手送到他面前，而他的机变与胆魄，还不等杯子递到她唇边，便已把它摔翻在地。说实在的，她当年在布洛涅的处境，丝毫不比此刻少了凶险，也不比此刻少了看似无望的绝境。她那时是囚徒，正同此刻一样；落在一伙人手里——两年来，这伙人的整副性命、全部的心思，都扑在搞垮猩红石榴红、将他斩尽杀绝上头。而这个被捆着、无助的女人，回想那大胆的冒险家一次次把仇敌们彻底斗败的种种往事，回想在布洛涅的那些日子——那时，无数无辜者的性命，要做她一人性命的抵偿——心底竟有一种冷峻的快意。



### II / II

===Original===

The embarkation took place somewhere on the coast around Birchington. When, at dead of night, the coach came to a halt, and the tang of sea air and salt spray reached Marguerite’s burning cheeks and parched lips, she tried with all her might to guess at her exact position. But that was impossible.

===Chinese===

登船的地方，在伯钦顿（Birchington）一带海岸的某处。万籁俱寂的深夜，马车停了下来，咸冽的海风夹着飞溅的盐沫，扑上玛格丽特滚烫的双颊和干焦的嘴唇。她竭尽全力，想猜出自己确切的方位。可那是办不到的。


===Original===

She was lifted out of the coach, and at once a shawl was thrown over her face, so that she could not see. It was more instinct than anything else that guided her perceptions. Even in the coach she had been vaguely conscious of the direction in which she had been travelling. All that part of the country was entirely familiar to her. So often she had driven down with Sir Percy, either to Dover or more often to some lonely part of the coast, where he took ship for unknown destinations, that in her mind she could, even blinded with tears and half-conscious as she was, trace in her mind the various turnings and side-roads along which she was being borne at unabating speed.

===Chinese===

她被人从马车里抬了出来，随即一条披巾兜头罩下，遮住了她的脸，叫她什么也看不见。为她引路的，与其说是别的什么，不如说是本能。即便在马车里，她对自己一路行进的方向，也始终存着蒙眬的知觉。那一带的乡野，她熟得不能再熟。她随珀西爵士（Sir Percy）驱车南下过多少回啊——不是去多佛尔（Dover），便是更多时候去海岸上某处僻静的所在，他从那里登船出海，驶向不知名的目的地——因此，纵然她泪眼迷离、神智半昏，心里仍能把载着她一路疾驰、片刻不停的那一道道拐弯、一条条岔路，一一描画出来。


===Original===

Birchington﻿—one of the favourite haunts of the smuggling fraternity, with its numberless caves and retreats dug by the sea in the chalk cliffs, as if for the express benefit of ne’er-do-wells﻿—seemed the natural objective of the miscreants who had her in their power. In fact, at one moment she was quite sure that the square tower of old Minister church flitted past her vision through the window of the coach, and that the horses immediately after that sprinted the hill between Minster and Acoll.

===Chinese===

伯钦顿——走私行帮最中意的落脚地之一，大海在白垩峭壁间掏出的那数不清的岩洞与藏身之所，仿佛专为那些不肖之徒备下——在擒住她的这伙恶徒看来，似乎正是顺理成章的去处。事实上，有一刹那她十分确信，古老的明斯特教堂（Minster）那方形的塔楼，曾掠过车窗、映过她的眼帘，随后马匹便撒开四蹄，冲上了明斯特与埃科尔（Acol）之间的那道山坡。


===Original===

Be that as it may, there was no doubt that the coach came to a halt at a desolate spot. The day which had begun in radiance and sunshine, had turned to an evening of squall and drizzle. A thin rain soon wetted Marguerite’s clothes and the shawl on her head through and through, greatly adding to her misery and discomfort. Though she saw nothing, she could trace every landmark of the calvary to the summit of which she was being borne like an insentient log.

===Chinese===

无论怎样，马车停在一处荒凉冷僻的所在，这一点是毫无疑问的了。这一日清晨还光华灿烂、艳阳满目，到入暮竟变作了狂风裹着斜雨的天气。蒙蒙细雨很快把玛格丽特的衣裳、连同她头上的披巾浸了个透湿，把她的凄苦与难受又添了几分。她虽然什么也看不见，可这条苦路（calvary）上的每一处地标，她都能一一辨明——她正像一段无知无觉的木头，被人抬着，抬向那苦路的顶巅。


===Original===

For a while she lay at the bottom of a small boat, aching in body as well as in mind, her eyes closed, her limbs cramped by the cords which owing to the damp were cutting into her flesh, faint with cold and want of food, wet to the skin yet with eyes and head and hands burning hot, and her ears filled with the dreary, monotonous sound of the oars creaking in the rowlocks and the boom of the water against the sides of the boat.

===Chinese===

有一阵子，她躺在一艘小船的船底，身子痛楚，心里更痛楚；双眼阖着；四肢叫绳索捆得抽紧麻木——绳子受了潮气，正勒进她的皮肉；她又冷又饿，虚弱得直欲晕去；浑身湿透，眼睛、脑袋和双手却烧得滚烫；耳朵里灌满了单调凄凉的声响：桨在桨架里吱呀，海水撞着船帮轰轰发闷。


===Original===

She was lifted out of the boat and carried, as she judged, by two men up a companion ladder, then down some steps and finally deposited on some hard boards; after which the wet shawl was removed from her face. She was in the dark. Only a tiny streak of light found its way through a chink somewhere close to the floor. A smell of tar and of stale food gave her a wretched sense of nausea. But she had by now reached a stage of physical and mental prostration wherein even acute bodily suffering counts as nothing, and is endurable because it is no longer felt.

===Chinese===

她被从船里抬出来，据她判断，是让两个男人抬着上了一道舷梯，又下了几级台阶，最后被放到几块硬木板上；随后，那条湿透的披巾从她脸上揭了下来。她身处黑暗之中。只有一线细细的微光，从贴近地板的什么缝隙里钻了进来。一股柏油味混着馊饭的气味，熏得她一阵阵恶心欲呕。可她此刻身心都已委顿到了一个地步——在那地步里，连剧烈的肉体苦痛也算不得什么；熬得住，只因早已感觉不到了。


===Original===

After a while the familiar motion, the well-known sound of a ship weighing anchor, gave another blow to her few lingering hopes. Every movement of the ship now bore her farther and farther from England and home, and rendered her position more utterly miserable and hopeless.

===Chinese===

过了片刻，那熟悉的摇荡，那船只起锚时再熟识不过的声响，又朝她残存无几的希望击了一记。船身每挪动一下，便把她载得离英格兰（England）和家远一分，也把她的处境变得更凄惨、更绝望一分。


===Original===

Far be it from me to suggest even for a moment that Marguerite Blakeney lost either spirit or courage during this terrible ordeal. But she was so completely helpless that instinct forced her to remain motionless and quiescent, and not to engage in a fight against overwhelming odds. In mid-Channel, surrounded by miscreants who had her in their power, she could obviously do nothing except safeguard what dignity she could by silence and seeming acquiescence.

===Chinese===

我万万无意暗示，玛格丽特·布莱克尼在这场可怕的磨难中丧过心气、失过勇气——哪怕一瞬也不曾。只是她实在全然无助，本能驱策着她一动不动、蛰伏隐忍，不去同那压倒一切的强敌较量。在海峡中央，四面围着她的是把她攥在掌中的恶徒，她显然什么也做不成，唯有以沉默和表面上的顺从，守住自己还能守住的那一点尊严。



### III / III

===Original===

She was taken ashore in the early dawn, at a spot not very far from Boulogne. Precautions were no longer taken against her possible calls for help; even the cords had been removed from her wrists and ankles as soon as she was lowered into the boat that brought her to shore. Cramped and stiff though she was, she disdained the help of an arm which was held out to her to enable her to step out of the boat.

===Chinese===

破晓时分，她被送上了岸，登岸的地点离布洛涅不远。他们不再防范她可能的呼救；她刚一被放进送她上岸的小船，腕上踝上的绳索便都解了去。她尽管又麻又僵，却不屑去扶那只伸过来搀她下船的胳膊。


===Original===

All the faces around her were unfamiliar. There were four or five men, surly and silent, who piloted her over the rocks and cliffs and then along the sands, to the little hamlet of Wimereux, which she knew well. The coast at this hour was still deserted; only at one time did the little party meet with a group of buxom young women, trudging along barefooted with their shrimping nets over their shoulders. They stared wide-eyed but otherwise indifferent, at the unfortunate woman in torn, damp clothes, and with golden hair all dishevelled, who was bravely striving not to fall whilst urged on by five rough fellows in ragged jerseys, tattered breeches, and bare-kneed.

===Chinese===

她四周全是陌生的面孔。四五个汉子，阴郁而沉默，领着她翻过礁石与崖壁，又沿着沙滩，一路走到维默罗（Wimereux）那座她熟识的小渔村。这个时辰，海岸上依然渺无人迹；只有一回，这一小队人遇上了一伙丰润健壮的年轻女子，赤着脚、肩上扛着捕虾网，一路跋涉而行。她们睁圆了眼睛，望着这个不幸的女人——衣衫破碎，湿漉漉贴在身上，一头金发纷乱披散，被五个身穿破旧针织衫、褴褛短裤、赤着膝头的粗汉催赶着，兀自竭力撑持、不肯跌倒——可她们也不过是望一望罢了，旁的一概无动于衷。


===Original===

Just for one moment﻿—a mere flash﻿—Marguerite at sight of these girls had the wild notion to run to them, implore their assistance in the name of their sweethearts, their husbands, their songs; to throw herself at their feet and beg them to help her, seeing that they were women and could not be without heart or pity. But it was a mere flash, the wild vagary of an overexcited brain, the drifting straw that mocks the drowning man. The next moment the girls had gone by, laughing and chattering. One of them intoned the “Ça ira!” and Marguerite, fortunately for her own dignity, was not seriously tempted to essay so futile, so senseless an appeal.

===Chinese===

只在那么一瞬——电光石火似的一瞬——玛格丽特一望见这些姑娘，心里便生出一个狂乱的念头：向她们奔过去，凭着她们的心上人、她们的丈夫、她们的歌，恳求她们搭救；扑倒在她们脚前，求她们帮一帮她——念在她们也是女人，总不至于没有心肝、没有怜悯。可那不过是一瞬，是一颗过度亢奋的头脑生出的狂想，是那根漂来漂去、偏要嘲弄溺水者的稻草。转瞬间，姑娘们已经说说笑笑地走了过去。其中一个哼起了《Ça ira!》（“就这样干！”）。幸而——为了她自己的体面——玛格丽特并没有当真受到蛊惑，去作那样徒劳、那样无谓的哀告。


===Original===

Later on, in a squalid little hovel on the outskirts of Wimereux, she was at last given some food which, though of the poorest and roughest description, was nevertheless welcome, for it revived her spirit and strengthened her courage, of which she had sore need.

===Chinese===

再往后，在维默罗村边一间污秽的小破屋里，她总算得了一些吃食。那东西虽然粗劣已极，却依然受欢迎，因为它让她精神一振、勇气一壮——而这份勇气，正是她万分需要的。


===Original===

The rest of the journey was uneventful. Within the first hour of making a fresh start, she had realised that she was being taken to Paris. A few words dropped casually by the men who had charge of her apprised her of the fact. Otherwise they were very reticent﻿—not altogether rough or unkind.

===Chinese===

余下的旅程再无波折。重新上路后头一个钟头里，她便明白了：自己正被押往巴黎（Paris）。看守她的汉子们不经意间漏出的只言片语，向她透露了这一事实。除此之外，他们都缄口不言——倒也并不怎么粗横，也不怎么刻薄。


===Original===

The coach in which she travelled during this stage of the journey was roomy and not uncomfortable, although the cushions were ragged and the leatherwork mildewed. Above all, she had the supreme comfort of privacy. She was alone in the coach, alone during the halts at wayside hostelries when she was allowed food and rest, alone throughout those two interminable nights when, with brief intervals whilst relays of horses were put into the shafts or the men took it in turns to get food or drink in some house unseen in the darkness, she vainly tried to get a snatch or two of sleep and a few moments of forgetfulness; alone throughout that next long day, whilst frequent summer showers sent heavy raindrops beating against the windowpanes of the coach, and familiar landmarks on the way to Paris flitted like threatening ghouls past her aching eyes.

===Chinese===

旅程这一段乘坐的马车，车厢宽敞，坐着也并不难受，虽说座垫已经破烂，皮件上也生了霉斑。最要紧的是，她享有一种至高的安逸：无人搅扰。她独自待在车厢里；在路边客栈歇脚、蒙准进食歇息的时候，独自一人；在那两个没完没了的漫漫长夜里，独自一人——其间只有短短的间歇，或换班的驿马套上车辕，或汉子们轮番摸黑到哪所瞧不见的屋子里去弄点吃喝——她徒然想抓得一两觉浅眠、偷得片刻的忘却；在随后那个漫长的白昼里，仍是独自一人——一阵阵夏日骤雨把沉甸甸的雨点打在车窗玻璃上，通往巴黎一路那些熟悉的地标，像一个个含着凶兆的食尸鬼，从她酸痛的双眼前一晃而过。


===Original===

Paris was reached at dawn of the third day. Seventy-two hours had crept along leaden-footed, since the moment when she had stepped into her own coach outside her beautiful home in Richmond, surrounded by her own servants, and with that traitor Moncrif by her side. Since then, what a load of sorrow, of anxiety, of physical and mental suffering had she borne! And yet, even that sorrow, even those sufferings and that anxiety, seemed as nothing beside the heartrending thoughts of her beloved, as yet ignorant of her terrible fate and of the schemes which those fiends who had so shamefully trapped her were even now concocting for the realisation of their vengeance against him.

===Chinese===

第三天拂晓，一行人抵达了巴黎。自从那一刻——她在里士满（Richmond）美丽宅邸的门外，被自家的仆人们簇拥着、身边伴着那个叛徒蒙克里夫，跨进自己马车的那一刻——七十二个钟头已经拖着铅铸的脚，一步一步爬了过去。打那以后，她担起了怎样一副重担啊——悲哀、忧惧、身心交迫的苦楚！然而，纵是那悲哀、纵是那些苦楚与忧惧，比起她对心上人肝肠寸断的悬想，也算不得什么了——他还一无所知，既不知她可怕的命运，也不知那伙无耻设局陷害她的恶魔，此刻正密谋着何等毒计，要借着她，把对他的报复做成真章。

## XXII. Waiting / XXII. 等待

### I / I

===Original===

The house to which Marguerite was ultimately driven, and where she presently found herself ushered up the stairs into a small, well-furnished apartment, appeared to be situated somewhere in an outlying quarter of Paris.

The apartment consisted of three rooms﻿—a bedroom, a sitting-room, and small *cabinet de toilette*﻿—all plainly but nicely furnished. The bed looked clean and comfortable, there was a carpet on the floor, one or two pictures on the wall, an armchair or two, even a few books in an armoire. An old woman, dour of mien but otherwise willing and attentive, did all she could to minister to the poor wearied woman’s wants. She brought up some warm milk and home-baked bread. Butter, she explained, was not obtainable these day, and the household had not seen sugar for weeks.

===Chinese===

玛格丽特（Marguerite）最终被送到的那座房子——她随即便由人引着上了楼，走进一套小巧而陈设精良的公寓——看方位，是坐落在巴黎（Paris）某个偏僻的街区。

这套公寓由三间屋子组成——一间卧室、一间起居室，还有一间小小的梳妆间（*cabinet de toilette*）——全都布置得朴素而雅致。床铺看着干净舒适，地上铺着地毯，墙上挂着一两幅画，还摆着一两把扶手椅，衣柜里甚至搁着几本书。一个老妇人，面色阴沉，除此以外倒还尽心周到，竭力照料这可怜的、疲惫已极的女人的种种需要。她端来了温热的牛奶和自家烤的面包。黄油，她解释说，如今是弄不到的了；这户人家，也已经好几个星期没见过糖了。

===Original===

Marguerite, tired out and hungry, readily ate some breakfast; but what she longed for most and needed most was rest. So presently, at the gruff invitation of the old woman, she undressed and stretched her weary limbs between the sheets, with a sigh of content. Anxiety, for the moment, had to yield to the sense of well-being, and with the name of her beloved on her lips Marguerite went to sleep like a child.

===Chinese===

玛格丽特困乏已极，又觉着饿，便痛快地用了些早餐；可她最渴望、最需要的，还是休息。于是不多时，应着老妇人粗声粗气的一句招呼，她宽了衣，把困乏的四肢舒展进被单之间，满足地叹了口气。忧惧一时只得让位给这份安适；唇边念着心上人的名字，玛格丽特像个孩子似的睡着了。

===Original===

When she woke, it was late afternoon. On a chair close by her bedside was some clean linen laid out, a change of stockings, clean shoes, and a gown﻿—a perfect luxury, which made this silent and lonely house appear more like the enchanted abode of ogres or fairies than before. Marguerite rose and dressed. The linen was fine, obviously the property of a woman of refinement, whilst everything in the tiny dressing-room﻿—a comb, hand-mirror, soap, and scented water﻿—suggested that the delicate hand of a cultured woman had seen to their disposal. A while later, the dour attendant brought her some soup and a dish of cooked vegetables.

===Chinese===

她醒来时，已是午后很晚的时分。床边近旁的椅子上，端端正正摆着干净的贴身衣衫、一副换洗的袜子、干净的鞋，还有一件长裙——真是十足的奢侈，叫这座寂静冷清的宅子，愈发像妖魔或仙子们施过魔法的洞府了。玛格丽特起身穿衣。那亚麻衣衫质地精细，显然是一位雅致女子的东西；而小小梳妆间里的一切——一把梳子、一面手镜、香皂，还有香花水——都表明有一只有教养的女子的纤手，替它们一一安置妥帖。过了一会儿，那面色阴沉的侍妇又给她端来了汤，还有一盘煮蔬菜。

===Original===

Every phase of the situation became more and more puzzling as time went one. Marguerite, with the sense of well-being further accentuated by the feel of warm, dry clothes and of wholesome food, had her mind free enough to think and to ponder. She had thrown open the window, and peeping out, noted that it obviously gave on the back of the house and that the view consisted of rough, uncultivated land, broken up here and there by workshops, warehouses, and timber-yards. Marguerite also noted that she was gazing out in the direction of the northwest, that the apartment wherein she found herself was on the top floor of a detached house which, judging by certain landmarks vaguely familiar, was situated somewhere outside the barrier of St. Antoine, and not very far from the Bastille and from the Arsenal.

===Chinese===

时日推移，这处境的每一层都愈发教人猜不透。玛格丽特身上是温暖干燥的衣裳，腹中是滋养的饭食，那份安适之感愈发深浓，头脑也就腾出足够的空闲，任她思前想后。她推开了窗，向外窥望，看出这窗分明朝着屋后，入眼是一片粗砺的未垦之地，零零星星夹着些作坊、货栈和木料场。玛格丽特还留意到，自己凭窗眺望的方向是西北；她置身的这套公寓，在一座独栋宅子的顶层——凭几处依稀眼熟的地标判断，这宅子坐落在圣安托万关卡（barrier of St. Antoine）外面的什么地方，离巴士底狱（the Bastille）和兵工厂（the Arsenal）都不很远。

===Original===

Again she pondered. Where was she? Why was she being treated with a kindness and consideration altogether at variance with the tactics usually adopted by the enemies of the Scarlet Pimpernel? She was not in prison. She was not being starved, or threatened, or humiliated. The day wore on, and she was not confronted with one or other of those fiends who were so obviously using her as a decoy for her husband.

===Chinese===

她又思量起来。她这是在哪里？为什么人们待她这般和善体贴，与猩红石榴红（Scarlet Pimpernel）的仇敌们惯使的手段全然相悖？她不在狱中。她没有挨饿，没有受威吓，也没有受羞辱。白日缓缓捱过，那些分明拿她当她丈夫的诱饵的恶魔，却一个也没有露面。

===Original===

But though Marguerite Blakeney was not in prison, she was a prisoner. This she had ascertained five minutes after she was alone in the apartment. She could wander at will from room to room; but only in them, not out of them. The door of communication between the rooms was wide open; those that obviously gave on a landing outside were securely locked; and when a while ago the old woman had entered with the tray of food, Marguerite had caught sight of a group of men in the well-known tattered uniform of the National Guard, standing at attention in a wide, long antechamber.

===Chinese===

然而，玛格丽特·布莱克尼虽不在狱中，却是个囚徒。这一点，她独自待在这套公寓里五分钟之后，便弄确实了。她尽可以从一间屋子踱到另一间屋子；可也只许在这几间屋子里，不许出屋。屋子与屋子之间的门大敞着；那几扇显然通向外头楼梯平台的门，却锁得严严实实；方才老妇人端着食盘进来时，玛格丽特瞥见一伙男人，穿着那身尽人皆知的国民自卫军（National Guard）破烂制服，在一条又宽又长的前厅里肃然伫立。

===Original===

Yes; she was a prisoner! She could open the windows of her apartment and inhale the soft moist air which came across the wide tract of a barren land; but these windows were thirty feet above the ground, and there was no projection in the outside wall of the house anywhere near that would afford a foothold to anything human.

===Chinese===

不错；她是个囚徒！她可以推开公寓的窗子，吸一吸那掠过大片荒地的湿润柔和的空气；可这些窗子离地足有三十英尺，屋子外墙的近旁又没有任何一处凸出，足以容一个活人立足。

===Original===

Thus for twenty-four hours she was left to meditate, thrown upon her own resources, with no other company save that of her own thoughts, and they were anything but cheerful. The uncertainty of the situation soon began to prey upon her nerves. She had been calm in the morning; but as the day wore on the loneliness, the mystery, the silence, began to tell upon her courage. Soon she got to look upon the woman who waited on her as upon her jailer, and when she was alone she was forever straining her ears to hear what the men who were guarding her door might be saying among themselves.

The next night she hardly slept.

===Chinese===

就这样，整整二十四个钟头，她被撇下独自思量，全凭自己去打熬，作伴的唯有自己的心事，而那些心事，没有一桩是快活的。处境的没个定准，很快便开始啮噬她的神经。早晨她还镇静自若；可随着白日渐渐捱深，这份孤寂、这团疑云、这片沉寂，慢慢磨蚀起她的勇气来。不多时，她便把那伺候她的妇人看成了狱卒；独处的时候，她总竖起耳朵，想听清守在她门外的那伙人彼此在嘀咕些什么。

第二夜，她几乎不曾合眼。

### II / II

===Original===

Twenty-four hours later she had a visit from citizen Chauvelin.

===Chinese===

二十四个小时之后，公民肖夫兰（Chauvelin）来探望她了。

===Original===

She had been expecting that visit all along, or else a message from him. When he came she had need of all her pluck and all her determination, not to let him see the emotion which his presence caused her. Dread! Loathing! These were her predominant sensations. But dread above all; because he was dressed with scrupulous care and affected the manners and graces of a society which had long since cast him out. It was not the rough, out-at-elbows Terrorist who stood before her, the revolutionary demagogue who hits out right and left against a caste that has always spurned him and held itself aloof; it was the broken-down gentleman at war with fortune, who strives by his wits to be revenged against the buffetings of Fate and the arrogance which ostracised him as soon as he was down.

===Chinese===

这场来访，她自始便料到了，不然，也该有他捎来的口信。他来的时候，她得拿出全副的胆气、全副的决断，才不致让他看出，他的出现在她心里激起的是怎样的情绪。恐惧！憎恶！这是她心头压倒一切的两种感觉。而恐惧更在一切之上；因为他衣着考究得一丝不苟，偏又装出那一副早就把他扫地出门的上流社会的风度与雅态。站在她面前的，不是那个粗鲁的、肘弯磨得绽了线的恐怖党徒（Terrorist），不是那个向着一向践踏他、自命清高绝不理睬他的阶级左右开弓乱打的革命煽动家；而是一个与命运交战的落魄绅士，正凭着自己的心机，竭力要向命运的拳脚寻仇，向那份趁他一蹶不振便将他逐出门墙的傲慢寻仇。

===Original===

He began by asking solicitously after her well-being; hoped the journey had not overfatigued her; humbly begged her pardon for the discomfort which a higher power compelled him to put upon her. He talked platitudes in an even, unctuous voice until Marguerite, exasperated, and her nerves on edge, curtly bade him to come to the point.

===Chinese===

他开口先殷勤探问她的安康；盼愿这一路旅程不曾使她过分劳顿；又谦卑地乞她恕罪，说是有一种更高的权势，迫着他不得不让她受这些委屈。他用一种平稳油滑的腔调说着这些陈词滥调，直说得玛格丽特心头火起、神经绷紧，不客气地命他谈正题。

===Original===

“I have come to the point, dear lady,” he retorted suavely. “The point is that you should be comfortable and have no cause to complain whilst you are under this roof.”

“And how long am I to remain a prisoner under it?” she asked.

“Until Sir Percy has in his turn honoured this house with his presence,” he replied.

===Chinese===

“我说的就是正题，亲爱的夫人，”他圆滑地应道。“正题就是：您住在这座屋檐底下的日子里，应当舒舒服服，没有可抱怨的地方。”

“那我要在这屋檐底下做多久囚徒？”她问。

“直到珀西爵士（Sir Percy）也轮到赏光、屈尊光临这座宅子的时候，”他答道。

===Original===

To this she made no answer for a time, but sat quite still looking at him, as if detached and indifferent. He waited for her to speak, his pale eyes, slightly mocking, fixed upon her. Then she said simply:

“I understand.”

===Chinese===

对这话她一时没有作答，只端坐不动地望着他，仿佛置身事外、漠不关心。他等着她开口，一双浅色的眼睛微微含着讥诮，牢牢盯在她身上。随后她简简单单地说：

“我明白了。”

===Original===

“I was quite sure you would, dear lady,” he rejoined blandly. “You see, the phase of heroics is past. I will confess to you that it proved of no avail when measured against the lofty coolness of that peerless exquisite. So we over here have shed our ardour like a mantle. We, too, now are quite calm, quite unperturbed, quite content to wait. The beautiful Lady Blakeney is a guest under this roof. Well, sooner or later that most gallant of husbands will desire to approach his lady. Sooner or later he will learn that she is no longer in England. Then he will set his incomparable wits to work to find out where she is. Again, I may say that sooner or later, perhaps, even aided by us, he will know that she is here. Then he will come. Am I not right?”

===Chinese===

“我原本就笃定您会明白的，亲爱的夫人，”他淡然自若地应道。“您瞧，逞英雄的阶段已经过去了。我向您坦白承认：在那位举世无双的花花公子那份高不可攀的冷静面前，它被证明全然无用武之地。所以我们这边的人，已经把那股热劲像卸斗篷一样卸了下来。我们如今，也安安静静，也无忧无扰，也安于等待。美丽的布莱克尼夫人（Lady Blakeney）是这座屋檐下的贵客。好啦，那位最豪侠不过的丈夫，早晚会想来到他夫人跟前。早晚会得知她已不在英格兰（England）。到那时，他便会开动他那无与伦比的头脑，去查明她在何处。我还可以说，早晚——或许还得靠我们从旁相助——他会知道她就在这里。到那时他就会来。我说得不对吗？”

===Original===

Of course he was right. Sooner or later Percy would learn where she was; and then he would come. He would come to her, despite every trap set for his undoing, despite every net laid to catch him, despite danger of death that waited for him if he came.

===Chinese===

他当然说得不错。迟早珀西会打听到她在哪里；到那时，他就会来。他会到她这里来——不顾为毁他而设下的每一道陷阱，不顾为擒他而布下的每一张罗网，不顾他一来便候在那里的死亡之险。

===Original===

Chauvelin said little more. In truth, the era of heroics was at an end. At an end those ominous “either﻿—ors” that he was wont to mete out with a voice quavering with rage and lust of revenge. Now there was no alternative, no deep-laid plot save one: to wait for the Scarlet Pimpernel until he came.

===Chinese===

肖夫兰没有再多说什么。说实在的，逞英雄的时代已然告终。他惯于用盛怒与复仇之欲激得发颤的嗓音发落出来的那些凶险的“要么……否则……”，也一并告终了。如今再无别的抉择，再无别的深谋密计，只剩一桩：守候猩红石榴红，直到他来。

===Original===

In the meanwhile she, Marguerite, must remain helpless and a prisoner; she must eat and drink and sleep. She, the decoy!﻿—who would never know when the crushing blow would fall that would mean a hundred deaths to her if it involved that of the husband whom she worshipped.

===Chinese===

与此同时，她，玛格丽特，只能束手无策地做着囚徒；只能吃饭、喝水、睡觉。她，这诱饵！——她永远不会知道，那灭顶的一击会在几时落下；那一击若连带着要了她所崇拜的丈夫的性命，于她便如同死上一百回。

===Original===

After a while, Chauvelin went away. In fact, she never knew actually when he did go. A while ago he had sat there on that upright chair, quiet, well groomed, suave of speech and bland of manner.

“Then he will come,” he had said quite urbanely. “Am I not right?”

===Chinese===

过了一阵，肖夫兰走了。其实，她压根不曾察觉，他究竟是什么时候走的。方才他还坐在那把直背椅上，安静，齐整，言语温润，态度和婉。

“到那时他就会来，”他当时极温文地如是说。“我说得不对吗？”

===Original===

When Marguerite closed her eyes she could still see him, his mocking gaze fixed upon her, his thin, white hands folded complacently before him. And presently, as the day wore on and the shades of evening blurred one object in the room after another, the straight-backed chair, still left in its place, assumed a fantastic human shape﻿—the shape of a meagre figure with narrow shoulders and thin, carefully be-stockinged legs. And all the faint noises around her﻿—the occasional creaking of the furniture, the movements of the men outside her door, the soughing of the evening breeze in the foliage of the elm trees﻿—all were merged into a thin, bland human voice, that went on repeating in a kind of thin, dreary monotone:

===Chinese===

玛格丽特一阖上眼，便还能看见他：那讥诮的目光牢牢盯住她，那双瘦削的白手，安然交叠在身前。渐渐地，白日捱尽，暮色把屋里一件件东西依次模糊了去，那把依然留在原处的直背椅，竟幻出了一个奇诡的人形——一个瘦削的身影，窄窄的肩，细细的、仔细套着长袜的两条腿。而她四周一切细碎的声响——家具偶尔的吱呀，门外那伙人的走动，晚风拂过榆树叶丛的沙沙——全都汇成了一个细弱而和缓的人声，以一种细弱、凄沉的单调，翻来覆去地说着：

===Original===

“Then he will come. Am I not right?”

===Chinese===

“到那时他就会来。我说得不对吗？”

## XXIII. Mice and Men / XXIII. 鼠与人

### I / I

===Original===

It was on her return from England that Theresia Cabarrus took to consulting the old witch in the Rue de la Planchette, driven thereto by ambition, and also no doubt by remorse. There was nothing of the hardened criminal about the fair Spaniard; she was just a spoilt woman who had been mocked and thwarted, and desired to be revenged. The Scarlet Pimpernel had appeared before her as one utterly impervious to her charms, and, egged on by Chauvelin, who used her for his own ends, she entered into a callous conspiracy, the aim of which was the destruction of that gang of English spies who were the enemies of France, and the first stage of which was the heartless abduction of Lady Blakeney and her incarceration as a decoy for the ultimate capture of her own husband.

A cruel, abominable act! Theresia, who had plunged headlong into this shameful crime, would a few days later have given much to undo the harm she had wrought. But she had yet to learn that, once used as a tool by the Committee of Public Safety and by Chauvelin, its most unscrupulous agent, no man or woman could hope to become free again until the work demanded had been accomplished to the end. There was no freedom from that taskmaster save in death; and Theresia’s fit of compunction did not carry her to the lengths of self-sacrifice. Marguerite Blakeney was her prisoner, the decoy which would bring the English milor inevitably to the spot where his wife was incarcerated; and Theresia, who had helped to bring this state of things about, did her best to smother remorse, and having done Chauvelin’s dirty work for him she set to to see what personal advantage she could derive from it.

===Chinese===

特蕾莎·卡巴吕斯（Theresia Cabarrus）正是从英格兰（England）回来之后，才开始时常去请教普朗谢特街（Rue de la Planchette）上那个老女巫的——驱使她这样做的，是野心，无疑还有悔恨。这位美丽的西班牙女子身上，没有半分积恶惯犯的狠毒心肠；她不过是个给惯坏了的女人，受过嘲弄、遭过挫败，一心只想着报复。猩红石榴红（Scarlet Pimpernel）出现在她面前时，对她的妩媚全然无动于衷；于是，在肖夫兰（Chauvelin）——那个拿她当工具、图一己之私的人——的怂恿之下，她参与了一桩冷酷无情的阴谋。这阴谋的目的，是消灭那帮与法兰西（France）为敌的英国间谍；而它的第一步，便是狠心劫走布莱克尼夫人（Lady Blakeney），把她囚禁起来充作诱饵，好最终擒获她的丈夫。

残酷而可憎的行径！特蕾莎当初一头扎进了这桩无耻的罪行，几天之后，便甘愿付出高昂的代价，来消弭自己闯下的祸害。可她还不曾明白：一旦被公共安全委员会（Committee of Public Safety）、被它最肆无忌惮的走狗肖夫兰当了工具使，无论男女，不把索要的差事办到底，就休想重获自由之身。要摆脱那位监工，除死之外别无他途；而特蕾莎那一阵良心的刺痛，还不曾把她推到自我牺牲的地步。玛格丽特·布莱克尼（Marguerite Blakeney）是她的囚徒，是那枚诱饵，注定要把英国爵爷不由分说地引到他妻子被囚的地方来；特蕾莎既是帮着把这个局面做成了的人，便只得竭力把悔恨捂死在心底；替肖夫兰干完了这桩肮脏的勾当，她又着手盘算起来，看能从中给自己捞到什么好处。

===Original===

Firstly, the satisfaction of her petty revenge: the Scarlet Pimpernel caught in a trap, would surely regret his interference in Theresia’s love affairs. Theresia cared less than nothing about Bertrand Moncrif, and would have been quite grateful to the English milor for having spirited that embarrassing lover of hers away but for that letter which had wounded the beautiful Spaniard’s vanity to the quick, and still rankled sufficiently to ease her conscience on the score of her subsequent actions. That the letter was a bogus one, concocted and written by Chauvelin himself in order to spur her on to a mean revenge, Theresia did not know.

But far stronger than thoughts of revenge where Theresia’s schemes for her own future. She had begun to dream of Robespierre’s gratitude, of her triumph over all those who had striven for over two years to bring that gang of English spies to book. She saw her name writ largely on the roll of fame; she even saw in her mind, the tyrant himself as her willing slave﻿ ﻿… and something more than that.

===Chinese===

头一样，是那点小小报复的快意：猩红石榴红一旦落进圈套，必定会懊悔当初插手了特蕾莎的情事。至于贝特朗·蒙克里夫（Bertrand Moncrif），特蕾莎非但毫不放在心上，简直视同无物；那位英国爵爷替她把这个惹尴尬的情人神不知鬼不觉地带走，她本来倒要大大领情——若不是那封信。正是那封信，把这位美丽的西班牙女子的虚荣伤到了根子上，至今犹自隐隐作痛；而这痛，恰恰够让她给自己随后的种种行径，在良心上开脱个干净。至于那封信原是伪作，是肖夫兰为了驱策她去行一场卑劣的报复、亲自编造、亲笔写下的，特蕾莎却一概不知。

可是，比利欲复仇的念头强烈得多的，是特蕾莎对自身前程的种种盘算。她已经开始梦想罗伯斯庇尔（Robespierre）的感念，梦想她压倒众人的凯旋——那些人两年来千方百计，就想把那帮英国间谍捉拿归案。她看见自己的名字赫然题上声誉的名册；她甚至在心目中看见，那暴君本人甘愿做她俯首帖耳的奴隶……乃至比这更甚的图景。

===Original===

For her tool Bertrand she had no further use. By way of a reward for the abominable abduction of Lady Blakeney, he had been allowed to follow the woman he worshipped like a lackey attached to her train. Dejected, already spurned, he returned to Paris with her, here to resume the life of humiliation and of despised ardour which had broken his spirit and warped his nature, before his gallant rescuer had snatched him out of the toils of the beautiful Spaniard.

Within an hour of setting his foot on French soil, Bertrand had realised that he had been nothing in Theresia’s sight but a lump of malleable wax, which she had moulded to her own design and now threw aside as cumbersome and useless. He had realised that her ambition soared far above linking her fate to an obscure and penniless lover, when the coming man of the hour﻿—citizen Tallien﻿—was already at her feet.

===Chinese===

至于她的工具贝特朗，她已再派不上什么用场了。作为对他劫持布莱克尼夫人那桩可憎勾当的犒赏，他被准许跟在他所崇拜的女人身后，像个编进她随行队列的跟班听差。他意气消沉，早已受了冷落，随她一同回到了巴黎（Paris），在这里重新过起那种屈辱的、一腔热忱尽遭人贱视的生活——早在那位豪侠的救命恩人把他从这美丽的西班牙女子的罗网里劫救出去之前，正是这样的生活摧折过他的精神、扭曲过他的天性。

踏上法兰西国土还不到一个钟头，贝特朗便已明白：他在特蕾莎眼里，从来不过是一团可捏可塑的软蜡，她照着自己的图样把他捏成了形，如今又嫌它累赘无用，随手丢在了一旁。他也明白了：眼下的当红人物——塔利安公民（Tallien）——已经拜倒在她的脚下，她的野心早已翱翔到高天之上，哪里还肯把命运拴在一个无名无钱的情人身上。

### II / II

===Original===

Thus Theresia had attained one of her great desires: the Scarlet Pimpernel was as good as captured, and when he finally succumbed he could not fail to know whence came the blow that struck him.

With regard to her future, matters were more doubtful. She had not yet subjugated Robespierre sufficiently to cause him to give up his more humble love and to lay down his power and popularity at her feet; whilst the man who had offered her his hand and name﻿—citizen Tallien﻿—was forever putting a check upon her ambition and his own advancement by his pusillanimity and lack of enterprise.

Whilst she was aching to push him into decisive action, into seizing the supreme power before Robespierre and his friends had irrevocably established theirs, Tallien was for temporising, fear that in trying to snatch a dictatorship he and his beloved with him would lose their heads.

===Chinese===

就这样，特蕾莎得偿了她的一大心愿：猩红石榴红已形同落网；等到他终于败下阵来，那致命的一击来自何方，他不可能不知道。

至于她的前程，事情就更难逆料了。她还没能把罗伯斯庇尔彻底降服，降服到叫他舍弃那段较为卑微的情爱、把他的权势与名望都放到她脚下的地步；而那个已把他的手与姓氏一并献给她的男人——塔利安公民——却又总在以他的怯懦和不思进取，拖住她的野心、也拖住他自己的前程。

她焦灼地巴望推他一把，促他当机立断，趁罗伯斯庇尔及其同党还未把权柄无可挽回地攥牢，抢先夺取最高权力；塔利安却一味主张拖延观望，生怕冒险去抢独裁之位，到头来连他自己带他心爱的人都要赔上脑袋。

===Original===

“While Robespierre lives,” Theresia would argue passionately, “no man’s head is safe. Every rival, sooner or later, becomes a victim. St. Just and Couthon aim at a dictatorship for him. Sooner or later they will succeed; then death to every man who has ever dared oppose them.”

“Therefore ’tis wiser not to oppose,” the prudent Tallien would retort. “The time will come﻿—”

“Never!” she riposted hotly. “While you plot, and argue and ponder, Robespierre acts or signs your death-warrant.”

===Chinese===

“只要罗伯斯庇尔还活着，”特蕾莎总要激烈地争辩，“谁的脑袋都不保险。每一个政敌，迟早都要沦为牺牲品。圣茹斯特（St. Just）和库东（Couthon）正图谋替他谋一顶独裁的冠冕。他们迟早要成功的；到那时，凡是斗胆反对过他们的人，一律都得处死。”

“所以说，不去反对才是上策，”谨慎的塔利安总要这样回敬。“时机总会来的——”

“永远也等不来！”她火辣辣地反唇相讥。“你们还在那儿密谋啊、争辩啊、掂量啊，罗伯斯庇尔早已动了手，或是签下了你的死刑状。”

===Original===

“Robespierre is the idol of the people; he sways the Convention with a word. His eloquence would drag an army of enemies to the guillotine!”

“Robespierre!” Theresia retorted with sublime contempt. “Ah, when you have said that, you think you have said everything! France, humanity, the people, sovereign power!﻿—all that, you assert, is embodied in that one man. But, my friend, listen to me!” she went on earnestly. “Listen, when I assert that Robespierre is only a name, a fetish, a manikin set up on a pedestal! By whom? By you, and the Convention; by the Clubs and the Committees. And the pedestal is composed of that elusive entity which you call the people and which will disintegrate from beneath his feet as soon as the people have realised that those feet are less than clay. One touch of a firm finger against that manikin, I tell you, and he will fall as dust before you; and you can rise upon that same elusive pedestal﻿—popularity, to the heights which he hath so easily attained.”

===Chinese===

“罗伯斯庇尔是万民崇拜的偶像；他一句话便能左右国民公会（the Convention）。他的雄辩能把整军的仇敌都拖上断头台！”
